# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Commands

```bash
# Install all workspace dependencies (run once after clone)
pnpm install

# Dev servers (hot-reload Rust + frontend)
pnpm dev:win          # Windows
pnpm dev:macos        # macOS
pnpm dev:linux        # Linux (Ubuntu/Debian)

# Production builds
pnpm build:win        # → target/release/bundle/msi/*.msi + target/release/inspector-rust.exe
pnpm build:macos      # → target/release/bundle/dmg/*.dmg
pnpm build:linux      # → .deb + AppImage (AppImage may fail locally; use build:linux:deb)
pnpm build:linux:deb  # → .deb only (recommended on Ubuntu)
```

**`scripts/install-macos.sh` self-cleans `target/` (v0.84.17)** so the Cargo build
dir can't balloon (it had grown to ~32 GB). After a successful install it: deletes
`target/debug` (dev-only artifacts), keeps only the newest `.dmg`, and — because
Cargo never GCs the rlibs of *old* dependency versions in `release/deps` — runs a
one-off `cargo clean` if `target/` is past a size cap (`IR_TARGET_CAP_GB`, default
12 GB; `0` disables). The release build cache is otherwise preserved so normal
rebuilds stay fast/incremental.

```bash
### Hidden game easter eggs (search bar)

Exact match in the popup search field (case-insensitive, no autocomplete):

| Trigger | Game |
|---------|------|
| `getshaky` | Pong (`PongGame.tsx`, `lib/pong.ts`) |
| `rockthebox` | Snake — walls kill (`SnakeGame.tsx`, `lib/snake.ts`) |
| `rockthabox` | Snake — wrap-around edges |
| `spacer` | Space Invaders (`SpaceInvadersGame.tsx`, `lib/space-invaders.ts`) |
| `learningtofly` | Flappy Bird (`FlappyGame.tsx`, `lib/flappy.ts`) |

# Tests
pnpm test                                     # frontend vitest (all, single run) — also fires the posttest badge updater
cargo test --workspace                        # all Rust unit tests
pnpm --filter inspector-rust-frontend test:watch                 # frontend vitest watch mode
cargo test -p inspector-rust-core --lib <module>                 # one Rust module (e.g. `snitch`, `hue`)
pnpm --filter inspector-rust-frontend exec vitest run src/lib/<f>.test.ts   # one frontend file
IR_SKIP_BADGES=1 pnpm test                    # skip the posttest badge recompute (fast local loop)

# Coverage (tooling: cargo-llvm-cov + @vitest/coverage-v8, both installed)
cargo llvm-cov --lib -p inspector-rust-core --summary-only        # Rust per-file coverage
cd core/frontend && npx vitest run --coverage --coverage.provider=v8 \
  --coverage.reporter=text --coverage.include='src/lib/**'        # frontend pure-logic coverage

# README badges (LOC + test counts) — recomputed from the real sources/runners,
# idempotent, aborts if a suite is red. Runs automatically as the `posttest` hook.
pnpm update-badges    # or: node scripts/update-badges.mjs

# Static analysis (clippy + tsc + eslint in one shot)
pnpm check            # or: bash scripts/check.sh

# Individual checks
pnpm typecheck        # tsc --noEmit
pnpm lint             # eslint src
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
```

Running Rust tests locally on Linux requires system libs:
```bash
sudo apt-get install -y libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libappindicator3-dev librsvg2-dev patchelf libxdo-dev libxcb-shape0-dev libxcb-xfixes0-dev
```

## Documentation contract — keep every surface in sync

**Whenever you add or change a feature, update ALL of the surfaces below in the same commit.** This is the single authoritative checklist (the per-command rules under *Adding a new search-bar command* are the command-specific slice of it). The recurring failure mode is a feature that ships in one surface and silently rots in another (e.g. `equalizer` was live but missing from the in-app Features tab for months) — these surfaces + their gates exist to make that impossible.

The surfaces, and what each is for:

| Surface | File(s) | Covers | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`features.txt`** | repo root | EVERY feature — one line, always current (commands, hotkeys, gestures, panels, cleaner categories, settings, animations…) | none (discipline) — but it's the human catalogue; never skip it |
| **CommandDoc registry** | `core/frontend/src/lib/commandDocs.ts` | every `COMMANDS` search-bar command → drives inline `?` help + README matrix + Features tab | `commandDocs.test.ts` (doc exists, ≥3 examples, ≥1 tip) |
| **Features-tab extras** | `core/frontend/src/lib/feature-extras.ts` | the hand-maintained Features-tab rows: non-command features, **hidden triggers** (games / `bpm` / `equalizer` / `2fa`), in-popup & preview actions | `feature-extras.test.ts` (every `HIDDEN_TRIGGERS` keyword has a row) |
| **README matrix** | `README.md` / `README.de.md` (`<!-- COMMANDS:START/END -->`) | the command table, both languages — **generated, never hand-edited** | `node scripts/gen-docs.mjs --check` in `scripts/check.sh` |
| **README prose + badges** | `README.md` / `README.de.md` | headline pitch + LOC/test badges | `scripts/update-badges.mjs` (posttest hook) |
| **CLAUDE.md** | this file | architecture + per-module design notes + gotchas | none (discipline) |
| **CHANGELOG.md** | repo root | user-facing Added/Changed/Fixed per version | none (discipline) |
| **`docs/*.md`** | `docs/` | deep reference for substantial features (link from CLAUDE.md + README) | `gen-docs --check` verifies every `see_also` file exists |

**Per change type — the minimum surfaces to touch:**

- **New search-bar command** → the 5-step checklist under *Adding a new search-bar command* (Catalogue + Dispatch + priority/accent + **CommandDoc** + gen-docs + `features.txt`). The CommandDoc auto-populates the README matrix + Features tab + `?` help.
- **New hidden trigger** (a game, a visualizer like `bpm`/`equalizer`, `2fa`) → add it to `feature-extras.ts` (`HIDDEN_TRIGGERS` + the matching row list) **and** `features.txt`, **and** a CLAUDE.md design note. `feature-extras.test.ts` fails by name if the trigger has no row.
- **New in-popup / preview action, gesture, hotkey, panel, setting** → `feature-extras.ts` (in-popup actions) or the FeaturesPanel hotkeys section as fits, **plus** `features.txt`, CLAUDE.md, CHANGELOG.
- **Behaviour change to an existing feature** → refresh its CommandDoc / `feature-extras.ts` note, its CLAUDE.md section, `features.txt` line, and add a CHANGELOG entry (e.g. the v0.96.0 shazam background/Spotify change updated the shazam CommandDoc + CLAUDE.md + `features.txt`).

**Verify before committing:** `bash scripts/check.sh` (clippy + tsc + eslint + gen-docs drift) and `pnpm test` (which runs the doc gates above + recomputes badges). If a doc gate is red, a surface drifted — fix the doc, not the test.

## Architecture

### Workspace layout

```
core/rust-lib/   — inspector-rust-core rlib: ALL business logic (DB, clipboard, hotkey, paste, snippets, notes, settings, backup, expander)
core/frontend/   — React 19 + TS + Tailwind v4 + Vite 7 (shared by all platforms)
win/src-tauri/   — Windows bundle shell: 2-line main.rs + Tauri config + capabilities
macos/src-tauri/ — macOS bundle shell: 2-line main.rs + Tauri config + capabilities
linux/src-tauri/ — Linux bundle shell: 2-line main.rs + Tauri config + capabilities (Ubuntu/Debian)
```

All three platform shells contain only `inspector_rust_core::run(tauri::generate_context!())`. All logic is in `core/rust-lib`. The Tauri CLI is invoked per platform via `pnpm --filter inspector-rust-{win,macos,linux} tauri {dev,build}`.

### Adding a new IPC command (end-to-end)

1. Implement logic in the relevant `core/rust-lib/src/*.rs` module.
2. Add a `#[tauri::command]` wrapper in `core/rust-lib/src/commands.rs`. **A sync command runs ON the main thread** — any command that can block (network, subprocess spawn, sleeps, heavy crypto/IO) MUST be `pub async fn` + `tauri::async_runtime::spawn_blocking` (clone the `DbHandle` Arc into the closure; async commands with borrowed `State` must return `Result`). The v0.105.0 audit converted ~10 violators (`hue_*`, `weather_fetch`, `token_usage_fetch`, `get_system_stats`, the backup family, `finder_open_terminal`) that froze the whole UI — and, via the auto_expand CGEventTap on the main run loop, stalled system-wide keystroke delivery.
3. Register it in the `invoke_handler![]` macro in `core/rust-lib/src/lib.rs`.
4. Add a typed `invoke("command_name", { ...args })` wrapper in `core/frontend/src/lib/ipc.ts`.
5. **Unit-test the logic from step 1** (the module's `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`), not the IPC glue — see *Testing & coverage*. If the frontend wrapper transforms the response, cover that in `ipc.test.ts`; a plain pass-through wrapper needs only the existing command-name/arg-shape guard.

### Testing & coverage

**The house style: extract the pure logic, test *that* — never the OS/FFI shell.** Every module keeps its deterministic core (parsers, math, state machines, arg-builders, formatters) as free functions and unit-tests them exhaustively; the impure edge (CoreAudio/Vision/CGEvent FFI, Tauri window builds, `ffmpeg`/`yt-dlp`/`osascript` spawns, Web Audio, the WS bridge) is left untested because it needs a live machine and would be non-deterministic. This is why headline coverage looks low (~59 % Rust line, ~20 % frontend statements — 2026-08-05, at **2967 tests = 1192 Rust + 1775 frontend**, ~98k LOC) while the code that *can* carry a test is well-covered: **frontend `src/lib` ≈ 84 % stmt / 95 % branch**, and the pure Rust cores that live in `*/mod.rs` alongside 0 %-covered `*/macos.rs` FFI (e.g. `window_snap/mod.rs` 93 %, `boom/mod.rs` 93 %, `gestures/mod.rs` 77 %). Judge a change by the coverage of the module's *pure* surface, not the workspace average.

- **Where tests live.** Rust: a file-final `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { use super::*; … }` per module (the badge LOC counter treats that marker as the end of source, so keep test modules at the bottom). Frontend: a sibling `src/**/<name>.test.ts(x)` using Vitest (`describe`/`it`/`expect`), happy-dom environment. **Pure data catalogues carry their own structural invariant tests** — `faker/registry.rs` (the `Args` parser + globally-unique lowercase names/aliases) and `sec/registry.rs` (cross-tool name/alias uniqueness, every preset starts with a real binary token, `writes-data → sharp`, honest tag vocabulary) both have bottom test modules on top of the behavioural tests in their `mod.rs`; a new generator/tool that breaks an invariant fails a named test rather than shipping. `iris` is the same idea applied to a *signal* path: `mic_capture::chunk_rms` is pinned against known waveforms (silence, square, sine ≈ 1/√2, a lone spike in 1024 samples), `iris.rs` covers the persistence contract against an in-memory DB (unset / blank / garbage / hand-edited-out-of-band all fall back or clamp rather than arming at a nonsense level) plus a simulated 25 Hz frame feed asserting a transient lights **exactly once**, and both sides assert the *same* four constants (`30 / 100 / 55 / offset 90`) so the TS mirror in `lib/iris.ts` cannot drift from the Rust source of truth.
- **Test behaviour, not implementation.** No getter tests, no snapshot-as-assertion, no pure mock-verification (the one exception is `lib/ipc.test.ts`, a deliberate 29-test *contract* guard pinning each `invoke` command-name + arg shape — kept, never expanded). Cover edge cases explicitly: empty/null, boundaries, Unicode/Umlaute, and error/fallback paths.
- **Determinism / offline.** Mock external deps. DB: `rusqlite::Connection::open_in_memory()` (Rust) — no temp files. Time: Rust takes explicit timestamps (`generate_at(secret, t)`); frontend uses `vi.useFakeTimers()` + `vi.setSystemTime()`. Randomness: `vi.spyOn(Math, "random")` / vary by index. DOM-absent branches: `vi.stubGlobal("DOMParser", undefined)`.
- **Encryption-at-rest invariant (`crypto.rs`).** The global `CIPHER` is **never** initialised in the test process — so `crypto::encrypt`/`decrypt` are plaintext pass-throughs and every DB/snippet/notes/TOTP test reads back what it wrote. **Do not call `crypto::init` (or `CIPHER.set`) from a test**: it would encrypt across the whole shared-process suite and break every plaintext-readback assertion. Test the cipher directly via `Cipher::new(&key)` (bypasses the global), as the existing `crypto` tests do.
- **A new test that fails = a possible bug.** Do NOT relax the assertion to the observed behaviour. Investigate; if it's a real defect, fix the code (or, if it's inherent, assert only the guaranteed invariant and flag it). This is how the v0.84.261 issuer-only-TOTP-export drift was caught + fixed.
- **Coverage tooling.** `cargo llvm-cov` (needs `llvm-tools-preview`) for Rust, `@vitest/coverage-v8` for the frontend — commands in the *Commands* block above. Both are installed as dev tooling; neither runs in CI by default.
- **Keep the badges honest.** After adding/removing tests, the counts auto-update via the `posttest` hook (`scripts/update-badges.mjs`) — see *README badges* below. If you commit test changes, the LOC + test-count badges should move with them.

### README badges — auto-computed (`scripts/update-badges.mjs`, v0.84.260)

The headline **lines-of-code** + **unit-test-count** badges in `README.md` / `README.de.md` are recomputed from the real sources by a Node-ESM script — never hand-edited. LOC counts all written code INCLUDING tests (policy change v0.126.1 — tests are maintained code; the old source-only rule understated the repo by ~40k lines): all workspace Rust incl. the file-final `#[cfg(test)]` modules + all frontend `src/**/*.ts(x)` incl. `*.test.ts(x)`, minus only the generated `openers-data.ts`; `node_modules`/`target`/`dist` are never scanned. (Test modules stay file-final by repo convention regardless.) Test counts come from the **actual runner output** — the summed `test result: ok. N passed` of `cargo test --workspace` + the `Tests N passed` of a direct `vitest run` — not by grepping `it(`/`#[test]` (which would miscount skips/todos). It **aborts if either suite is red** (a badge must never advertise a passing suite that isn't) and is **idempotent** (re-running is a no-op). Wired as both `pnpm update-badges` and the `posttest` hook; the runners are invoked **directly** (`cargo test`, `vitest run`), never via `npm test`, so the hook can't recurse. **Runner output is ANSI-stripped before parsing** (v0.84.262) — vitest colourises even into a pipe, and the escape codes land *between* the words the summary regex matches (`Tests \e[1m\e[32m907 passed`), which silently broke the parse ("Could not parse vitest output — badges NOT updated") and left the badges stale. `IR_SKIP_BADGES=1` turns the hook into a fast no-op for a tight local `pnpm test` loop.

### Adding a new search-bar (custom) command — REQUIRED checklist

Custom commands must **always** outrank clipboard history (spliced above fuzzy clips) **and** render with the red (`rose`) accent, **and** be discoverable in the docs + the app's Features tab. When adding one, do **all** of:

1. **Catalogue** — add the `CommandKind` + a `CommandSpec` entry to `core/frontend/src/lib/commands.ts` (keyword, syntax, description, `requiresArg`, optional `platform` gating). Update the `COMMANDS.length` assertion in `commands.test.ts`.
2. **Dispatch** — handle the kind in `App.tsx`'s `dispatchCommand` (run it / enter its inline mode).
3. **Priority + red accent** — the command surfaces as a `command` `ListEntry` via the `commandEntry` `switch` in `App.tsx`. Add a tailored `case` for a nice label/hint; if you forget, the `default` arm now builds a **generic runnable row** anyway (v0.84.63) so the command still outranks clips and is red (`isCustomCommand` keys off `entry.kind === "command"` in `HistoryItem.tsx`). Only kinds with their **own** dedicated `ListEntry` (`pwgen`, `bruno`) `return null` there; whole-list takeovers (`kill`, `meme`) are handled before the switch. (This invariant exists because forgetting it caused the v0.84.59 `stats`/`trim` bug: no row → a clip won Enter.)
4. **Inline help doc (REQUIRED)** — add a `CommandDoc` to `core/frontend/src/lib/commandDocs.ts` (synopsis, description, args/flags, **≥3 examples**, **≥1 tip or caveat**, related, version, tagline + `tagline_de`; group hidden aliases under the primary's `aliases`). This is not optional: `commandDocs.test.ts` fails by name if a non-hidden command has no doc / too few examples. **This single registry drives three surfaces automatically** — the inline `?` help (`CommandHelp.tsx`), the **Features tab** (`FeaturesPanel.tsx` maps `COMMAND_DOCS`, so you do *not* hand-add a command row there anymore), and the README command matrix. See [inline-help.md](./docs/inline-help.md).
5. **Regenerate + docs** — run `node scripts/gen-docs.mjs` to refresh the README command matrix (both languages, between the `<!-- COMMANDS:START/END -->` markers — **never hand-edit that block**), update the *Power commands* bullet if the pitch changes, **and add a one-line entry to `features.txt`**. `bash scripts/check.sh` runs `gen-docs --check` and fails CI on drift.

Steps 4 + 5 are not optional: **every new custom command must have a `CommandDoc` (which auto-populates the Features tab + README matrix + `?` help) and a `features.txt` line.**

**`features.txt` (repo root) is the always-current one-line feature catalogue** — this applies to EVERY new feature, not just search-bar commands (panels, hotkeys, gestures, cleaner categories, settings, …): whenever functionality is added, extend `features.txt` in the same commit.

### Inline command help (`?`) — the CommandDoc registry (v0.84.273)

`core/frontend/src/lib/commandDocs.ts` (`COMMAND_DOCS: CommandDoc[]`) is the **single source of truth** for every command's documentation, and the *only* place it's authored. Appending `?` to a command in the search bar renders its full help in the preview column; `?` alone renders a categorised index. Full reference: [docs/inline-help.md](./docs/inline-help.md).

- **Trigger parsing** — `lib/commandHelp.ts::parseHelpQuery` (pure, unit-tested) turns a trailing `?` into a `HelpTarget` (`index` (+ `filter`) | a resolved `CommandDoc`), or `null` when the `?` is **literal**. **Browsable index (v0.87.2):** `?` fills the LEFT list with `help`-`ListEntry` rows (every doc, **sorted alphabetically by command name**; `? <text>` full-text-filtered via the pure `searchDocs` — keyword/alias fuzzy > tagline > description/tips substring, alphabetical tie-break), ↑/↓ browses and the preview renders the SELECTED row's doc live (`lookupDoc` in the render); Enter puts the command into the search bar (+ trailing space when its syntax takes an argument). Examples in the doc view are clickable (fill the search bar); an `← Index`-chip navigates back; a no-match filter falls back to the grouped `IndexView` with a notice. It fires ONLY for a single command-shaped token (`[a-z0-9]+`) + at most one space + a lone trailing `?`, so `?` inside a template (`faker tpl "warum? {name}"`), glob (`a?b`), URL (`…?id=1`) or after an argument (`bruno hallo?`) never triggers. **Leading `?<command>` (no space, v0.98.2):** a `?` immediately followed by a command token — `?snitch`, `?weather`, `?cal` — resolves straight to that command's doc (exact/alias/prefix), because a LEADING `?` is unambiguously a help request (no history search or command starts with `?`; every literal-`?` collision has the `?` LATER in the string). An unmatched leading term (`?zzzzz`) falls back to the filtered index; `? <term>` WITH a space stays the full-text index search. `App.tsx` derives `helpTarget = parseHelpQuery(query)` and renders `<CommandHelp>` as the **highest-precedence** preview branch (above every inline panel); the index case blanks the left list.
- **One registry, three surfaces (can't drift):** (1) inline `?` help (`components/CommandHelp.tsx`); (2) the **README command matrix** in both languages — generated by `scripts/gen-docs.mjs` between `<!-- COMMANDS:START/END -->` markers (English `tagline`, German `tagline_de ?? tagline`), **never hand-edited**; (3) the **Features tab** (`FeaturesPanel.tsx` maps `COMMAND_DOCS` at runtime).
- **Gates:** `commandDocs.test.ts` (every non-hidden `COMMANDS` keyword has a doc; ≥3 examples; ≥1 tip/caveat; documented args/flags; real related links; no orphan/duplicate keywords), `commandHelp.test.ts` (trigger grammar + every literal-`?` collision), and `node scripts/gen-docs.mjs --check` in `scripts/check.sh` (README matrix in sync + every `see_also` file exists). The generator transpiles the zero-import registry with the bundled esbuild (no new dep). Hidden triggers (games, `2fa`/`otp`, `bpm`, `equalizer`) aren't in `COMMANDS`, so they have no CommandDoc / README-matrix / `?`-help entry — instead they're documented in the **Features tab** via `lib/feature-extras.ts` (`HIDDEN_TRIGGER_FEATURES`/`HIDDEN_GAMES`), gated by `feature-extras.test.ts` (every `HIDDEN_TRIGGERS` keyword must have a row). `opener` is the one deliberate exception — an undocumented easter egg.

### Database — five tables in one SQLite file

`DbHandle = Arc<Mutex<Connection>>` (rusqlite + parking_lot). Managed as Tauri state. File location:
- Windows: `%APPDATA%\InspectorRust\history.db`
- macOS: `~/Library/Application Support/InspectorRust/history.db`
- Linux: `~/.local/share/InspectorRust/history.db`

| Table | Purpose | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `entries` | Clipboard history | SHA-256 deduped; capped via `prune_locked`; sorted by `pinned DESC, last_used_at DESC`. **Configurable cap (v0.98.0)**: the prune keeps `effective_max_entries(conn)` rows — the `history.max_entries` setting (read from the SAME connection while pruning) clamped to `[MIN_HISTORY_ENTRIES=50, MAX_HISTORY_ENTRIES=100000]`, defaulting to `MAX_ENTRIES = 1000` when unset/blank/invalid (safe if the `settings` table is absent, e.g. a bare in-memory test DB). `db::max_entries` / `db::set_max_entries` (clamps, persists, prunes immediately) + IPC `get_history_max` (→ `HistoryLimit { max, min, ceiling }`) / `set_history_max` (emits `clipboard-changed`); Settings → **Clipboard history** (`HistoryLimitSection`, deep-link id `clipboard-history`). **Pinned clips (v0.76.0)**: a `pinned` column (lazy `ALTER TABLE` migration) exempts a row from the prune (`WHERE pinned = 0`) and floats it to the top. `db::set_pinned` + IPC `set_clip_pinned`; the pin ★ toggle lives on each `HistoryItem` row. **Pinned-only view (v0.94.0)**: a pin toggle in the `HistoryList` toolbar flips `App.tsx`'s `pinnedOnly` state; while on, the `combined` `useMemo` returns *only* the pinned clips (via the pure `lib/history-filter.ts::pinnedClips`, still query-filtered) and suppresses the whole command/snippet/launcher stack — a clean pinned-clips browser. It's reset on `popup-hidden` like the other transient view modes. **Clip notes (v0.84.106)**: a `note` column (lazy `ALTER TABLE`, AES-encrypted at rest like content) attaches a user note to a clip; noted rows are **highlighted yellow** in the list (+ note icon, text on hover) and are **exempt from the prune** too (`WHERE pinned = 0 AND note IS NULL`, both subquery + outer). `db::set_note` (empty string clears) + IPC `set_clip_note`; the note button sits top-right in `PreviewPanel`'s `meta` row (`NoteButton`: add / edit / delete popover, yellow when set). Notes round-trip through backup (restored after `upsert_clip` via `set_note`/`set_pinned`). **Clip lineage (v0.93.1)**: `derived_from` (source entry id) + `derived_kind` (the transform kind) columns (lazy `ALTER TABLE`, plaintext) record that a clip was produced by copying another one in a different shape. Written **only on insert** by `db::upsert_clip_derived` (`upsert_clip` delegates with `None`) — on a hash collision the existing row only gets its recency bumped, so an organically captured clip is never retroactively relabelled as derived (unit-tested). **Slim list (v0.84.16)**: the popup history *view* uses `db::list_slim`, which blanks `content_data` for `image` rows (the list renders an icon, not the bitmap, and image actions + paste re-read by id) — image clips hold multi-MB base64 PNGs, and shipping the full set on every popup-open / clipboard-change was decrypting + JSON-serialising + IPC-marshalling **~140 MB** (the "overlay loads slowly" bug). `get_history`/`search_history` use `list_slim`; the preview fetches a selected image's pixels on demand via the `get_clip` IPC (`db::get`). The full `db::list` (with image data) is retained for the backup export, which needs every byte. **Perf (v0.105.0):** `idx_pinned_recency(pinned DESC, last_used_at DESC)` serves both the list's ORDER BY (was full scan + temp B-tree per copy/open, EXPLAIN-verified) and the prune subquery; `prune_locked` + the cap read run only on a **genuine insert** (a hash-dedup bump can't grow the table) — together this un-quadratics the backup restore; and `list`/`list_slim`/`get` fetch raw rows under the mutex but **decrypt after dropping it** (`row_to_entry` raw + `decrypt_entry`, the totp_store pattern) so AES work never parks the watcher/tracking/stats behind the DB lock. The frontend additionally **skips the `clipboard-changed` refetch while the popup is hidden** (`useClipboardHistory` visibility gate; `window-shown` — now emitted by EVERY show path, not just the toggle — refreshes unconditionally). |
| `snippets` | Text expander templates | `abbreviation` + `title` + `body`; index on `abbreviation`. **No cap — never pruned** (unlike `entries`, capped at `MAX_ENTRIES = 1000`); `list_all` returns every row, so you can store arbitrarily many. **Storage readout (v0.95.0)**: `snippets::storage_stats` (IPC `get_snippet_storage` → `SnippetStorage { count, bytes }`) sums the **on-disk** column bytes (`SUM(LENGTH(CAST(abbreviation/title/body AS BLOB)))` — the body counts at its AES-ciphertext size = true footprint; `CAST AS BLOB` so `LENGTH` is bytes, not chars). Shown in **Settings → Snippets** (`SnippetsSection`, count + `formatBytes(bytes)` "on disk (encrypted)"; deep-link id `snippets`). **The Snippets-tab list is virtualised** (`@tanstack/react-virtual`, dynamic measurement, same pattern as `HistoryList`) so thousands of rows scroll smoothly. **Curated AI-prompt seed (`seed.rs`)**: 27 hand-written `ai*`-prefixed prompt templates in `seed/ai_prompts.json` are imported on first run (`maybe_seed_defaults`, guarded by `seed.default_snippets_v1`) and re-importable via the Snippets-tab "Restore defaults". Seed tests assert the count, `ai`-prefix, uniqueness, non-empty bodies. **Material-Design colour pack (v0.84.127)**: 255 `shortcut → hex` snippets in `seed/material_colors.json` (e.g. `reda700` → `D50000`, hex without `#`) are seeded the same way behind a **separate** flag (`seed.material_colors_v1`) so existing installs receive them on the next launch; `import_pack` does both, `restore_defaults` restores both. Tests assert count=255, unique lowercase shortcuts, 6-hex-digit bodies. **Snippet groups (`snippet_categories` table, v0.84.259)**: an id-keyed `snippet_categories(id, name UNIQUE, sort_order)` table + a nullable `snippets.category_id` FK (lazy `ALTER TABLE`) organises snippets into groups. The API exposes the group **NAME** (`SNIPPET_COLS`/`SNIPPET_FROM` LEFT-JOIN in every SELECT; `Snippet.category: Option<String>`), assignment is by **id** (`create`/`update`/`upsert_by_abbreviation` gained a `category_id: Option<i64>` arg; the upsert **preserves** the existing group when a category-less re-import passes `None`, so a plain snippet re-import never wipes grouping). Group CRUD: `create_category` (idempotent — returns the existing id if the name is taken, `sort_order = MAX+1`), `rename_category`, `delete_category` (**ungroups** its snippets — sets `category_id = NULL` — then deletes; snippets are never lost), `reorder_categories` (txn), `set_category`, `list_categories` (with per-group `count`), `category_id_for_name` (get-or-create, used by backup import). **Seeded groups**: `maybe_seed_defaults` creates "AI Prompts" + "Colors" and imports each pack into its group; `categorize_existing` is a one-time migration (flag `seed.categorized_v1`) that assigns the bundled AI/colour abbreviations to those groups **only where `category_id IS NULL`** (respects a user's manual grouping). IPC: `list_snippet_categories` · `create_/rename_/delete_/reorder_snippet_category(ies)` · `set_snippet_category`; `upsert_snippet` gained `category_id`. Frontend: the Snippets tab has a group-filter chip row (All / each group / Ungrouped), a group `<select>` (incl. inline "＋ New group…") in the editor, and a **Manage groups** sub-view (create / click-to-rename / ↑↓-reorder / inline-confirm delete) behind the folder button. Groups ride along in **both** snippet-only and full-settings backups **by name** (see Backup). |
| `notes` | Persistent bookmarks | Not pruned; `title` + `category`; any clipboard entry can be saved here |
| `settings` | Key/value app settings | Simple `key TEXT PK, value TEXT`; used for expander hotkey, theme, bruno defaults, input-lock chord, etc. |
| `totp_entries` | 2FA / TOTP accounts (v0.47.0) | `issuer` + `account` + `secret_enc` + `digits`/`period`/`algorithm`; index on `LOWER(issuer)` |

Rust unit tests use `Connection::open_in_memory()` — no temp files needed.

**Field-level encryption at rest (`crypto.rs`, v0.47.0).** Sensitive columns are AES-256-GCM-encrypted: `entries.content_text`, `entries.content_data`, `snippets.body`, `notes.content_text`, `notes.content_data`, and all TOTP secrets. Timestamps, IDs, content-type tags, hashes, abbreviations, titles, and categories stay plaintext (needed for sort/dedup/index). The key lives in the OS keychain (`keyring` crate; service `io.celox.inspector-rust`, user `history-db-key-v1`) with a `0600` `.dbkey` file fallback in the data dir. Storage format is `"v1:" + base64(12-byte nonce ‖ ciphertext+tag)`. `decrypt` is permissive — any value **not** prefixed `v1:` is treated as legacy plaintext and returned as-is, which is how `migrate_table` lazily upgrades pre-encryption rows. TOTP secrets are decrypted only on-demand for code generation and **never cross the IPC boundary**.

### Frontend data flow and `ListEntry` union

The history tab renders a unified `ListEntry` discriminated union (`lib/types.ts`):

```ts
type ListEntry =
  | { kind: "clip";               data: ClipEntry }
  | { kind: "snippet";            data: Snippet }
  | { kind: "calc";               data: CalcEntry }
  | { kind: "color";              data: ColorEntryView }
  | { kind: "command";            data: CommandEntryView }        // runnable power command
  | { kind: "command-suggestion"; data: CommandSuggestionView }   // autocomplete hint
  | { kind: "kill-target";        data: KillTargetView }          // process in the kill picker
  | { kind: "opener";             data: OpenerEntryView }         // German pickup line (hidden)
  | { kind: "finder-file";        data: FinderFileView }          // current Finder selection
  | { kind: "bruno";              data: BrunoEntryView }          // net-pay calculator result
  | { kind: "app";                data: AppEntryView }            // app-launcher hit
  | { kind: "pwgen";              data: PwgenEntryView }          // generated password
  | { kind: "bpm";                data: BpmTriggerView }          // BPM-detector launcher row
  | { kind: "totp-manage";        data: { label: string } }      // "2fa" → TOTP overlay launcher
  | { kind: "totp";               data: TotpListView }            // "otp <issuer>" autocomplete
```

Assembly order in `App.tsx` (`combined`): **runnable command first, then command suggestions (highest priority, v0.72.0/v0.74.0)** → opener → **all keyword-command rows (bruno/pwgen/bpm/2fa+otp/rz)** → app-launcher → calc result → color result → snippet matches → fuzzy clips. **INVARIANT — every keyword-triggered custom command outranks the app-launcher hit (`appEntry` is spliced AFTER all of them, v0.84.198).** Not just `commandEntry` + `suggestionEntries` but also the dedicated keyword rows (`brunoEntry`/`pwgenEntry`/`bpmEntry`/`totpManageEntry`/`totpAutocompleteEntries`/`resizePresetEntries`) must come before `appEntry` — otherwise an app whose name fuzzy-matches the keyword wins (e.g. the **"OTP Manager" app beating the `otp` command** — the v0.84.198 bug — or Terminal.app beating `terminal`). When adding a new keyword-command row, place it above `appEntry`.

**INVARIANT — a runnable single-row command needs a `case` in the `commandEntry` `switch` (`App.tsx`), not just a `dispatchCommand` branch.** `commandEntry` is what builds the `command` `ListEntry` that gets spliced to the **top** of `combined` (above fuzzy clips), giving commands their always-highest priority. The `switch`'s `default` arm `return null`s, so a command kind that's wired into `dispatchCommand` but **forgotten** in the `switch` produces **no** command row — and a clip whose text fuzzy-matches the keyword wins Enter instead. This was the v0.84.59 bug: typing `stats` (and `trim`) pasted a clip containing "stats" instead of running the command. The only kinds that intentionally fall through to `default` are those rendered as their **own** dedicated `ListEntry` (`pwgen` — explicit `return null`; `bruno` — `brunoEntry`) or handled as whole-list/whole-popup takeovers **before** the switch (`kill`, `meme`). When adding a new single-row command, add **both** the `dispatchCommand` branch **and** the `commandEntry` `case`. **Every keyword-triggered command row is rendered with a reddish (`rose`) accent** in `HistoryItem.tsx` (`isCustomCommand` → reddish chip, icon, row background; selected = solid `bg-rose-600`) so it's visually obvious you're about to trigger a command rather than paste a clip / launch an app. `isCustomCommand` covers **every row reached by typing a command keyword, uniformly** — including with a parameter (`kill slack`, `meme cat`, …): `command` + `command-suggestion`, the dedicated keyword-command rows `totp-manage` (2fa) · `totp` (otp) · `pwgen` · `bruno` · `bpm`, and the whole-list command pickers `kill-target` + `meme` (v0.79.1/v0.79.2 — previously only the generic command rows were tinted). **As of v0.84.27 `calc` is also in the set** — calculator/converter results get the rose accent + the command entrance/icon-pop, and the **search input itself** turns rose with a pulsing calculator icon (`SearchBar` `calcMode`), so computing feels as "active" as a keyword command. The remaining expression result (`color`) and non-command rows (`app`, `finder-file`, `clip`, `snippet`, `opener`) keep the neutral accent. (The `kill` chip stays the more-alarming `red-500` since it's destructive; everything else uses `rose`.) Several **whole-list / whole-popup overrides**: in **kill-mode** (`kill` parsed) the list becomes `kill-target` rows; **game-mode** replaces the whole popup with a game (`<PongGame>` `getshaky`, `<SnakeGame>` `rockthebox`/`rockthabox`, `<SpaceInvadersGame>` `spacer`, `<FlappyGame>` `learningtofly`); **`2fa`** replaces it with `<TotpOverlay>`; **`bpm`** (Enter) replaces it with `<BpmDetector>`; **`freeze`** starts the input lock.

**Show-while-fully-typed (v0.130.0).** A COMPLETE panel keyword renders its panel in the preview directly (the `sound` pattern, generalised) — Enter only hands over keyboard focus. Converted: `brightness` · `sound` · `hue` · `stats` · `boom` · `uptime` · `weather` · `clock` · `adb` · `tokens` · `snitch` (arg picks apps/map like the dispatch branch) · `rickroll` (a focus-gained effect retries `play()` — the Enter IS the autoplay gesture) · `nosleep` (**bare form only** — the panel acts on an `on`/`off` arg immediately, admin prompt, so those stay Enter-only) · plus the already-live `calendar`/`random`/`alias`. **Deliberately still Enter-activated:** `disk`/`loc`/`repo`/`clean` (heavy scans/clones must not fire per keystroke) and `shazam`/`iris`-bare (microphone). When adding a panel, pick the side by its mount side-effects, not by habit.

**INVARIANT — inline preview-panel commands canonicalise the query on open, ONCE, centrally (`dispatchCommand` top, v0.84.247).** Every command that renders in the right preview column and stays open via a `!isXCmd` auto-exit effect — `brightness`/`sound`/`hue`/`stats`/`boom`/`uptime`/`calendar`/`clean`/`snitch` (the `PANEL_KINDS` list) — must set the search query to its canonical keyword the moment it opens. Otherwise, entering the mode from a **partial fuzzy suggestion** (Enter on the `snitch` hint while the field still reads `sni`) leaves the query un-parseable as that command → the auto-exit effect fires on the same tick → the panel **flashes open-then-closed**. This is done in ONE place at the top of `dispatchCommand` (`if (PANEL_KINDS.includes(kind)) setQuery(...)`, keeping the arg for the arg-driven sub-views `calendar <date>` / `snitch map`), NOT per-branch — the per-command copy of this `setQuery` is exactly what got forgotten for `snitch` + `clean` (v0.84.246, "opens then instantly closes from a partial suggestion"). When adding a new preview-panel command, add its kind to `PANEL_KINDS`; do **not** re-add a per-branch `setQuery`.

Snippet matches come from `findSnippets(query)` — backend two-pass search (v0.105.0): abbreviation-prefix + title-contains in SQL, then a Rust **decrypt-filter over the bodies** for the remaining slots (the body column is AES-encrypted at rest, so the historical `LOWER(body) LIKE` matched ciphertext — body search silently never worked in production; the test process couldn't catch it because crypto is a passthrough there). Ranking: abbreviation prefix > title > body, pinned by tests. The inline calculator (`lib/calc.ts`) runs `tryEvaluate(query)` — returns non-null only when the input contains an operator, function, or constant. The inline **converter** (`lib/convert.ts`, v0.76.0) runs `tryConvert(query)` and surfaces a `calc` row too: unit conversions (`5 km in mi`, `72 f to c`, `2 gb in mb` — length/mass/data/time/speed factor tables + special-cased temperature), number-base (`0xff in dec`, `255 in hex`, `0b1010 in dec`), and epoch→ISO (`1717000000 as date`). It's only consulted when the calculator didn't match (mutually exclusive). Color rows come from `tryParseColor`. Command rows + suggestions come from `lib/commands.ts` (`parseCommand` / `commandSuggestions`).

**Smart preview actions (`lib/smart-actions.ts`, v0.76.0).** `detectSmartActions(text)` (pure + unit-tested) inspects the selected text clip and `PreviewPanel`'s `SmartActionsBar` renders one-tap buttons: a URL/bare-domain → **Open link**, an email → **Compose email** (`mailto:`), a phone number → **Call** (`tel:`), `lat,lng` → **Open in Maps**, plus a **Make QR** for any short single-line value (encodes via `lib/qr.ts` + the `qr_copy_png` IPC). Opens go through `tauri-plugin-opener`.

### Tabs

`App.tsx` manages `activeTab: "history" | "snippets" | "notes" | "features" | "settings"`. Each tab is a separate panel component:

| Tab | Component | Backing data |
|---|---|---|
| History | `HistoryList` + `PreviewPanel` | `useClipboardHistory` + `useFuzzySearch` |
| Snippets | `SnippetsPanel` | `useSnippets` |
| Notes | `NotesPanel` | `useNotes` |
| Features | `FeaturesPanel` | read-only catalogue; fetches live shortcuts (`get_popup_hotkey` / `get_history_hotkey` / `get_expander_config` / `get_direct_slots` / `get_input_lock_chord`) |

**Edit a snippet from the search results (`SnippetEditor.tsx`, v0.84.265).** Typing an abbreviation (`aisetup`) surfaces the snippet as a row; the preview's **✏️ Edit** button — or **Cmd/Ctrl+E** on the selected snippet row — swaps the read-only preview for the **real editor** (abbreviation / title / group / body), so a prompt can be fixed without leaving the search. The form is ONE component (`SnippetEditor`), used by both the Snippets tab and the preview — a second copy would drift. Three things make it work: (1) `App.tsx`'s `snippetEditingId` is in the `useKeyboardNav` `enabled` gate, so Enter saves instead of pasting the snippet (**Cmd/Ctrl+Enter** saves from any field — plain Enter in the body inserts a newline, it's a multi-line template); (2) a `snippetRev` counter is in the deps of the `findSnippets(query)` effect — the query doesn't change when you save, so without it the preview would keep showing the **pre-edit body**; (3) `snippetEditingId` is cleared on selection/query change (a mouse-click on another row would otherwise hide the editor but leave the nav gate closed) and on `popup-hidden`. After saving, focus returns to the search field with the query intact → Enter pastes the edited version straight away.

**Popup open hotkeys (two, both configurable).** The popup is opened by a **main** hotkey (`popup.hotkey`, default `Ctrl+Space`; `register_popup`) **and** an optional **second clipboard-history** hotkey (`popup.history_hotkey`, default `Ctrl+Shift+V`; `register_history_hotkey`, v0.83.0). Both live in `PopupShortcutState` (`current` + `history`), both call `toggle_popup`, both are validated against each other + the reserved globals (OCR/screenshot/eyedropper/finder/markdown/record) + the expander/direct-slots. An **empty** history string disables the second hotkey. IPC: `get_/set_/get_default_popup_hotkey` + `get_/set_/get_default_history_hotkey`; Settings → *Popup hotkey* + *Clipboard-history hotkey* (both `HotkeyCapture`).

**Configurable global action hotkeys (`hotkey.rs` `ActionId` registry, v0.84.128).** The 8 feature hotkeys that used to be hard-coded in `hotkey::register` — **OCR** (`Ctrl+Shift+O`), **screenshot** (`Ctrl+Shift+S`), **eyedropper** (`Ctrl+Shift+C`), **Finder selection** (`Ctrl+Shift+F`), **Markdown→PDF** (`Ctrl+Shift+M`), **screen recording** (`Ctrl+Shift+Alt+S`), **audio swap** (`Ctrl+Shift+Alt+M`), **Timesheet** (`Ctrl+Shift+T`) — are now **rebindable**, plus (v0.84.204) a 9th action **Toggle time tracking** (`Ctrl+Shift+Alt+T`, start/stop a timesheet session from anywhere with a status toast). Single source of truth: the `ActionId` enum (`key`/`label`/`default_spec`); each binding persists under settings `hotkey.<key>` (**empty string = disabled**, missing = default). One `dispatch_action(app, id)` holds all 8 handler bodies (preserving each one's threading: worker for OCR/shot/finder/audioswap/markdown, `run_on_main_thread` for record/timesheet + the window builds). `apply_action_hotkeys` (re)registers the whole set from a managed `ActionShortcutState` (unregister-then-register), called at startup (via `register`) and after any change. `all_bound_shortcuts(app, except)` gives the unified collision set (popup + history + expander + direct-slots + actions) so `set_action_hotkey` rejects conflicts with a human message. IPC: `list_action_hotkeys` / `set_action_hotkey` / `reset_action_hotkey`; Settings → **Global shortcuts** (`GlobalShortcutsSection.tsx`: per-row `HotkeyCapture` + Reset + Off + inline conflict). Fixes e.g. the Windows `Ctrl+Shift+T` clash with the browser's "reopen closed tab". **All hotkey validators share ONE live collision set (v0.84.221):** `live_bindings(app, owner)` (with a `BindingOwner` self-exclusion — Popup/History/DirectSlots/Action) replaces the legacy hard-coded default lists in `register_popup`, `register_history_hotkey` AND `register_direct_slots` — previously a freed default (e.g. OCR re-bound elsewhere) was still falsely blocked, while a *custom* action binding could be silently double-bound. `all_bound_shortcuts` is now a shim over it; the pure `find_conflict` is unit-tested. (The Features tab still shows the default specs.) Registered at startup in `lib.rs`. `hotkey::show_and_position` centres the popup (horizontally + ~⅓ down) on **the monitor under the cursor**. **macOS positions it SYNCHRONOUSLY via the native `NSWindow setFrameOrigin` (`position_popup_native_macos`, v0.84.182) — this is the reliable path, do not regress it.** Why native: Tauri's `window.set_position` **queues** the move onto the main-thread event loop, so it only applies *after* `show()` returns — the popup flashed on the *previous* monitor for a frame then jumped (read as "opens twice" / "open-close-open"; proven by the v0.84.181 diag logs, where `outer_position()` post-show equalled the *previous* call's position). The native objc2 call (`setFrameOrigin:` to the centred point on the NSScreen under the live `NSEvent mouseLocation`) applies immediately, before show → the popup opens once, already centred. The Tauri `clamp_into_monitor` path is the **fallback** (+ Windows/Linux); it sizes the centring from `native_window_logical_size` (the NSWindow frame in points — valid even while hidden, unlike `outer_size()`, which is **0/stale for a hidden macOS window** and used to make the bail-on-zero guard skip positioning) × the target monitor's scale. The cursor monitor (fallback path) is found via `hotkey::pick_cursor_monitor` → `screenshot_preview::pick_cursor_monitor_globally` (global `CGEventGetLocation`) — NOT `WebviewWindow::cursor_position()`, which is stale on the popup window (v0.84.8). **Entrance flash fix (v0.84.183):** the OS makes the window visible *before* the frontend's `window-shown` handler runs, so the shell briefly showed full-opacity content before `playEntrance` faded it in (a "flash/twitch"); `App.tsx`'s `popup-hidden` handler now **primes** the shell to the entrance start (`opacity:0` + `scale .97`) *while hidden*, and `playEntrance` (`fill:"forwards"` + a `finish` `settle` that clears the inline prime) fades in cleanly with no full-opacity flash. The point-in-monitor core is the pure, unit-tested `screenshot_preview::monitor_index_for_point` (half-open `[x,x+w)×[y,y+h)` bounds so adjacent monitors never both claim a shared edge; v0.84.55) — the per-OS branches differ only in obtaining the cursor + building the bounds (points on macOS, physical pixels on Windows/Linux).
| Settings | `SettingsPanel` | IPC to `settings.rs` + `expander.rs` |

**Features tab** (`FeaturesPanel.tsx`) — a read-only, tabular reference of every function grouped into *Global hotkeys* · *Search-bar commands* · *In-popup & preview actions* · *Hidden games*, each row showing the **currently configured** shortcut/trigger + a short how-to. Configurable hotkeys are fetched live on mount (the panel remounts on each tab switch, so values are current); fixed global hotkeys are literal constants mirroring `hotkey.rs`. Tauri shortcut specs are pretty-printed by `lib/platform.ts::formatHotkey` (`Alt+Digit1` → `⌥1`, `Ctrl+Shift+V` → `⌃⇧V` on macOS).

### Tauri events

The table maps each emitted event to where it's emitted and what the frontend does with it (grep `\.emit(` in `core/rust-lib/src` for the authoritative list).

| Rust `app.emit(...)` | Emitted from | Frontend reaction |
|---|---|---|
| `"clipboard-changed"` | `clipboard_watcher` + every `db::upsert_clip`-adjacent IPC | `useClipboardHistory` re-fetches the list |
| `"capture-state-changed"` | Tray "Pause Capture" toggle | Header label flips between paused / active |
| `"window-shown"` | `hotkey::show_popup` | Resets to History tab + focuses search bar |
| `"popup-hidden"` | `hotkey::hide_popup` | Clears any transient toast / inline editor that shouldn't survive between sessions |
| `"open-snippets-tab"` | Tray "Manage Snippets" | Frontend switches to Snippets tab |
| `"open-notes-tab"` | Tray "Manage Notes" | Frontend switches to Notes tab |
| `"ocr-permission-needed"` | OCR / Screenshot hotkey fails Screen Recording pre-check | Popup opens, Settings tab + amber banner with `Open System Settings` + `Force reset` |
| `"expander-permission-needed"` | Expander hotkey fails Accessibility pre-check | Popup opens, Settings tab + amber banner (same shape as OCR banner) |
| `"expander-blocked"` / `"expander-hotkey-forwarded"` | Expander hotkey handler | Diagnostics / toast feedback on a blocked or forwarded expansion |
| `"finder-automation-needed"` | `Ctrl+Shift+F` Finder read fails the Automation TCC pre-check | Settings tab + amber banner (same shape as OCR), errno -1743 path |
| `"finder-selection-loaded"` | `get_finder_selection` succeeds | Popup shows the selected files as `finder-file` rows |
| `"autostart-changed"` (v0.14.0) | Tray "Start at Login" toggle | Settings → Startup checkbox reconciles to the now-effective OS state |
| `"color-picked"` | `pick_screen_color` worker completes (NSColorSampler / GDI overlay) | `ColorPickerModal` stores the hex; payload is `string \| null` (`null` = cancelled) |
| `"screenshot-saved"` (v0.19.2) | Screenshot pipeline finishes in save-to-file mode | Frontend toast confirming the file path the PNG was written to |
| `"screenshot-pending"` | `run_screenshot_pipeline` stashes a capture | Spawns the floating `ScreenshotPreview` window (see Screenshot preview) |
| `"editor-screenshot-changed"` | Editor opened / pending screenshot swapped | `ScreenshotEditor` reloads the source PNG |
| `"timer-fired"` / `"timers-changed"` | `timer.rs` worker on expiry / list mutation | Popup banner on fire; footer LED count reconciles |
| `"wakelock-changed"` | `wakelock_set` | Footer keep-awake indicator toggles |
| `"bruno-defaults-changed"` | `bruno_set_defaults` | Settings → Bruno section re-reads defaults |

### Text expander (`expander.rs`)

**Dynamic placeholders (`snippet_template.rs`, v0.50.0+).** Snippet bodies are stored verbatim and expanded at *paste* time by the pure `snippet_template::render(body, now, clipboard) -> Rendered { text, cursor_back }`. Tokens: `{date}`/`{date:FMT}`, `{time}`/`{time:FMT}`, `{datetime}` (chrono strftime), `{clipboard}` (clipboard text at paste time), `{cursor}` (removed; the caret is repositioned there afterwards via `paste::move_cursor_left`), and `{{`/`}}` for literal braces; an unknown `{token}` or a malformed strftime is emitted verbatim (never panics). Rendering happens at the **leaf paste primitives** so every path gets it: `commands::paste_snippet` (popup, via `expander::render_snippet_body` — current clipboard), `expander::paste_over_selection` and `expand_via_clipboard` (the saved pre-cycle clipboard is the `{clipboard}` source, since the live clipboard transiently holds the abbreviation there), and the AX in-place `try_replace_word_before_cursor` arm. The Snippets editor shows a one-line placeholder cheat-sheet under the body field.

**Feedback sounds (`sound.rs`, v0.84.45; multi-cue + master toggle v0.84.57).** Short one-shot WAV cues for tactile feedback. The `Sound` enum maps each cue to an embedded WAV (`include_bytes!` from `core/rust-lib/assets/*.wav` — WAV not MP3 so there's no decoder spin-up): **`Expand`** (`paste_mechkey.wav`, mechanical-keyboard click), **`Ocr`** (recognition done), **`Screenshot`** (capture done), **`RecordStart`** / **`RecordStop`**, **`Copy`** (eyedropper hex → clipboard). `sound::play(Sound)` materialises the file once to the cache dir (memoised per-cue in a `Mutex<HashMap>`) and plays it on a worker thread via the per-OS CLI player (`afplay` / PowerShell `SoundPlayer` / `paplay`/`aplay`) — fire-and-forget so it never blocks the caller (expansion runs on the main thread on macOS). The **expand** cue (`play_paste()`, kept as a back-compat shorthand) fires at the three actual-expansion leaves — the AX in-place `Replaced` arm + `paste_over_selection` + `expand_via_clipboard` (each right after the paste/replace, so it never fires on a no-match) — and at the buffer/terminal hotkey path (`auto_expand::try_hotkey_expand()` returning true); passive auto-expansion (mode 4) is deliberately silent. The other cues fire from their `commands.rs` pipelines (`run_ocr_pipeline`, `run_capture_pipeline`, `start_/stop_screen_record`, `write_eyedropper_result`). A single **master toggle** (settings key `sound.enabled`, default on) gates every cue via an in-process `AtomicBool` (`set_enabled`/`is_enabled`) seeded from settings at startup (`lib.rs`) and updated by the `set_sound_enabled` IPC — so the hot path never reads the DB. Settings → **Sounds** (top of the panel). IPC: `get_sound_enabled` / `set_sound_enabled`. **Selectable screenshot sound (v0.111.0):** the `Screenshot` cue plays one of three shutter styles from the [`sound-effects`](../sound-effects) archive — `snap` (sharp finger snap, **default**), `dslr` (rich mirror-slap + double curtain + metal ring; authored as the new `screenshot_dslr` generator IN the archive so it stays reproducible, then vendored) or `switch` (mechanical switch click) — or `off` (only the screenshot stays silent; every other cue keeps following the master toggle). Assets `assets/shutter_{snap,dslr,switch}.wav` (the legacy `screenshot.wav` is gone); same atomic-seeded pattern as the master toggle (settings key `sound.screenshot_style`, `AtomicU8`, seeded in lib.rs setup), `normalise_screenshot_style` whitelists any stored value back to `snap` (a hand-edited value must never silence the cue by accident), and the pure `screenshot_asset(style)` keeps the structural WAV tests independent of the process-global atomic. Distinct per-style cache filenames so switching styles re-materialises the right WAV. `set_screenshot_sound` persists + applies + **plays the new sound once as preview** (picking a sound you can't hear would be guesswork). IPC: `get_screenshot_sound` / `set_screenshot_sound`; Settings → Sounds → "Screenshot sound" select.

Four expansion modes exist:

1. **Search-based** (always on): type an abbreviation in the search field → matching snippets appear at top of list → Enter pastes. Handled entirely in the frontend via `findSnippets()`.

2. **Abbreviation hotkey** (`expander.rs`, default hotkey `Alt+Digit1` — shown as `Alt+1`): fires from any app without opening the popup. Three paths via `text_field::FieldAccess::try_replace_word_before_cursor` → `ReplaceOutcome`:
   - **`Replaced`** — AX/UIA read the word + replaced it in place; on macOS this is verified by re-reading `AXValue`. No clipboard touch.
   - **`SelectionActive`** — AX *selected* the abbreviation but the in-place text set was a no-op (Electron / Chromium / Mac-Catalyst: WhatsApp, Slack, Discord, VS Code, …). `expander::paste_over_selection` pastes the body over the live selection (one clipboard write + paste + restore, **no** re-select).
   - **`Unsupported`** — the focused element exposes no settable text attributes → legacy cycle: save clipboard → `Opt/Ctrl+Shift+←` selects previous word → copy → look up → paste body → restore clipboard.
   Enabled/disabled + hotkey configurable in Settings tab (with `Alt+1`/`Alt+2`/`Alt+3` quick-pick presets). Pre-0.12 the default was `Alt+Backquote`, unreachable on German ISO Macs — `expander::migrate_legacy_default` bumps an un-customised install to `Alt+Digit1` once (idempotent).

   **Buffer-backed (v0.64.0) — works everywhere, including terminals.** When the abbreviation hotkey is enabled, the passive keystroke monitor (`auto_expand.rs`, see mode 4) is armed in *track-only* mode and the hotkey handler tries `auto_expand::try_hotkey_expand()` **first**: it expands the abbreviation the user just typed straight from the tracked keystroke buffer (longest-suffix match, ignoring the auto-trigger), via the blind-Backspace+paste injector — so it **never reads the focused field** and therefore works in terminals (iTerm2, Terminal.app, …) too. The legacy AX/UIA paths above remain the **fallback** for when the monitor isn't tracking (Accessibility ungranted) or the buffer didn't match (e.g. the word was pasted, not typed, or focus moved). `commands::set_expander_config` calls `auto_expand::apply` so enabling the hotkey arms the tracker; `apply` installs the monitor when **either** the hotkey **or** auto-expansion is enabled. **Diagnosability (v0.112.2):** the buffer-path decline logs `buffer len` + `ms since the tap last delivered ANY event` (never content — keystrokes are sensitive), and the terminal fallback emits `expander-blocked` with `"terminal-empty"` (len 0: a click/arrow/Enter reset the buffer, or the tap is dead — the age tells which) vs `"terminal"` (typed text matched no abbreviation); the two banners explain each case honestly. The old banner claimed terminals can't work at all — stale since v0.64.0. The track-only contract (hotkey-only config still fills the buffer + `match_buffer_now` expands) is pinned by `track_only_mode_fills_the_buffer_and_hotkey_matching_works`. **Both expansion paths MUST wait for the hotkey's `Alt` to be released before synthesizing keystrokes** (shared `expander::wait_for_alt_release`, ≤80 ms poll): the hotkey is `Alt+<key>`, and a still-held `Alt` turns our `Backspace` into Alt+Backspace (delete-word) and our `Cmd+V` into Alt+Cmd+V (not paste) — the intermittent v0.88.4 "deleted the word but pasted nothing / nothing happened in the terminal" bug (the AX path always waited; the buffer path v0.64.0 didn't).

3. **Direct hotkey → snippet slots** (`expander.rs` + `hotkey::register_direct_slots`, v0.13.0): bind a hotkey straight to a snippet — `expander::DirectSlot { hotkey, snippet_id }`, persisted as a JSON array under settings key `expander.direct_slots`. On press: `expander::paste_snippet_body` (AX-gated on macOS, runs on main thread) → **blind-delete the snippet's abbreviation length in Backspaces** (so typing `aiplan` + hotkey replaces, not appends — v0.25.2+; character count, multibyte-safe) → write body to clipboard → synthesize `Cmd/Ctrl+V` → restore clipboard. Reads nothing, so it still works **everywhere including terminals**; the Backspace approach is the trade-off — it deletes N chars before the cursor whether the user typed the abbreviation or not. `register_direct_slots` validates against collisions with the popup/OCR/abbreviation hotkeys + duplicates. `ExpanderShortcutState.direct: Vec<(Shortcut, i64)>`. IPC: `get_direct_slots` / `set_direct_slots`. Re-registered at startup from settings. Settings UI: "Direct hotkey → snippet" section (rows of `[HotkeyCapture] → [snippet <select>] [×]` + Add + Save).

4. **Passive auto-expansion** (`auto_expand.rs`, v0.56.0 — the aText / Espanso "it just works" mode): a **system-wide keystroke monitor** watches typing in any app, keeps the last ≤64 chars in a ring buffer, matches the suffix against the snippet abbreviations (longest wins, multibyte-safe) and expands **with no hotkey**. macOS uses an active `CGEventTap` on the main run loop (same raw-FFI pattern as `input_lock.rs`); Windows a `SetWindowsHookEx(WH_KEYBOARD_LL)` low-level hook on a dedicated message-loop thread (Windows runtime-unverified — written compile-clean from the macOS reference); Linux is a documented no-op (no rootless Wayland tap → the clipboard-paste fallback stays). **Pure core fully unit-tested:** `AbbrevTable` (suffix matcher), `AutoExpander::feed(KeyEvent) -> ExpandAction` state machine (delimiter vs. immediate trigger, word-boundary gate, case sensitivity, single-Backspace undo). The impure executor `expander::auto_expand_inject` (delete N backspaces → render placeholders → paste body → re-emit the trailing delimiter → restore clipboard) and `auto_undo_inject` run on the main thread (macOS, via `run_on_main_thread`) / a worker (Windows). An `INJECTING` guard + the Windows `LLKHF_INJECTED` flag stop our own synthetic keystrokes from re-triggering. Slow safety gates (`pre_inject_ok`: not-frontmost, not secure-input, **not a password field** via `text_field::is_focused_field_secure`) run in the deferred executor, keeping the hot monitor callback cheap. Settings keys `expander.auto_expand_{enabled,trigger,match_case,inside_words,undo}`; IPC `get_auto_expand_config`/`set_auto_expand_config`; the abbreviation table is rebuilt from the snippets table by every snippet-CRUD command (`auto_expand::rebuild_table`) and at startup via `auto_expand::apply` — which arms the monitor when **either** auto-expansion **or** the abbreviation hotkey (mode 2) is enabled (and, on macOS, Accessibility is granted). When only the hotkey is on, the engine's `cfg.enabled` is false so the monitor *tracks only* (never auto-expands); the buffer it maintains is what mode 2's `try_hotkey_expand` consumes. Settings UI: "Auto-Expansion (aText-Stil)" section (enable + trigger + match-case + inside-words + undo). The three existing modes are unchanged.

On macOS, if Accessibility isn't granted the hotkey handler short-circuits *before* the doomed cycle: `expand_at_cursor` returns the `expander::ERR_NO_ACCESSIBILITY` (`"ax.permission_denied"`) sentinel, and `hotkey::register_expander`'s callback pre-checks `accessibility_granted()` → on a miss it shows the popup + emits `"expander-permission-needed"` (frontend turns it into an amber banner). Mirrors the OCR `screen.permission_denied` path.

The Settings panel includes a **"Diagnose"** button that calls `diagnose_at_cursor` — runs the capture half (no paste) and returns what would have been matched (or, on macOS without Accessibility, an explanatory error), for debugging.

### Screen-region OCR (`region_picker.rs`, `ocr.rs`)

Triggered by `Ctrl+Shift+O` — literal Control on every OS (v0.14.1+), not Cmd on macOS; avoids the `⌘⇧O` collision with VS Code / IntelliJ "Go to Symbol". Registered alongside the popup hotkey in `hotkey::register` or via the tray's **OCR Region** menu. Pipeline lives in `commands::run_ocr_pipeline(app)`, shared between the IPC `ocr_region` command, the global-shortcut callback, and the tray handler. Always dispatched to a worker thread (`std::thread::spawn`) because `screencapture -i` blocks until the user finishes the marquee.

- **Region capture** (macOS) shells out to `/usr/sbin/screencapture -i -x -t png <tmpfile>`. Read the file back, delete it. Empty / missing file = user pressed Esc → return `region_picker::Cancelled`. **Windows** (v0.19.2+) uses a GDI fullscreen layered overlay in `region_picker.rs` — the user drags a marquee, the picker blits the selected rect into a PNG. No external tool. **Linux** (v0.25.0+) shells out to `grim` + `slurp` on Wayland, or `scrot -s` on X11 — a missing tool yields a descriptive error pointing at the `apt` package.
- **OCR** (macOS) uses Vision via raw `objc2` msg_send: `NSData::dataWithBytes:length:` → `VNImageRequestHandler.alloc().initWithData:options:` → `VNRecognizeTextRequest` (recognitionLevel=0/Accurate, usesLanguageCorrection=true) → `performRequests:error:` synchronously → enumerate `request.results` taking `topCandidates(1).string`. Vision is linked explicitly via `core/rust-lib/build.rs` (`cargo:rustc-link-lib=framework=Vision`). **Windows** (v0.19.2+) uses WinRT `Windows.Media.Ocr` + `Windows.Graphics.Imaging` — picks up whatever language packs are installed in *Settings → Time & Language*; COM is initialised per-thread on the worker and the WinRT futures are `.get()`-blocked to keep the pipeline synchronous. **Linux** (v0.25.0+) shells out to the `tesseract` CLI — write the PNG to a temp file, `tesseract <tmp> stdout -l <langs>`, read stdout; offline, no extra Rust deps (`apt install tesseract-ocr tesseract-ocr-eng`, `-deu` optional).
- **Output**: text written to system clipboard (with `WatcherState::mark_self_write` so the watcher doesn't recapture it), plus two history entries — **source PNG first, recognised text second** (v0.14.2+), so the text wins the later `last_used_at` and is the most-recent entry at the top of the list (Enter then pastes text, not the screenshot). Returns `OcrResult { text, cancelled, chars }` so the frontend can show "recognised N chars" toasts.

### Screen-region screenshot (`commands::run_screenshot_pipeline`, v0.15.0)

Triggered by `Ctrl+Shift+S` (literal Control on every OS) or the tray's **Screenshot Region** menu. Same `region_picker::capture` + Screen-Recording TCC gate as OCR but **no OCR step** — the captured PNG is written straight to the system clipboard via `ClipboardContext::set_image` and persisted to history as a `[screenshot · N B]` image entry. Works on regions that contain no recognisable text (charts, buttons, photos, UI mockups). `mark_self_write(Image, b64)` arms the watcher to skip the round-trip. IPC: `screenshot_region` returns `ScreenshotResult { cancelled, bytes }`. `register_direct_slots` rejects `Ctrl+Shift+S` alongside the popup/OCR/abbreviation hotkeys.

**Capture modes (v0.57.0).** `region_picker::CaptureMode { Region, Fullscreen, Window }` adds non-interactive **full-screen** and **active-window** capture plus a **self-timer** and **repeat-last**, all feeding the same staging → clipboard → floating-preview flow via the generalised `commands::run_capture_pipeline(app, mode, delay_seconds)` (`run_screenshot_pipeline` is now the region/no-delay shorthand). macOS: `screencapture -x` (fullscreen) / `screencapture -w -o` (Apple's click-a-window picker); Windows: `region_picker::win_impl::capture_fullscreen` (virtual-screen GDI blit) / `capture_window` (foreground-window rect blit, both reuse `extract_png`) — **Windows runtime-unverified**; Linux: `grim`/`scrot` full-screen, window falls back to full-screen. IPC `screenshot_capture(mode, delay_seconds)` (persists `screenshot.last_mode`) + `screenshot_repeat_last`. Search-bar commands `shot [n]` · `shotfull` · `shotwin` · `shotlast`.

### Screen recording (`screen_record.rs`, v0.81.0; pause/resume v0.82.0)

Triggered by **`Ctrl+Shift+Alt+S`** (⌃⇧⌥S — literal Control + Alt on every OS; the extra Alt keeps it distinct from `Ctrl+Shift+S` screenshot-region. Registered in `hotkey.rs`; the hotkey handler dispatches to the **main thread** via `run_on_main_thread` because it builds a window). Records a screen **region** to an **MP4 (H.264)** with the **same workflow on macOS, Windows 11, and Linux** (Linux x11grab added v0.84.0; X11 / XWayland only). Flow: hotkey → `commands::screen_record_open_overlay` builds a fullscreen transparent `record-overlay` window (`RECORD_OVERLAY_LABEL`) covering **the monitor under the cursor** (a single window can't reliably span mixed-DPI monitors, so it covers just the cursor's screen). The cursor's monitor is found via the **global** cursor query `screenshot_preview::pick_cursor_monitor_globally` (`CGEventGetLocation`, point-space bounds-check) — NOT `WebviewWindow::cursor_position()`, which is stale on a freshly-built window and always resolved to the primary (the v0.84.6 bug where a secondary-monitor selection never worked; fixed v0.84.7). Geometry (`set_position`+`set_size` to the monitor's physical pos/size) is applied before+after `show` **and re-applied ~90 ms later** on the main thread, because `set_size(PhysicalSize)` converts via the window's current scale factor, which lags a move to a different-scale display. Esc aborts from anywhere via a **temporary global Esc shortcut** (`arm_overlay_escape`/`disarm_overlay_escape`), so the user needn't click the focus-less overlay first; disarmed on cancel / record-start. **`arm_overlay_escape` MUST run on a worker thread** (`std::thread::spawn`), never inline: `screen_record_open_overlay` executes *inside* the global-shortcut event handler when triggered by the record hotkey, and that handler holds the plugin's manager mutex — calling `global_shortcut().unregister`/`on_shortcut` inline re-entered that mutex and **deadlocked the main thread**, hanging the whole app (no hotkey fired again, beach-ball) until the recorder was used once (the v0.84.7→v0.84.10 bug). The worker blocks on the mutex only until the handler returns, then arms. Move the cursor to a different screen before triggering to record it. → `RecordOverlay.tsx` runs three phases (**select** marquee-drag → **configure** System/Mic audio toggles + Record → **countdown** 3-2-1, then goes fully transparent for ~150 ms so the first frames are clean) → `start_screen_record(region, audio)` spawns ffmpeg, closes the overlay (on the main thread — closing isn't a build, so it's safe), then **opens the `record-stop` bar from a worker thread** (`std::thread::spawn`; `RECORD_STOP_LABEL`, `RecordStopBar.tsx` — pulsing red dot + elapsed timer + **Pause/Resume** + Stop). **The worker thread is essential**: a sync `#[tauri::command]` runs *on* the main thread, and calling `WebviewWindowBuilder::build()` there **deadlocks** (the build needs the main-thread event loop to pump, but the command blocks it) — this was the v0.81/v0.82.0 bug where the stop bar never appeared. Building from a worker thread lets Tauri marshal the window creation onto the event loop cleanly, the same proven pattern as `screenshot_editor::open_editor`/`show_preview` (v0.82.1). **Stop** → `stop_screen_record` finalises + concatenates the segments, reveals the MP4 in Finder/Explorer (`reveal_in_file_manager`), emits `"recording-saved"`. Region is sent in **physical pixels** (the overlay converts its CSS marquee rect × `devicePixelRatio`; W/H floored to even for `yuv420p`). **Multi-monitor (v0.84.5):** `start_screen_record` adds the overlay window's screen position so the region becomes **absolute virtual-desktop** coords. Windows/Linux capture the whole desktop with absolute offsets (`gdigrab -offset_x` / `x11grab :0.0+x,y`) so any monitor works directly. macOS `avfoundation` captures one display at a time, so `resolve_args` uses `cg_displays::physical_rects` (CoreGraphics `CGGetActiveDisplayList`/`CGDisplayBounds`) + the pure `pick_display_for_region` to find which display the region lands on, picks that `Capture screen N` device (`pick_screen_index_n`), and crops relative to that display. (macOS multi-display assumes a uniform scale factor across monitors; mixed-DPI may be slightly off.)

- **Engine: ffmpeg** — the only engine giving one cross-platform workflow + MP4 (native ScreenCaptureKit + Windows.Graphics.Capture would be two divergent implementations). `ffmpeg_path()` probes PATH + the usual install dirs; a miss returns the `record.no_ffmpeg` sentinel (frontend shows an install hint).
- **macOS:** `avfoundation` — `resolve_args` runs `ffmpeg -f avfoundation -list_devices true -i ""`, the **pure** `parse_avf_devices`/`pick_*_index` parsers pick the screen-capture video index + the BlackHole-style system-loopback + the mic, region via `-vf crop=W:H:X:Y`. **Windows:** `gdigrab` (`-offset_x/-offset_y/-video_size`) + `dshow` audio (parsed by `parse_dshow_audio`/`pick_dshow_*`) — **runtime-unverified**. **Linux (v0.84.0):** `build_args_linux` uses `x11grab` (`-video_size WxH -i <DISPLAY>+X,Y`, region captured directly — no crop) + PulseAudio (`default.monitor` for system, `default` for mic); X11 / XWayland only (`resolve_args` errors if no `DISPLAY`). Two audio tracks are mixed with `amix=inputs=2`. The **mic is boosted `+10 dB`** (`MIC_GAIN`; macOS built-in mics record quiet — applied via `-af volume=` for mic-only or a `volume=` node on the mic input inside the filter graph for "both"; system/loopback audio is left untouched). Output: `libx264 -preset ultrafast -pix_fmt yuv420p -r 30 -movflags +faststart` → `dirs::download_dir()/Recording-<ts>.mp4`. **Audio quality (v0.84.24):** every audio-bearing path (and the `atempo` re-encode) sets `-b:a 256k -ar 48000` (`push_audio_quality`) — without an explicit `-b:a`, ffmpeg's native AAC encoder defaulted to ~62 kbps, which sounded crackly on music/system audio; the captures also get `-thread_queue_size 1024` (`THREAD_QUEUE`) to avoid dropped-packet clicks under load. The stop-time post-process (`fix_audio_sync`) always runs **`adeclick`** (+ conditional `atempo`) — BlackHole-loopback system audio clicks because the playing app and ffmpeg use different clocks on the virtual device (diagnosed: the capture path is silent-clean); `adeclick` removes the impulse noise and leaves clean audio untouched (verified), so it's safe to always apply. A *complete* loopback-crackle fix would need native ScreenCaptureKit capture (larger macOS-only change). **`-r 30` locks the output to CFR** — the avfoundation screen input reports an undefined "1000k fps" nominal rate, so without the lock the output timebase is irregular (can play too fast in some players + makes the pause/resume concat unreliable).
- **Audio-sync post-process (`fix_audio_sync`, v0.84.14).** avfoundation **systematically under-delivers audio samples** — a clip whose video spans N s (steady CFR frames) holds only ~85–90 % of `N × sample_rate` audio samples (verified: a real 9.27 s recording = 8.14 s of samples). The samples are continuous (no silence gaps), so the audio is *time-compressed*: plays ~1.15× too fast, runs out before the video ends. **The trap (which defeated v0.84.12/v0.84.13):** the MP4 muxer writes *stretched* PTS for the under-delivered audio, so the audio stream's `duration` metadata reads ≈ the video length (a lie) — only the decoded **sample count** (`astats` "Number of samples", immune to PTS) is ground truth. `stop()` therefore measures the finished file (true audio samples via `count_audio_samples` ÷ sample_rate, vs. video stream `duration` via `ffprobe`) and, when they diverge >2 %, re-syncs with one **pitch-preserving `atempo`** pass (`-c:v copy -c:a aac`, no inline resampler → no stutter/crackle). The factor is computed **per recording** (the shortfall varies per run); the pure `atempo_ratio(samples, rate, video_s)` (clamped to atempo's `[0.5,2.0]`, returns `None` within 2 %) is unit-tested. The pass is a no-op when audio is already in sync, so it's safe on every platform (Windows/Linux paths that don't under-deliver skip it). Only runs when a track was captured. `ffprobe` is located as a sibling of the resolved `ffmpeg` (`ffprobe_path`). The inline `apad`+`-shortest` only fills a missing *tail*; it does **not** fix the compression — that's this pass's job.
- **Pause/resume = segment + concat (v0.82.0).** ffmpeg can't truly pause a live capture, so each contiguous run is recorded to its own temp segment in `dirs::cache_dir()/InspectorRust/recordings/`. `pause` finalises the current segment (sends `q`, waits); `resume` spawns a fresh ffmpeg into the next segment (re-runs `resolve_args` — the device list rarely changes); `stop` finalises the last segment and concatenates them all losslessly via ffmpeg's concat demuxer (`-f concat -safe 0 -c copy`, no re-encode). A single never-paused segment is just `move_file`'d to the output (rename, copy+remove fallback across volumes), skipping concat. The stop bar's elapsed timer freezes while paused (frontend-tracked). `concat_list_contents` (single-quote-escaped list body) is pure + unit-tested.
- **State:** `RecordState { Mutex<Option<Session>> }` (managed Tauri state); `Session { region, audio, ffmpeg, final_out, segments: Vec<PathBuf>, current: Option<(Child, PathBuf)>, seq }` — `current` is `None` while paused. The pure arg-builders (`build_args_macos`/`build_args_windows`) + device parsers + concat-list builder + the `atempo_ratio` audio-sync math are unit-tested (no ffmpeg needed). **Caveats:** ffmpeg must be installed. **System audio auto-routing (v0.84.21):** macOS avfoundation can only *capture* a loopback (BlackHole), which is silent unless the system **output** is routed through it. When recording with system audio, `screen_record::arrange_system_audio` calls `audio::loopback_capture_target()` — which inspects each output device's **CoreAudio aggregate sub-device list** (`kAudioAggregateDevicePropertyFullSubDeviceList`) for a BlackHole/loopback member alongside a real output — and, if the current default output doesn't already route to a loopback, temporarily switches the default output to that Multi-Output device (so audio is captured *and* audible), restoring the previous device in `stop()` (`Session.restore_output`). Verified end-to-end (captured BlackHole went −91 dB → −18 dB). If no loopback-capable Multi-Output exists, recording proceeds with a silent system track and a `tracing::warn!` (the user must create one in Audio MIDI Setup). The routing is arranged *before* ffmpeg spawns (+150 ms settle). macOS may prompt ffmpeg for its own Screen-Recording TCC the first time. IPC: `screen_record_open_overlay`, `cancel_record_overlay`, `start_screen_record`, `pause_screen_record`, `resume_screen_record`, `stop_screen_record`, `is_recording`. Frontend wrappers in `ipc.ts`; both windows routed in `main.tsx` and listed in each `capabilities/default.json`.

### Social download + trim (`social_dl.rs`, `media_trim.rs`, v0.84.28)

**Social-media download** (YouTube / Instagram / TikTok / Facebook via `yt-dlp`). `social_dl::detect_platform(url)` (pure, tested) classifies a URL by host; `lib/social.ts::detectSocial` mirrors it in the frontend. When a **clip** or the **search-bar query** contains a social URL, App.tsx surfaces a `social` `ListEntry` (and the clip preview's `SocialDownloadBar` does too) → the preview offers **Download video** (all platforms) + **Download audio** (YouTube only). `build_dl_args` (pure, tested) builds the yt-dlp argv: video = `-f bv*+ba/b --merge-output-format mp4`, audio = `-x --audio-format m4a`; `--` guards against argv flag-smuggling and `download` rejects non-social URLs before spawning. Output → `~/Downloads`, revealed. **Smart library names (`media_name.rs`, v0.129.0):** the download is renamed to `Artist - Track (Edition).ext` — the `--print after_move:` marker line (unit-separator-delimited, `|` defaults so absent fields are EMPTY not "NA") carries filepath + artist/track/title/uploader/release_year; the pure, exhaustively-tested `smart_stem` prefers music metadata (artist+track), else splits the title at its first ` - `/` – `/` — `/` | `, else uses the cleaned uploader (` - Topic`/glued `VEVO`/trailing `Official` stripped). Bracket groups are CLASSIFIED, not copied: junk words dropped (`Official Video`, `4K`, `Lyric`, …), keeper-indicated groups survive (`live`, `remix`, `feat`, years, …) — a mixed `(Official Live Video)` keeps only `(Live)`, and a kept `Remaster` without a year picks up `release_year`. `smart_rename` is best-effort + collision-safe (never overwrites, any failure keeps the original path); no metadata → `strip_id_suffix` at least removes the ` [videoid]` tail. IPC `social_download` (async) + `social_ytdlp_available`. Enter on the `social` row downloads the selected mode; **for a YouTube row, Tab flips video↔audio (v0.84.37)** — `App.tsx` tracks `socialMode` (reset per URL) + bumps a `socialRunSignal` on Enter that the (now controllable) `SocialDownloadBar` consumes to run the chosen mode, so the keyboard path shows the same progress animation as a click; the Tab-selected button is highlighted. **Auth-wall / anti-bot retry (v0.84.31, generalised v0.84.130):** if an anonymous download fails with an auth/anti-bot wall — YouTube's "Sign in to confirm you're not a bot" **or** a TikTok/Instagram/Facebook login-required / `--cookies` hint (`is_bot_block`, broadened beyond YouTube) — `download` retries with `--cookies-from-browser` over `chrome`/`firefox`/`brave`/`edge` (first success wins; Safari omitted — macOS sandboxing blocks its cookie DB). The final error message is **platform-named** (`Platform::display_name`), not hard-coded "YouTube". First use may prompt for Keychain access to Chrome Safe Storage. **Stale-yt-dlp hint (v0.84.258):** a failure whose stderr matches `looks_stale_or_rate_limited` (pure, tested — "content isn't available"/"requested format is not available"/"nsig extraction failed"/"rate-limited"/"please report this issue on github.com/yt-dlp") appends an actionable hint to update yt-dlp (`brew upgrade yt-dlp` / `yt-dlp -U`) or wait out a rate-limit — the #1 cause of YouTube breakage is an outdated yt-dlp (YouTube changes constantly). The `youtube:player_client=default,ios,web_safari` SABR workaround (`build_dl_args`) is still applied. **Note:** the app calls the *system* yt-dlp — it's not bundled — so YouTube fixes require the user to keep yt-dlp updated. **While the download runs (v0.84.34)** the `SocialDownloadBar` replaces the buttons with `DownloadAnimation` — a Material 3 Expressive flourish (bobbing download glyph in two expanding accent rings over a scrolling wavy indeterminate-progress line; CSS keyframes `md3-dl-ring`/`md3-dl-icon`/`md3-dl-wave`, `prefers-reduced-motion`-aware).
