**Trim** (`trim` search-bar command → `trim-overlay` window). `media_trim::build_trim_args` (pure, tested): **lossless** = input-side seek + `-c copy` (fast, keyframe-snapped); **frame-accurate** = output-side seek + re-encode (`libx264` for video, AAC 256 k). `apply_trim` probes `has_video_stream` for codec choice; output is a sibling `<stem>-trim.<ext>` (lossless keeps the container; re-encode → mp4/m4a), revealed. `TrimOverlay.tsx` auto-opens a file dialog, then shows duration + start/end sliders + the mode toggle. IPC `trim_open_overlay` / `trim_cancel_overlay` / `trim_file_info` / `trim_apply` (async). Both new windows are in each `capabilities/default.json` + routed in `main.tsx`.

### Audio swap — replace/overlay a video's audio (`audio_swap.rs`, `AudioSwapOverlay.tsx`, v0.84.22)

**`Ctrl+Shift+Alt+M`** (⌃⇧⌥M — the extra Alt keeps it distinct from `Ctrl+Shift+M` markdown→PDF) reads the **Finder selection**, finds the first video (`audio_swap::is_video_path`, by extension), stashes it in `AudioSwapState`, and opens the **`audio-swap` overlay window** (`commands::build_audio_swap_overlay`, a centered decorated window). Threading mirrors the recorder: the hotkey handler reads the selection on a **worker thread** (slow osascript), then dispatches the window build to the **main thread** via `run_on_main_thread`. In the overlay you pick an audio source — a **local file** (native open dialog) or a **YouTube track** (`yt-dlp -x --audio-format m4a`, found on PATH like ffmpeg; missing → `ERR_NO_YTDLP` install hint) — set a **start position** in the video + optional **trim** (in/out) via sliders, choose **Replace** (drop original audio) or **Mix** (keep original + overlay, with volume sliders), and Apply.

- **Engine: ffmpeg** (video stream-copied `-c:v copy` → fast/lossless; only audio re-encoded to AAC). Output is a **sibling** `<stem>-audioswap.mp4` (non-destructive), revealed in Finder. The pure argv builders (`build_swap_args` — Replace uses `[1:a]atrim,asetpts,adelay,apad` + `-map 0:v -map [a] -t <video_dur>`; Mix uses `…amix=inputs=2:duration=first:normalize=0`) and `build_ytdlp_args` + `output_path_for` + `is_video_path` are unit-tested (8 tests, no ffmpeg). `apply_swap` downgrades Mix→Replace if the video has no audio stream (`has_audio_stream` via ffprobe). Verified end-to-end (Replace: silence before the start position, inserted tone after; Mix: both tracks).
- **IPC** (`commands.rs`): `open_audio_swap_overlay`, `audio_swap_get_selected_video`, `audio_swap_probe` (ffprobe duration), `audio_swap_ytdlp_available`, `audio_swap_download_youtube` (async → off-main, to `dirs::cache_dir()/InspectorRust/audioswap`), `audio_swap_apply` (async; emits `audio-swap-done`), `audio_swap_cancel_overlay`. `SwapSpec` is `#[serde(rename_all="camelCase")]` for clean frontend interop. The `audio-swap` window is in each `capabilities/default.json` and routed in `main.tsx`. `Ctrl+Shift+Alt+M` is in the reserved-hotkey sets (popup / history / direct-slots can't collide).

### Eyedropper — global hotkey (`commands::run_eyedropper_pipeline`, v0.17.0; custom loupe v0.84.69)

Triggered by `Ctrl+Shift+C` or the tray's **Pick Color** menu. **macOS now uses a custom loupe** (`color_loupe.rs` + `commands::open_color_loupe`) instead of `NSColorSampler` — see *Color loupe* below — so the **live hex is shown right under the magnifier** (Apple's loupe can't render that). Windows still uses `screen_picker::pick_color_blocking` (GDI overlay). The eyedropper **does not open the popup** the way `pick_screen_color` (the in-modal entry point) does.

**Color loupe (`color_loupe.rs`, `ColorLoupe.tsx`, v0.84.69).** Both eyedropper entry points (the `Ctrl+Shift+C` hotkey → clipboard+history; the modal *Pick from screen* → `color-picked` event) call `open_color_loupe(app, event_mode)` on macOS. It runs on a worker thread: pick the cursor's display index (`pick_cursor_monitor_globally` matched against `screen_record::cg_displays::physical_rects()` order), one-shot **snapshot** that display (`screencapture -x -D <n>`), stash it (base64) + the mode in `LoupeState`, build a fullscreen transparent `color-loupe` overlay on the cursor's monitor (same builder + multi-DPI caveats + worker-thread-arm-Esc pattern as the record overlay), and arm a global Esc. `ColorLoupe.tsx` loads the snapshot into an offscreen canvas, hides the OS cursor, and on a `requestAnimationFrame` loop draws a circular magnifier (nearest-neighbour zoom + pixel grid + centre reticle) that follows the mouse, reads the centre pixel via `getImageData` → hex, and renders the **live hex under the loupe** (enlarged 21 px mono; v0.84.85) with a per-char **airport split-flap** flip (`.loupe-flap` `rotateX` keyframe, `prefers-reduced-motion`-guarded) **plus** the "heat" colour flare each time a digit changes. Click → `color_loupe_pick(hex)`; Esc/`color_loupe_cancel`. **Snapshot-based** (not per-frame capture) → smooth; **reuses the Screen-Recording grant** already needed for OCR/screenshots (the old NSColorSampler path needed none — this is the trade-off for the live hex). The NSColorSampler path (`screen_picker::pick_color_async`) is retained `#[allow(dead_code)]` as a revert fallback. IPC: `color_loupe_data` / `color_loupe_pick` / `color_loupe_cancel`; `color-loupe` window in each `capabilities/default.json`, routed in `main.tsx`. **macOS only** so far (Windows keeps the GDI picker; Linux unchanged). On result: the hex string is written to the system clipboard via `ClipboardContext::set_text`, marked self-write so the watcher skips it, and persisted as a Text history entry. Cleanup (`clear_eyedropper_no_popup`) defers `demote_to_accessory` + `suppress_hide` clear via a 500 ms thread so the macOS focus-loss event from the policy demote doesn't fire before we want it to. No Screen Recording TCC grant needed — NSColorSampler / GDI overlay don't go through `screencapture`. IPC: `eyedropper_to_clipboard`. `register_direct_slots` rejects `Ctrl+Shift+C` alongside the popup/OCR/screenshot/abbreviation hotkeys.

**Multi-screen note (v0.19.1+)**: before hiding the popup, both eyedropper entry points call `hotkey::park_on_cursor_monitor` — `NSColorSampler` renders its loupe on the calling app's *primary* screen, which macOS derives from the last-active window. Parking the hidden popup on the cursor's monitor anchors the activation there so the loupe appears under the cursor, not on the main display.

### Power commands — search-bar palette (`commands.rs`, `lib/commands.ts`, v0.18.0+)

The search bar parses shell-style commands via `lib/commands.ts::parseCommand`. Complete commands surface as a `command` `ListEntry`; partial / **fuzzy** keywords surface as `command-suggestion` autocomplete rows. **Platform gating (v0.84.1):** a `CommandSpec` may carry an optional `platform?: ("mac"|"win"|"linux")[]`; `isCommandAvailable(spec)` (against `platform.ts::CURRENT_PLATFORM`) gates it. App.tsx filters both the runnable `parsedCommand` and the suggestion list by availability so a command whose backend doesn't exist on the current OS (e.g. `freeze` off macOS; `touch`/`mkdir`/`terminal`/`md2pdf` on Linux) never surfaces — no silent failures. The pure parsers stay platform-agnostic (deterministically testable); gating is applied at the render layer only. `commandSuggestions` uses `lib/commands.ts::fuzzyScore` (v0.52.0): exact > prefix (shorter keyword wins) > a **first-char-anchored subsequence** for 3+ char queries (so `wlk`→`wakelock`, `frz`→`freeze`, `pwg`→`pwgen`; 1–2 char queries stay prefix-only to avoid flooding). **Enter on a suggestion whose completion is a complete command runs it in one keystroke** — `App.tsx`'s `activate` shares a `dispatchCommand(kind, arg)` helper between the `command` and `command-suggestion` rows; it returns `false` for kinds it doesn't own (pwgen → has its own preview row, kill → kill-mode), so those fall back to autocompleting the input instead. Tab / → still autocompletes without running. The `COMMANDS` catalogue (fuzzy-matchable):

| Keyword | Action | Backed by |
|---|---|---|
| `tren` / `trde` / `tr <text>` | Live translate in preview; Enter copies, ⇧Enter opens Google Translate; click boxes to copy | frontend + `translate.rs` |
| `trde2it` / `trit2de` / `trde2sp` / `trsp2de` / `trde2pl` / `trpl2de` (v0.75.0) | German↔Italian / German↔Spanish (`sp`→`es`) / German↔Polish Google Translate. All translate commands are data-driven from `TRANSLATE_LANGS` (`{sl,tl,target}` per `CommandKind`) — `translateUrl` + the row label/hint + `isTranslateKind` all read it, so a new pair is one map entry + one `COMMANDS` row | frontend only |
| `g` / `ddg` / `gh` / `yt` / `npm` / `crates` / `so` / `mdn` / `wiki` `<query>` (v0.76.0) | Web-search **bangs** — open a site's search for the query. Data-driven from `SEARCH_BANGS` (`{name, url(q)}` per keyword), which also generates the `COMMANDS` rows (kind `websearch`); dispatch looks up the bang by keyword | frontend only |
| `uuid [n]` / `slug <text>` / `hash <text>` / `json` / `jwt` (v0.76.0) | Dev quick-tools → clipboard: random v4 UUID(s) · URL-safe slug · SHA-256 hex · pretty-print clipboard JSON · decode clipboard JWT (header+payload). Pure helpers in `lib/devtools.ts` (`slugify`/`generateUuids`/`sha256Hex` via Web Crypto/`formatJson`/`decodeJwt`); `json`/`jwt` read the clipboard, the rest transform the arg | frontend only |
| `qr <text>` (v0.76.0) | Generate a QR code — **previews live in the right `PreviewPanel`** (canvas, black-on-white so it always scans), Enter copies the **PNG to the clipboard** (+ history). `lib/qr.ts` (`qrMatrix` pure + unit-tested, `drawQr`/`qrPngBase64` canvas) uses the dependency-free `qrcode-generator`; the PNG base64 is handed to the `qr_copy_png` Rust IPC (decode → `image_ops::write_clipboard_png_canonical` → arm `mark_self_write` with the **canonical** b64 + history upsert with that same payload). **Canonical b64 (v0.84.35):** the PNG is re-encoded through clipboard-rs's own encoder — exactly what the watcher reads back — so our own clipboard write is recognised by the fuse and the watcher doesn't add a duplicate `[image W×H]` row next to the `[qr · …]` one; the stored clip is byte-identical to the clipboard | `lib/qr.ts` + `qr_copy_png` |
| `rz <W>x<H>` | **Resize the selected image(s) in Finder/Explorer** (v0.84.72). The dimensions accept `200x200`, `200 x 200`, **or a plain space `200 200`** (`parseResizeArg`, v0.84.73). Reads the **live** selection (`get_finder_selection` — no need to be in finder-mode), writes `<stem>-WxH.<ext>` next to each (Lanczos3; PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF/BMP). Falls back to the **clipboard image** (16 MP cap) when nothing usable is selected / Automation isn't granted. Mirrors `optim`'s selection-first behaviour. | `image_ops::resize_file_to_neighbor` (IPC `resize_file`) + `resize_clipboard_image` |
| `optim` | **Compress the selected image(s) in Finder/Explorer** (v0.84.71): reads the **live** selection (`get_finder_selection` — no need to be in finder-mode first), writes `<stem>-optim.<ext>` next to each — **PNG** lossless via `oxipng`, **JPEG** re-encoded at q85 (kept only if smaller). Falls back to the **clipboard PNG → Downloads** when nothing usable is selected / Automation isn't granted. | `image_ops::optimize_file_to_neighbor` (IPC `optimize_file`) + `optimize_clipboard_image` |
| `rmvvls <text>` | Strip vowels (aeiou + AEIOU + ä/ö/ü) → clipboard | IPC `remove_vowels_to_clipboard` |
| `kill [-9] [pattern]` | Process kill picker (see System commands) | `system_commands` |
| `reboot` / `shutdown` / `lock` | System power / lock (macOS) | `system_commands` |
| `mute` | Toggle system mute (macOS) | IPC `toggle_mute` |
| `freeze` | Input lock (block keyboard+mouse until unlock chord) | `input_lock` |
| `wakelock on` / `wakelock off` (alias `caffeine on/off`) | Keep-awake on / off (arg parsed by `parseWakelockArg`; the old `=1`/`=0` syntax was removed v0.52.0) | `wakelock` |
| `touch <name> [> text]` / `mkdir <name>` | Create a file / folder in the frontmost Finder (macOS) / Explorer (Windows) window's folder. **`touch` accepts inline content (v0.84.74):** `touch hallo.txt > das ist ein test` writes that content (split on the first `>`). **Nested relative paths (v0.84.75):** `mkdir a/b/c` and `touch a/b/c.txt` create the intermediate directories (`create_dir_all`); `sanitize_relpath` allows `/` (and `\` on Windows) but still rejects absolute paths, `..` traversal, NUL and reserved chars, so creation can't escape the target folder. | `finder_selection::create_file(name, content)`/`create_dir` (both `create_dir_all`) |
| `terminal` | Open a terminal at the frontmost Finder/Explorer folder — iTerm2/Terminal (macOS), Windows Terminal/PowerShell/cmd (Windows) | `finder_selection::open_terminal` |
| `bruno <€>` | German net-pay calculator | `bruno` |
| `timer <n>[s/min]` | Countdown timer (status toast on set) | `timer` |
| `alarm <HH:MM>` | Alarm at a clock time — next occurrence (`parseAlarmArg`); reuses the timer scheduler; status toast on set | `timer` |
| `md2pdf [path]` | Markdown → PDF — same as `Ctrl+Shift+M`. macOS: Finder selection or path. Windows: path → Edge headless. | `md_to_pdf_run` |
| `pwgen [N]` | Password generator (bare = default length, runnable so it outranks snippet matches) | `lib/pwgen.ts` |
| `calendar [month year]` (alias `cal`) | **Month-view calendar in the preview** (v0.84.234) — research which weekday a date was. Shows directly while typed (`sound` pattern); Enter hands the arrows to it: **←/→** month, **↑/↓** year, **PgUp/PgDn** month, **T**/Home today, Esc exits; click a day → full-date readout + distance from today ("in 142 days"). Arg jumps the view live: `calendar märz 1990` · `cal 3.2024` · `cal 2024-03` · bare year. Monday-start weeks + ISO week-number gutter; today = accent ring, neighbour-month days dimmed; directional slide on month change (reduced-motion-guarded). Pure, unit-tested date math in `lib/calendar.ts` (`monthMatrix`/`isoWeek`/`addMonths`/`parseCalendarArg` DE+EN month names/`dayDelta`); `CalendarPanel.tsx` renders. Frontend-only. | `lib/calendar.ts` + `CalendarPanel.tsx` |

`image_ops.rs` holds the resize/optim pipelines; `oxipng` is a workspace dep (pure-Rust, statically linked).

**Hidden triggers — exact word, NOT in `COMMANDS`** (never autocompleted; detection lives in `lib/commands.ts`): `getshaky` (Pong), `rockthebox`/`rockthabox` (Snake), `spacer` (Space Invaders), `learningtofly` (Flappy Bird), `opener` (German pickup line), `2fa`/`otp` (`is2faTrigger` → TOTP overlay), `otp <issuer>` / `2fa <issuer>` (`parseOtpQuery` → TOTP autocomplete rows), `bpm`/`bpms`/`bpmusic` (`isBpmTrigger`, Enter-activated → BPM detector). The app-launcher and Finder-selection rows are also implicit (no keyword).

### Doc text renders its inline Markdown (`lib/inline-md.ts`, `InlineMd.tsx`, v0.131.0)

The CommandDoc registry, `feature-extras` notes and the `CommandSpec` descriptions are written in light Markdown — **177 code spans + 7 bold** in the real field values — because the SAME strings also generate the README. The UI rendered them RAW, so the `?` panel showed literal `**Info**` and backticks (field report). `parseInline(text) -> InlineToken[]` (pure, 12 tests) + `<InlineMd text=…>` now turn them into real `<strong>`/`<code>` nodes — **never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`**.

**Exactly two constructs, and the omission is the load-bearing part.** Code spans win (CommonMark precedence) and their content is never re-parsed — that is what protects glob patterns like `src/**/*.ts` and any `**` inside backticks. Bold needs a non-empty, non-whitespace-flanked body **and** a body without `*`, so `a ** b` and glob text stay literal. `*italic*`/`_italic_` are deliberately NOT supported: a repo scan found a real caveat reading "PowerShell *function*" that a lone-star rule would have silently italicised (and `*` is everywhere in glob/CLI text). The scan also confirmed **0 unpaired `**` outside backticks**, which is why bold is safe to parse at all. **INVARIANT: `reserialize(parseInline(x)) === x`** — an unmatched marker stays literal, so no text can ever be swallowed; pinned for every degenerate shape.

**⚠️ The renderer sets NO colour.** These strings appear in four contexts — the preview, muted hint rows, the Features tab, and the SELECTED command row, which is white-on-rose. A fixed `--color-accent` chip would clash or vanish there, so the chip is `color-mix(… currentColor 14% …)` and bold only sets weight; a test pins the absence of `--color-accent|fg` in the chip class. Wired at **12 render sites**: CommandHelp (tagline · description · argument + flag descriptions · example result + note · tips · caveats · the index list), PreviewPanel (command hint + suggestion description), HistoryItem (hint · description · help-row tagline), FeaturesPanel (notes column). Deliberately NOT wired: `title=` attributes carrying **user** text (a clip's own note may legitimately contain backticks). Guard: a registry-wide test renders EVERY doc field and fails if a marker that had a valid pair in the source is still visible — mutation-verified (4 tests go red when the renderer falls back to raw text).

**Search matches the stripped text.** `searchDocs` and the Features-tab filter run over `stripInlineMarkup(...)`, because searching the raw value made a query spanning a marker ("info — live") miss a doc whose text reads `**Info** — live`.

### Destructive confirmations — `lib/confirm.ts` (v0.84.242)

**NEVER use `window.confirm` for a destructive action.** It is unreliable in the Tauri WKWebView in BOTH directions — the TOTP delete's confirm silently returned false (v0.84.200, "delete didn't work"), and the `clean` command's confirm **auto-passed without rendering** (v0.84.242 field report: "wird direkt gecleand"). Every destructive flow uses `confirmDialog(message, title)` from `lib/confirm.ts`: the dialog plugin's `ask()` (a real native alert rendered by Rust, independent of the webview), wrapped in `set_suppress_hide` so the popup survives the dialog's focus steal, **failing closed** on any plugin error. Requires the `dialog:allow-ask` capability (in all three platform `capabilities/default.json`). Current users: `kill`, `reboot`, `shutdown`, Notes "delete all", Snippets "restore defaults". Alternatively use an inline two-stage consent (TOTP delete's ✓/✗, the CleanPanel's double-Enter) — also fine; just never `window.confirm`.

### System commands (`system_commands.rs`, v0.19.0+)

Four system-level commands, also in the search-bar palette:

- **`kill [-9] [pattern | pid]`** — `system_commands::list_running_processes` (via the `sysinfo` crate, sorted by memory desc, **includes our own PID** so `kill inspector` can terminate IR — v0.100.1) drives a live picker rendered as `kill-target` `ListEntry` rows; App.tsx overrides the whole list in kill-mode. The arg filters by **name/exe tokens** (every whitespace word must appear, so `kill inspector rust` hits `InspectorRust`) *or*, when it's all digits, by **exact PID** (`kill 1234` surfaces that process, floated to the top, still shown with its name). Pure filter: `lib/kill-filter.ts`. `kill_process_by_pid(pid, force)` sends SIGTERM (or SIGKILL with `-9`) on macOS/Linux via `sysinfo`; on **Windows** it maps to `TerminateProcess` (no signals → forced terminate). Native confirm via `confirmDialog` before the kill.
- **`reboot` / `shutdown`** — macOS: `osascript` → `loginwindow` Apple Events (`aevtrrst` / `aevtrsdn`); Windows: `shutdown /r|/s /t 0`; **Linux (v0.84.0): `systemctl reboot` / `systemctl poweroff`** (logind, no sudo). No sudo. Native `window.confirm` first.
- **`lock`** — macOS: `pmset displaysleepnow`; Windows: `rundll32 user32.dll,LockWorkStation`; **Linux (v0.84.0): `loginctl lock-session`** with `xdg-screensaver` / GNOME / Cinnamon fallbacks (`system_commands::run_first_ok` tries each in order). No confirm (cheap to undo). IPC: `list_processes`, `kill_process`, `system_reboot`, `system_shutdown`, `system_lock`. (Windows paths runtime-unverified.)
- **`mute`** — toggles system output mute: macOS `osascript`, Windows multimedia VK key, **Linux (v0.84.0) `wpctl`/`pactl` set-mute toggle**; volume similarly via `wpctl`/`pactl` set-volume. IPC `toggle_mute` (`adjust_volume` is the related volume IPC).

### 2FA / TOTP manager (`totp_store.rs`, `totp_import.rs`, `crypto.rs`, v0.47.0)

RFC 6238 authenticator built into the popup. Two entry points: typing **`2fa`** *or* bare **`otp`** (`is2faTrigger` matches both, v0.84.197) replaces the popup body with `<TotpOverlay>` (List / Add / Import-Export tabs — manual token creation lives in the **Add** tab). **Direct add path (v0.104.0):** **`2fa add [issuer]`** / `otp add [issuer]` (`parse2faAdd`, checked BEFORE `parseOtpQuery` — otherwise `add` would be read as an issuer search; `2fa addepar` stays a search, the word boundary is the space) opens the overlay **straight on the Add form**, the optional argument pre-filling the Issuer field (focus then jumps to Account). Typing `2fa` surfaces `2fa add` as a visible sub-row (`totpSubEntry`, the snitch/shazam sub-command pattern; Enter on it dispatches directly — it's special-cased in the `command-suggestion` activate branch since `2fa` isn't a catalogue command) and the totp-manage **preview carries a subtle "＋ Add account" button** (`onTotpAdd` prop on `PreviewPanel`). The which-rows-for-which-query logic is the pure, unit-tested `lib/totp.ts::totpCommandRows` (bare trigger → manager + add sub-row; add form → the reverse; issuer searches → neither). `App.tsx`'s `totpInitial` state (`{tab, issuer}`) is set at each dispatch and consumed once at `TotpOverlay` mount via the `initialTab`/`initialIssuer` props (the overlay mounts fresh per open — no signal needed; `TotpOverlay.test.tsx` pins tab choice, pre-fill and the pre-fill→Account focus jump with mocked IPC). **While the overlay is open it pins the popup alive** (`setSuppressHide(true)` on mount → `false` on unmount, v0.84.199) so click-away / focus-loss can't dismiss it — **only Esc closes it** — which lets you **drag-and-drop an import file** in from Finder: a window-wide `onDragDropEvent` listener reads the dropped path via the `totp_import_file` IPC (Rust reads the file, no FS plugin) → same autodetecting importer; a "Drop to import" overlay shows while dragging. typing **`otp <issuer>`** or **`2fa <issuer>`** (v0.84.235 — `parseOtpQuery` accepts both keywords; it requires an argument, so bare `otp`/`2fa` fall through to the overlay) surfaces matching accounts as `totp` rows with the live 6-digit code — Enter copies it (e.g. `2fa hosti` → Hostinger's current token). The List tab refreshes every 1 s via `totp_current_codes_all` and draws a countdown ring.

- Storage is the `totp_entries` table; secrets are `crypto::encrypt`-ed and **never** returned over IPC — only generated codes (`TotpCode { code, seconds_remaining }`) cross the boundary.
- **Import** (`totp_import.rs`) autodetects format from the first bytes: `otpauth://totp/…` single URI, `otpauth-migration://offline?data=…` (Google Authenticator bulk protobuf), Aegis JSON, 2FAS JSON, the **OTPManager (macOS) "Tokens JSON"** export (a top-level JSON **array** of `{Issuer,Username,Secret,Digits,Period,Algorithm}` — `parse_otpmanager_json`, dispatched on a leading `[`; the `HMAC-` algorithm prefix is stripped; v0.84.197), or a plaintext file of one `otpauth://` per line. Per-line failures are recorded in `ImportSummary { added, failed }`, never aborting the batch.
- IPC: `totp_list`, `totp_add`, `totp_delete`, `totp_current_code`, `totp_current_codes_all`, `totp_import`, `totp_import_file`, `totp_export`, plus (v0.84.200) `totp_set_order` / `totp_update` / `totp_remove_duplicates` / `totp_delete_all`. Frontend types in `lib/totp.ts` (`matchTotpEntries` is the fuzzy issuer/account ranker).
- **Overlay management (v0.84.200):** the List tab has a **type-to-filter (v0.84.236)** — printable keys anywhere on the tab feed a fuzzy issuer/account filter (same `matchTotpEntries` ranker as `otp <issuer>`); the top match is accent-ringed and **Enter copies its current code + hides the popup** (immediately pasteable), Esc is two-stage (clears the filter, then exits), drag-reorder is disabled while filtering (a partial list has no meaningful persistent order; `popup-hidden` also resets `totpMode` so the hidden overlay never resurrects on the next open). It supports **pointer-drag reorder** (grip handle → `totp_set_order` persists a `sort_order` column; `list` orders by it. Pointer events, NOT HTML5 DnD, because Tauri's OS drag-drop for file import swallows element drags), **inline edit** (pencil → the Add tab pre-fills as an Edit form → `totp_update`; the secret is never exposed by the list so it's left blank = "keep current", a non-blank value replaces it), **reliable delete** (an **inline** ✓/✗ confirm — `window.confirm` is unreliable in the Tauri webview, which is why delete "didn't work"), and **cleanup** (`Remove duplicates` / `Clear all`). **Import dedup:** `totp_import` skips entries whose `(lower issuer, lower account, normalised secret)` `dedup_key` already exists (`existing_keys` decrypts once), so re-importing the same export no longer duplicates; the toast reports `added` + `skipped`.
- **Short-secret fix (v0.84.200):** `generate_at` uses `TOTP::new_unchecked` instead of `TOTP::new` — the latter rejects secrets < 128 bits, but many real, working secrets are shorter (80-bit/16-char base32 from OTPManager, Stripe, …; Google Authenticator generates codes for them). This was why "many imported entries showed no code".

### Markdown → PDF (`md_to_pdf.rs`, v0.46.0)

Standalone, **no external `mrxdown` CLI**. Triggered by **`Ctrl+Shift+M`** on the current Finder selection **or** the **`md2pdf [path]`** search-bar command (`md_to_pdf_run` IPC — bare = file-manager selection, or an explicit path). Pipeline: `pulldown-cmark` (CommonMark + GFM) → HTML with embedded GitHub CSS (`render_html`, shared) → platform backend. **macOS:** WKWebView `createPDF` (main-thread-only; `md_to_pdf_run` bounces convert onto the main thread via a oneshot channel from its worker). **Windows (v0.55.0):** `windows_edge::render_html_to_pdf` writes the HTML to a temp file and runs `msedge --headless=new --print-to-pdf` (pure process-spawn, no COM/WebView2 SDK — compiles cross-platform; runtime needs verification on a real Windows box). **Linux:** still no backend (`backend_unavailable`). Output PDF lands sibling to source (`foo.md` → `foo.pdf`). `ConvertSummary { converted, skipped, failed, backend_unavailable }`. **Note:** the `md2pdf` *selection* path + `Ctrl+Shift+M` are macOS-only so far (Windows Explorer-selection reading is TODO); on Windows use `md2pdf <path>`.

### Monitor brightness (`brightness.rs`, v0.62.0; rewritten v0.72.0; EDR v0.84.185)

**EDR brightness extension (`edr.rs`, macOS, v0.84.185 — phase 1 of 2).** On EDR-capable displays (14"/16" MBP XDR, Pro Display XDR) the *same* brightness slider runs **past 100 %** toward the EDR headroom (Vivid-style), driving the display above the SDR max; on Windows/Linux + non-EDR macOS the slider behaves exactly as before (ends at 100). **Phase 1 (done):** runtime capability detection — `edr::display_headroom(cg_id)` reads the matching `NSScreen`'s `maximumPotentialExtendedDynamicRangeColorComponentValue` (raw `objc2 0.6` msg_send, matching the rest of the codebase — the typed `objc2-app-kit` is only 0.2.2 in the lockfile = objc2 0.5, incompatible); `edr::edr_max_percent(headroom)` (pure, unit-tested) maps it to `MonitorInfo.edr_max` (100, or >100 capped at 300). The frontend slider's upper bound = `edr_max`; one value drives both paths — `BrightnessPanel.push` sends gamma `min(v,100)` via `set_monitor_brightness` AND the EDR boost `v>100?v:0` via the new `set_edr_level` IPC. Bar shows an amber EDR segment + an SDR-max marker above 100; a heat/battery/colour warning shows while in the EDR range. **Phase 2 (`edr::macos`, v0.84.186–.194 — the overlay, CONFIRMED WORKING on M1 Pro XDR):** above 100 % `set_level(app, cg_id, percent)` builds a borderless, transparent, click-through (`ignoresMouseEvents`), level-1000, all-Spaces NSWindow covering the target `NSScreen`, hosting a **`CAMetalLayer`** (`wantsExtendedDynamicRangeContent`, `RGBA16Float`, `extendedLinearDisplayP3` colorspace). Each frame a Metal clear fills the drawable with a uniform value `factor = 1 + above·MULT_GAIN` (`above` = 0..2 for 100..300 %, `MULT_GAIN=3.0` → up to 7×). **The brightening mechanism is the layer's `compositingFilter`, set to the blend-mode *name string* `"multiplyBlendMode"`** (the BrightXDR/Vivid technique). That blend, in the extended-linear RGBA16Float layer, **preserves EDR values > 1**, so the overlay multiplies the desktop behind it by `factor`: **black × f = black (no wash), bright × f → EDR**. Hard-won gotchas: (1) **NOT `CIMultiplyCompositing`** — that *compositing op* clamps to SDR (could only darken → "no brightening"); the blend-mode *string* keeps EDR. (2) **All Metal/CA drawing MUST be on the main thread** — drawing a `CAMetalLayer` from a background thread raises an Obj-C exception that unwinds through tao's run-loop observer and `SIGABRT`s; so a worker thread only *ticks* (~10 fps, `TICK_MS`) and dispatches each `draw_edr_frame` to the main thread via `run_on_main_thread`. The draw is wrapped in `objc2::exception::catch` (on a raise: set `RENDER_FAILED`, log the NSException reason, stop the ticker — never aborts) — that's how the bogus `-[CAMetalLayer maximumExtendedDynamicRangeColorComponentValue]` read (that selector is on **NSScreen**, not the layer) was caught + removed. (3) window/layer/queue are resolved on the main thread in `build_window`; the render thread only gets the layer + queue pointers (never NSWindow/NSView). Diagnostics confirmed the OS grants headroom: the screen's *current* `maximumExtendedDynamicRangeColorComponentValue` rose 1.0 → ~9.5 once EDR content was on screen (potential 16). Below SDR-max / 0 it tears down (stop thread → join → close window on main). All raw `objc2 0.6` msg_send (Metal linked via `#[link(name="Metal")]`, extended-linear colorspace via `CGColorSpaceCreateWithName`). **Hardware safety:** EDR brightness uses the same path as HDR video (≤1000 nits sustained / 1600 peak, within spec); macOS thermal-throttles the headroom automatically + mini-LED has no burn-in — cost is battery + heat only. The additive-veil path (`VEIL_*` consts, used when `MULTIPLY_OK=false`) stays as a fallback if the blend string is ever rejected. **Still TODO:** sleep/wake headroom-drop rebuild, `NSApplicationDidChangeScreenParameters` re-detect, app-quit teardown (the OS currently closes the window on quit).

`brightness` (alias `bri`) opens a slider overlay with one slider per monitor + an "all" master — Lunar / TwinkleTray style. The control **mechanism is chosen per platform**, because pure DDC/CI turned out not to work on the maintainer's Apple-Silicon Mac (an external monitor through a DP/HDMI adapter returned `invalid DDC/CI length` for every VCP read, and `IOAVServiceWriteI2C` writes silently no-op'd through the adapter — verified live):

- **macOS → software (gamma-table) dimming.** `CGSetDisplayTransferByFormula` (raw CoreGraphics FFI, `#[link(name="CoreGraphics", kind="framework")]`, same pattern as `input_lock.rs`) scales each display's output transfer function. Works on **every** display — the built-in Liquid Retina panel *and* external/adapter-connected monitors — with no DDC, no extra TCC permission, no Apple-Silicon I²C quirks. This is the "Software Dimming" Lunar/MonitorControl fall back to. Displays are enumerated via `CGGetActiveDisplayList` + `CGDisplayIsBuiltin`. **Confirmed working on Apple Silicon (M-series) with an external adapter-connected monitor.**
- **Windows → software (gamma-ramp) dimming.** `SetDeviceGammaRamp` (`windows` 0.61 bindings, `Win32::UI::ColorSystem`) per monitor enumerated via `EnumDisplayMonitors`/`GetMonitorInfoW`. Mirrors the macOS path; covers built-in + external uniformly. **Windows runtime-unverified** — written compile-clean (validated via an isolated `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu`) against the same GDI bindings `screen_picker.rs` uses.
- **Linux → DDC/CI VCP feature `0x10`** via `ddc-hi` (wraps `ddc-i2c`) — real hardware backlight for external monitors.

Pure, unit-tested mappings: `percent_to_raw`/`raw_to_percent` (DDC, scaled to each device's `maximum()`), `percent_to_gamma_fraction` (macOS/Windows; clamps to a `MIN_PERCENT=5` floor so the screen can never dim to unrecoverable black), and `gamma_ramp_entry` (Windows 16-bit ramp entry; `index*257*fraction`). The applied level is tracked **in-process** per display (gamma has no "read current backlight"); it resets to 100% at logout. **Persisted levels (v0.87.5):** the full slider value (EDR boosts > 100 included) is stored per stable display key (macOS: CG display UUID via `CGDisplayCreateUUIDFromDisplayID`; Windows: index) in the settings map `brightness.levels` (pure `parse_levels`/`serialize_levels`, unit-tested; 100 % removes the entry). `set_monitor_brightness`/`set_edr_level` IPCs call `persist_current` after each change; `brightness::restore_saved(app, db)` (lib.rs setup, worker + 1.5 s settle) re-applies gamma + EDR at startup — without it every restart silently reset all displays to 100 % (gamma dies with the process). The macOS cache `Entry` carries an `edr` shadow so the effective level is known without asking the overlay. Re-enumeration preserves the current dim level. Caches are platform-specific (`Vec<Entry>` for the gamma paths, `Mutex<Option<DisplayCache>>` for DDC). `supports_ddc` now means "controllable" (always `true` on the gamma paths; on Linux a non-answering monitor is `false` and hidden). IPC: `list_brightness_monitors`, `get_monitor_brightness`, `set_monitor_brightness`. **Caveat:** software dimming reduces emitted light, not backlight — it can only go *darker* than native, never brighter.

**Frontend — inline in the preview column (v0.72.1).** `brightness` does **not** open a separate window. Pressing Enter on the `brightness` command row sets `App.tsx`'s `brightnessMode` + `brightnessFocus`, which renders `BrightnessPanel.tsx` in the **right preview column** (replacing `PreviewPanel`) and gives the arrow keys to the sliders: **↑/↓** select a monitor, **←/→** adjust ±5 (debounced ~80 ms, floor 5%), **Enter** hands the arrows back to the left list (`onUnfocus`, re-focuses the search field), **Esc** leaves brightness mode (`onExit`). `useKeyboardNav` is disabled while `brightnessFocus` is true so the list nav doesn't double-fire (same `enabled` gate as games/bpm/totp). `brightnessMode` auto-exits when the query is no longer the `brightness` command. (The old separate `brightness-overlay` window + `BrightnessOverlay.tsx` / `brightness_open`/`_close` IPC are retired but left inert in the tree — its webview didn't reliably load the monitor list, which is why the redesign moved control inline.)

### boom — system-wide audio EQ (`boom/`, `boom-driver/`, v0.84.143–.157 macOS; v0.84.220 Windows via Equalizer APO)

`boom` opens an inline **audio-enhancement controller** in the right preview column (same inline family as `stats`/`hue`; `boomMode`/`boomFocus` in `App.tsx`, `CommandSpec.platform=["mac","win"]` since v0.84.220). Working system-wide EQ + presets + volume boost. **macOS needs a one-time virtual-driver install** (NOT driverless — see the history note; that's a real macOS limitation, not a choice); **Windows drives Equalizer APO** instead (see the Windows-backend note below — no own driver, runtime-unverified).

- **DSP engine (`boom/mod.rs`, pure + unit-tested):** the realtime signal chain — **pre-amp → 10-band graphic EQ (RBJ peaking biquads, transposed DF-II) → volume boost → soft-knee limiter**. `Biquad` (0 dB = exact passthrough), `DspChain::process_interleaved` (allocation-free hot path), `soft_limit` (transparent below **0.95**·ceiling — v0.84.207: the original 0.8 knee sat at −2 dBFS and permanently tanh-compressed the top 2 dB of every music peak, which made flat boom audibly duller than bypass; now only genuine overs are shaped, ceiling = 1.0 — never clips/blows speakers), 20 presets (15 genre + 5 device-correction), `BoomConfig` (persisted as JSON under settings `boom.config`; clamped by `normalize`). 11 unit tests (biquad boost/cut, limiter ceiling, flat-chain passthrough, boost-then-limit, preset/normalize, version gate). IPC: `boom_available` · `boom_presets` · `get_/set_boom_config`.
- **The virtual driver (`boom-driver/`, v0.84.155):** an `AudioServerPlugIn` — **BlackHole** (MIT, vendored verbatim as `BlackHole.c`) rebranded to "boom Audio" **only via `-D` compile defines** in `build.sh` (own device UID `BoomAudio_UID`, bundle id `io.celox.boom.driver`, fresh CFPlugIn factory UUID) so it never collides with an installed BlackHole. `build.sh` → a universal, ad-hoc-signed `.driver` bundle; `scripts/boom-driver-install.sh` copies it to `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/` + restarts `coreaudiod` (admin; `--remove` uninstalls). **Distribution needs Developer-ID signing + notarization** (ad-hoc only loads locally).
- **The bridge (`boom/macos.rs`, v0.84.156–.157):** enabling boom routes the **system default output** to "boom Audio" (`find_device_by_uid("BoomAudio_UID")` + `set_default_output`) so apps render into it; a **capture IOProc** on boom Audio pushes its loopback input into a lock-free SPSC **`Ring`** buffer; a **playback IOProc** on the real device pops it, runs `DspChain` in place, and outputs. No tap, no muting, no doubling. The capture side zeroes boom Audio's own output (no feedback). **Perceptual system-volume taper (v0.84.213):** while boom is active the system volume controls boom Audio, whose stock BlackHole curve is **linear over −64..0 dB** (40 % = −38 dB ≈ inaudible, 20 % = −51 dB, huge steps up top — the "volume scaling feels wrong" bug). The driver now **publishes the volume control but doesn't apply it** (`-DkEnableVolumeControl=false` in `build.sh` — still zero source edits); the playback bridge applies `boom::volume_gain(scalar)` = **scalar^1.5** (50 % ≈ −9 dB, 25 % ≈ −18 dB, 10 % ≈ −30 dB; was scalar² until v0.84.268 — that squeezed all audibility into the top slider half on quiet HDMI-monitor speakers, 25 % = −24 dB ≈ inaudible) post-DSP — RT-safe via `VOLUME_GAIN_BITS` atomic, kept fresh by a `kAudioDevicePropertyVolumeScalar` listener on boom Audio (`add_volume_listener` at start; keys/HUD/our slider all write that scalar). Driver-side **mute still applies in the driver** (separate branch). Requires the rebuilt driver (reinstall via panel / `scripts/boom-driver-install.sh`). **boom Audio's sample rate is matched to the real device** at start (`set_device_sample_rate`) — else apps render at 48k while we play 44.1k → slow playback + ring overruns (clicks); a ~60 ms silence cushion (30 ms until v0.84.238) is pre-filled for startup, drift slack and brief capture stalls during device reconfigurations (webview mic open). **`start_locked` re-applies the saved config params to the fresh chain it swaps in (v0.84.207)** — `Engine.params` stores the last push; without it enabling boom ran a **default (flat) chain until the next slider touch** (the "settings only apply after moving a slider" bug). IOProc closures are realtime-safe (lock-free ring; `DspChain` params via `try_lock`). Default output is saved + restored on disable / app-quit (`RunEvent::Exit`). **Last-output memory (v0.87.5):** every bridge (re)start persists the real target device's UID (`boom.last_output_uid`, via APP_HANDLE→DbHandle); when a start finds the default stale-stuck on boom Audio (unclean exit — the install script's `killall` skips `RunEvent::Exit`), it re-targets the REMEMBERED device first (`find_device_by_uid`, if present with output) and only then falls back to `pick_real_output`'s built-in preference — fixes "restart lands on the MacBook speakers instead of the BT box". A device-arrival listener (auto re-bridge when the BT box connects later) is deliberately not implemented. **Mute transparency (v0.112.1):** with boom active, keys/HUD/gestures write boom Audio's mute — which the driver applies and which used to PERSIST invisibly (no HUD) across boom-enable and device switches; three field incidents of "boom is on and I hear nothing" were exactly this stale mute (never a broken bridge). `start_locked` now **clears a set mute on BOTH boom Audio and the real device** on every bridge (re)start (enable, crash recovery, output-changed re-bridge — one call site since they all run through `start_locked`): right after enabling boom or switching outputs, silence is never the desired state. ⚠️ v0.112.0 first shipped the prettier rule (ADOPT the real device's mute, macOS per-device semantics) and it healed nothing — the live repro kept the mute, and the `probe_device_mutes` ignored test (kept in `boom/macos.rs` as a diagnostic) revealed why: **real devices carry stale master-mute reads that don't even reflect audibility** (the MacBook speakers reported `mute=true` while audibly playing — likely master vs. per-channel divergence), so the adoption saw "both muted = matching" and skipped the write. Don't re-derive intent from real-device mute reads. The idle-gate resume deliberately does NOT clear (same device, no user-visible transition — a deliberate mid-session mute survives). Pure rule `boom::should_clear_stale_mute` (no write on unmuted/control-less devices), unit-tested. `BoomLevels` gained `muted` (ORed over `LEVELS_BOOM_DEV` + `LEVELS_REAL_DEV` in `macos::levels()`, set/cleared with the session) and the BoomPanel shows an amber "Output is muted — that's why it's silent" banner whose Unmute button calls the `boom_unmute` IPC (`boom::unmute()` → clears both devices — plain `toggle_mute` would only reach the default). Diagnose-Griff: `osascript -e 'get volume settings'` → `output muted`. `boom::apply(db)` (after `set_boom_config` + at startup) syncs the engine. **Idle gate (v0.84.240, battery):** a running IOProc makes coreaudiod hold a `PreventUserIdleSystemSleep` assertion for the owning process — with boom enabled the Mac could **never idle-sleep** (measured: a full night awake with the display off, 94 % → 41 %). After **60 s of true silence** (per-buffer peak vs `GATE_SILENCE_EPS` in the capture IOProc — BlackHole delivers exact zeros when nothing renders; pure decision helpers `gate_is_audible`/`gate_should_suspend` are unit-tested) a `boom-idle-gate` thread **stops both IOProcs** (procs stay registered → assertions released) — but only if, with our procs stopped, **no other client** runs on boom Audio (`kAudioDevicePropertyDeviceIsRunningSomewhere` probe, polled ≤2.4 s; otherwise abort + keep running, audio must never be lost). The **wake listener is armed BEFORE the probe** so a client starting in the gap is never missed; any app starting playback fires it → resume in ms (`Ring::reset_to_silence` re-primes exactly the startup cushion, so latency never creeps across cycles; a failed resume falls back to a full re-bridge). The **webview's warm AudioContext is itself a client on boom Audio** and is parked/woken alongside via `warm-audio-suspend`/`warm-audio-resume` events (`boom::set_app_handle` from lib.rs setup; `warm-audio.ts::suspendWarmIfIdle` refuses while a mic is live — bpm/disco running → probe fails → bridge stays up, correct). Disabling boom also emits `warm-audio-suspend` (a lone running silent output unit would otherwise hold an assertion on the speakers), and **the launch pre-warm is gated on boom being enabled** (`App.tsx` checks `getBoomConfig` — without boom the context stays lazy, like pre-v0.84.238).
- **Frontend:** `BoomPanel.tsx` (on/off, preset dropdown, pre-amp, 10 vertical EQ sliders, boost slider with 100 % tick + over-100 % warning, enhancement-effect sliders, **live input/output level meters + clip indicator** polling `boom_levels` ~8 Hz while running). **While boom is OFF the whole settings surface (preset/pre-amp/EQ/boost/effects/meters) is hidden** — only header, toggle, driver status and an "audio passes through untouched" hint show (the engine is stopped / EqAPO renders passthrough, so the controls would do nothing). **The on/off control is a state-first switch (v0.84.232)** — knob + colour + an explicit "Active"/"Off" word and a state-aware status line; the old "On"-labelled pill read as an *action* ("press to turn on"), so users couldn't tell whether boom was currently active. **In-app driver install/uninstall (v0.84.159):** the `.driver` is bundled into the app's `Contents/Resources/` and installed from the panel via `boom_install_driver` (admin osascript copy to the HAL dir + `coreaudiod` restart, reading the bundled bundle from `resource_dir`); `boom_driver_installed` gates the UI (an "Install driver" card when missing), `boom_uninstall_driver` removes it. **The driver is NOT bundled via tauri.conf `bundle.resources` (v0.84.176):** that path (`boom-driver/build/…`) doesn't exist on a fresh/CI checkout, and `tauri-build` validates resource paths *at compile time*, which broke `cargo build --workspace` on CI (Linux). Instead `install-macos.sh` builds the driver (`boom-driver/build.sh`) then **copies it into `Contents/Resources/boom-driver.driver` after the tauri build but *before* signing** (so `codesign --deep` seals it).
- **History (why not driverless):** the first attempt used Core-Audio **process taps** (macOS 14.2+). Taps are a *capture* API — re-outputting processed audio to the **same** device is impossible: muting the source to avoid doubling silences the shared device output (verified across unmuted / muted / mutedWhenTapped / aggregate-as-default). All real system EQ (eqMac, Boom, SoundSource) uses a virtual driver. The dead tap code (`make_tap_description`/`build_aggregate`, `MUTE_BEHAVIOR`/`DIAG_*`) is retained for reference under `#![allow(dead_code)]`. Full write-up: `docs/boom-driver-plan.md`.
- **Enhancement effects (`boom/mod.rs`, v0.84.184):** the 5 panel sliders are now real DSP, applied per-frame after the 10-band EQ: **Bass** (low-shelf @ 90 Hz, ≤ +9 dB), **Clarity** (presence peak @ 3 kHz, ≤ +6 dB), **Fidelity** ("air" high-shelf @ 9 kHz, ≤ +6 dB), **Ambience** (mid/side stereo widen, up to 1.8× side), **Night** (linked-stereo downward compressor, up to 5:1 + makeup — levels loud/quiet for low-volume listening). Each is intensity 0..1, **0 = exact passthrough**; the limiter runs last. `Biquad` gained `set_lowshelf`/`set_highshelf`; `DspChain` holds the per-channel shelf/peak biquads + a `Compressor` (precomputed coeffs, only the envelope is state). 6 unit tests (each effect lifts its band / widens / compresses; off = passthrough).
- **Windows backend (`boom/windows.rs`, v0.84.220, runtime-unverified):** no virtual driver (Windows audio drivers are kernel-mode + WHQL-signed) — boom drives **Equalizer APO** instead: the pure, unit-tested `boom::apo_config_text(cfg)` renders the saved config as an EqAPO include file mirroring the DspChain stage order (pre-amp → 10-band `PK` filters Q 1.41 → bass `LS` 90 Hz / clarity `PK` 3 kHz / fidelity `HS` 9 kHz → ambience as a simultaneous `Copy:` stereo-widen matrix); the volume boost folds into the `Preamp:` line (20·log10) and `controlled_boost` subtracts the largest positive gain as clipping headroom (EqAPO has no limiter). `windows::apply_config` writes `<EqAPO config dir>\inspector-rust-boom.txt` (dir from registry `HKLM\SOFTWARE\EqualizerAPO@ConfigPath`, `%ProgramFiles%` fallback) + idempotently appends the `Include:` line to `config.txt` (`apo_ensure_include`, pure); EqAPO hot-reloads on write, its installer grants Users write access (no elevation). Disabling rewrites the include as a passthrough comment. Night (compressor) has no EqAPO equivalent → hidden in the Windows panel; level meters too. `boom_driver_installed` on Windows = EqAPO present; the panel shows a "Get Equalizer APO" card (download link + re-check) when missing. `CommandSpec.platform = ["mac","win"]`.
- **TODO:** Developer-ID signing + notarization for distribution, output-device-change + sample-rate-change rebuild, residual clock-drift correction (rare click over long sessions). Phase 2: 31-band EQ, device auto-detect.

### iris — mic-triggered screen vignette (`iris.rs`, `IrisOverlay.tsx`, `IrisPanel.tsx`, v0.102.0)

`iris [dB]` watches the default microphone in the background and lights a **red vignette along the edges of every screen** while the level exceeds a threshold — a peripheral "you are being loud" signal. Overlays are **click-through** (`set_ignore_cursor_events(true)`), never focused, and draw **no text**, so the machine stays usable. Ported from the raspi5 dB-analysis page's `body.over-iris` wash (disco-controller `public/stats.html`) — same palette, same 1.8 s breathe, same 0.55 s release fade on `cubic-bezier(.2,0,0,1)`.

- **Threshold unit is SPL on the disco-controller convention** (`spl = dBFS + SPL_OFFSET`, offset **90**), so a number that works on raspi5 means the same here; default **55** = the raspi5 `warn_thr`, band 30–100.
- **Two deliberate departures from the reference.** (1) **Edge-anchored, not full-page** — the reference floods the very content you are working on. (2) **The breathe actually breathes**: `stats.html` animates `iris-breathe 1.8s`, but **`@keyframes iris-breathe` is not defined anywhere in that repo**, so on the web page that layer is silently static — the real pulse only ever existed on the LED strip in Python (`hue_warn.py`: `BRI_TROUGH 90` → `BRI_PEAK 254` over `BREATHE_S 1.8`, alternating). `IrisOverlay.tsx` takes its opacity range (0.354 → 1.0) from that working implementation.
- **Two layers: a muted field + the bursts (v0.102.1).** The field is **8 light blobs** from the pure, unit-tested `lib/iris.ts::makeBlobs(rand)` — anchored at the 4 corners + 4 edge midpoints but **jittered ±14 % off**, each with its own size, palette, drift vector, period and blur; periods are **mutually incommensurate** (`5.1 + i*0.83 + rand*1.7` s) so the composite motion has no visible loop. `mix-blend-mode: screen` in an `isolation: isolate` root lets overlaps brighten into moving hot spots. `rand` is injected so the geometry is testable, and since every monitor is its own window no two screens (or arms) look alike. **`makeBlobs` is unit-tested for the load-bearing invariant that no blob ever reaches the middle of the screen.** Since v0.102.1 the field is deliberately **muted** — it is only the constant "you are over" backdrop.
- **⚠️ The punch is the BURSTS, and the first port missed them entirely (v0.102.1).** The raspi5 page's character does **not** come from its background wash — that layer is static there (`iris-breathe` is animated but its keyframes are *never defined* in that repo). It comes from `spawnIrisWord()`: discrete impulses on a randomised gap, each snapping in hard, holding, then fading while growing. `iris-burst-pop` ports that curve verbatim — `0% → 18% → 62% → 100%`, scale `.66 → 1.05 → 1 → 1.16`, same `cubic-bezier(.2,0,0,1)` — so the attack lands at 18 % of ~1.5 s ≈ **270 ms**. That timing *is* the impulsiveness; a slow bloom reads as ambient no matter how vivid it is. Palette is the reference's `IRIS_TINTS` (warm-graded red → salmon → amber → near-white — why it never looks like one flat red). No lettering: each impulse is a rotated, blurred light cloud.
- **Burst cadence is level-reactive — a deliberate step past the reference.** The reference fires blind at 1.7–4.8 s for as long as the flag is set. `burstGapMs(intensity, rand)` interpolates that toward **0.42–1.2 s**, and `makeBurst` grows the impulse brighter/bigger/shorter, as `burstIntensity(spl, threshold)` rises over a 25 dB span — a loud room reads as urgent rather than merely busy. The overlay gets `intensity` from the ~10 Hz `iris-level` stream via a **ref**, so the ~10 events/s never re-render or re-subscribe the scheduler. Both helpers are unit-tested, including that the gap window **never collapses to a fixed beat at any volume** (the reference's own comment: "a fixed beat reads mechanical") and that a burst's peak opacity stays translucent (≤ 0.78) so it can never black out the screen.
- **Concurrency is capped on the live array, never on a counter.** Straight from the reference's hard-won comment: a counter only decremented on `animationend` drifts the moment one of those events fails to arrive (a throttled window suffices), and once it drifts past the cap it blocks spawning **forever, silently**. Removal is `animationend` **plus** a `life + 600 ms` timeout as a second path. Since v0.102.3 bursts stay in the edge band like the field — the centre-clear guarantee holds for both layers.
- **Arm/disarm is one pair of states, two different-feeling transitions.** While dark each blob sits pushed *outward* past its anchor (`ox`/`oy`, computed away from centre); lighting up pulls them **inward** to rest on a spring overshoot (`cubic-bezier(.16,1.24,.3,1)`), staggered **corner-first** (`--i * 46ms`), under one short `iris-flash` beat that is keyed on a rise counter so it replays on every edge. Going dark plays the same geometry in reverse, slower (`RELEASE_S * 1.35`) and staggered **edge-first** (`(7 - --i) * 42ms`) — the corners are the last lights to die. The enter/exit transform lives on the outer element and the endless drift on an inner one, so the two never fight over `transform`.
- **⚠️ The calibration panel must NOT take keyboard focus (v0.102.2).** Arming used to set an `irisFocus` flag, which is in the `useKeyboardNav` `enabled` gate — so the list navigation was disabled while the panel was open and **the second Enter never reached `dispatchCommand`**: `iris 55` followed by `iris` left it armed, and the toggle looked dead. `iris` is the one panel command whose Enter must keep working *while the panel is open*, because Enter is the toggle. So the panel is a focus-free readout, the threshold is edited in the search bar instead (`iris 60`, debounced `iris_set_threshold`, the `weather` "argument drives it" model), and the toggle resolves against the **live backend** (`iris_status`) via the pure, unit-tested `lib/iris.ts::irisAction(arg, active)` rather than React state — a popup reopened long after arming would otherwise flip the wrong way. `irisAction`'s tests replay the exact reported sequence.
- **The impulses are soft multi-lobed flares in the edge band (v0.102.3; reworked from the v0.102.2 clip-path shards, which were too harsh).** Each burst is 1–3 overlapping radial gradients from the pure `makeBurstLobes(rand, streak)` — white-hot core falling through the tint into transparency, so the outline is irregular but every edge is a falloff; blur is only feathering (0.8–5.5 px). Positions come from `edgePosition(rand)`: uniform along the perimeter, **quadratically rim-biased**, never deeper than `BURST_EDGE_DEPTH` = 24 % — so the CENTRE of the screen is genuinely clear for BOTH layers now (unit-tested: the 26–74 box is unreachable; plus a statistical rim-clustering test). ~36 % are thin streaks (`aspect` 0.08–0.2, life ≤ 0.45 s); flares live ≤ 1.0 s. Since v0.102.5 the envelope is a **full-life strobe train**: no hold at all — the impulse flickers between peak and near-black (3–15 % of peak) for its whole life, one last half-strength flare at 74 %, then dies while growing; each spawn tick fires a **volley** of 1–4 (pure `volleySize(intensity, rand)`, louder = more) and, on a loudness-scaled share of volleys (`volleyFlash`, capped — a constant flash is not a flash), the **full-rim `iris-flash`** replays with it; `BURST_MAX` = 7. Peak cap 0.98 — never 1.0, that line separates "strobe" from "screen takeover" and its test says so. Rotation drift (`rotDrift` ≤ ±14°) runs throughout; the reference's `cubic-bezier(0.2,0,0,1)` easing stays.
- **Beat-matched since v0.103.0 (`beat.rs`).** A `BeatDetector` runs inside the iris capture callback — a faithful Rust port of the `lib/bpm.ts` `BpmAnalyzer` core (same constants: kick band HP 30/LP 100 Q 1.5, 1024-sample hops, moving-average baseline ×1.4, **SuperFlux lagged-peak gate** LAG 4/WIN 4/MARGIN 1.04 against ghost onsets from kick decay/room echo, **3 s calibration gate** against the startup false-onset burst, 300 ms refractory, IOI-median BPM folded into [60,200] + consistency confidence). Two deliberate differences: **time comes from the sample count** (deterministic under test, immune to callback jitter), and the display-smoothing layer (octave snap, display mean, stale reset) is omitted — iris needs onsets to flash on, not a UI-stable readout. Each onset emits ONE `iris-beat` event `{strength, bpm, confidence}` from Rust; the overlays fire a volley on it (`beatVolley` — at least a pair, a beat must land harder than chance; `beatFlash` follows the KICK's salience, not the room level) and the random cadence degrades to a watchdog via `beatDriven` (2.5 s `BEAT_HOLD_MS`). One detector, one event → **all monitors flash on the same beat**. The biquad is a minimal local RBJ implementation, deliberately NOT reusing `boom::Biquad` (which exposes shelves/peaking for the EQ — a coupling between system-audio EQ and beat detection would be strange for ~30 lines of math). ⚠️ Test-fixture gotcha, learned here: hash-noise via `i.wrapping_mul(K)` must cast to **u32** first — on `usize` it never wraps and the "noise" becomes a huge in-band ramp that fires onsets between the kicks (cost a debugging round).
- **⚠️ macOS: the overlays MUST set `setCanHide:NO` (`show_overlay_window`).** `hotkey::hide_popup` ends in `app.hide()` (`NSApp.hide(nil)`) — that is what hands key focus back to the app you were working in, and it hides **every** window this process owns. Without the flag the vignette vanished the instant the popup closed, i.e. exactly when a background monitor has to be visible (the v0.102.0 field report "auch sichtbar wenn overlay geschlossen"). Show with **`orderFrontRegardless`, never Tauri `show()`** (= `makeKeyAndOrderFront:`), or every arm steals key from the app underneath — same rule as the snap overlay.
- **The decision lives in Rust.** `iris-over` is emitted **on the edge only**, mirroring the raspi5 rule that the server's `warn_over` flag is the single source of truth and the client never re-derives it from a displayed level; the overlay is a dumb renderer. A separate `iris-level` event (throttled ~10 Hz) feeds the calibration meter.
- **Anti-flap, which the reference does not have.** raspi5 is purely level-triggered (`round(spl,1) > warn_thr`) — fine for a 1 Hz server log, but it strobes at the boundary on a live feed sampled ~25×/s. `decide_over(spl, thr, currently_over, held_ms)` (pure, unit-tested) adds `HYSTERESIS_SPL = 2.0` on release plus `MIN_HOLD_MS = 400`, so a single transient reads as a visible pulse and a level parked on the threshold lights **once** (`a_sustained_loud_signal_stays_lit_across_many_frames` pins exactly that).
- **Capture** is `mic_capture::start_level` — a second `Sink` variant added alongside the existing PCM one, so the cpal device/format handling is shared rather than duplicated. It hands back an RMS per ~40 ms chunk; shipping base64 PCM to a webview 25×/s just to square it would be waste, and the threshold decision belongs in one place anyway.
- **Overlay windows stay visible and fully transparent while idle** rather than being shown/hidden per event: a transparent click-through window is invisible regardless, and window-ordering churn twice a second is both janky and a focus hazard. Only CSS opacity animates. One window per monitor (`iris-overlay-<n>`), positioned in **physical** px (a HiDPI monitor gets a half-covered overlay from logical `inner_size` alone), `set_visible_on_all_workspaces(true)` so it follows across Spaces.
- **Toggle semantics** (`lib/iris.ts::parseIrisArg`, pure + tested): bare `iris` toggles, `iris 0` (or any non-positive value) is off — 0 is free to repurpose because a 0 SPL threshold would mean "always lit" — and a positive number arms or retunes. Unparseable input falls back to *toggle*, never to arming at a wrong level. A comma decimal (`iris 57,5`) is accepted like the `bruno` parser.
- **`iris` is in `PANEL_KINDS` with `keepArg`, and the number-form LIVE-ARMS while typing (v0.102.4).** Typing `iris 50` starts monitoring after a ~350 ms debounce — no Enter — and opens `IrisPanel` (live SPL meter + threshold marker); editing the number retunes the running session directly (`iris_set_threshold`), marker included. This supersedes the earlier "typing must never open the microphone" rule for the number-carrying form, on explicit user request. **Since v0.102.6 fully live in BOTH directions:** a positive number arms/retunes, and editing down to a bare `iris` (or `iris 0`) live-DISARMS a running session — quietly (no toast, no popup-hide; the user is mid-typing). Only garbage does nothing. The 350 ms debounce is what keeps transient edit states (`iris 5`, an empty arg while retyping) from bouncing through a disarm; after a live-disarm the row label flips to „scharfschalten“, so Enter re-arming is announced. Enter still resolves `irisAction` against the live `iris_status`. Typing `iris` while a background session runs shows the meter (no mic side-effect — the capture is already running). **Esc leaves the panel without disarming**; `popup-hidden` resets `irisMode` but never calls `iris_stop`.
- **Never auto-starts after an app restart** — opening the mic silently at login would be a nasty surprise. Only the threshold persists (`iris.threshold_spl`).
- IPC: `iris_start` · `iris_stop` · `iris_status` · `iris_set_threshold` (all **async** — `iris_start` builds windows, and building a webview window from a *sync* command blocks the main thread and deadlocks, the same trap documented for the recorder's stop bar). `iris-overlay-*` is in each platform's `capabilities/default.json` and routed by label prefix in `main.tsx`.

### alias — guided shell-alias builder (`shell_alias.rs`, `AliasPanel.tsx`, `lib/alias.ts`, v0.127.0)

`alias [name]` shows a guided alias builder in the preview (show-while-typing, `sound` pattern — pure UI until a button is pressed; Enter hands it focus, the arg pre-fills the name). Two inputs (command + alias name) → one card per OS with the exact create one-liner: macOS/zsh + Linux/bash use `printf '%s\n' "<alias line>" >> ~/.zshrc|.bashrc && source …`, Windows a PowerShell `$PROFILE` **function** (`Set-Alias` can't carry arguments). Each card has a copy button (`writeText`); the current OS's card has a create button → `alias_create` IPC. **Quoting is the load-bearing part** (pure + unit-tested on BOTH sides): the alias value is POSIX single-quoted with close-reopen `'\''` (the adb lesson — no backslash escaping inside single quotes), the display line double-quotes it for `printf` escaping `` \ ` " $ ``, PowerShell doubles embedded `'`. ⚠️ The TS test pins the printf payload **semantically** (expand the double-quote escapes, compare against the rc line) — `\'` and `\\'` are both valid spellings inside double quotes, so a literal comparison would over-constrain the builder. Rust `shell_alias.rs`: `valid_name` (mirrors `lib/alias.ts::validAliasName`, cross-language pin in the tests), `rc_target($SHELL basename → .zshrc/.bashrc; fish → honest refusal — writing bash syntax into fish's config would be silent breakage; unknown → OS default)`, `already_defined` (trimmed `alias NAME=` line-start — comments don't count), `create` (append with newline guard, duplicates refused, returns a human message naming the file + reload hint). Windows `create` spawns PowerShell so `$PROFILE` resolves (runtime-unverified). **Manager section (v0.128.0):** below the builder, the rc file's existing aliases — searchable (`filterAliases`, pure: name+command substring, always alphabetical), edit fills the builder (the create button flips to "Aktualisieren"; `overwrite` is DERIVED from the loaded list, and the backend's race guard still refuses a definition that appeared since the last refresh), delete is a two-stage inline confirm (never `window.confirm`). Pure + tested: `unquote_shell` (single-/double-quoted/bare values, close-reopen round-trip, unquoted `#`/`;` ends the value), `parse_aliases` (later definition wins like the shell, comments/invalid names skipped), `remove_alias` (only the target dies, misses reported), `upsert_alias` (replace IN PLACE keeps the file spot; duplicate definition lines collapse). Windows lists empty + delete refuses (honest gap — `$PROFILE` functions aren't cheaply enumerable). **⚠️ The list walks the WHOLE startup chain (v0.128.1 — field report: aliases in a `source`d file were invisible):** seeds are the shell's own read order (`.zshenv` → `.zprofile` → `.zshrc` / the bash trio), `collect_aliases` follows `source`/`.` lines recursively (pure over an injected reader; only files under `$HOME` — plugin trees like /opt/homebrew stay out; depth ≤ 3, visited-set cycle guard; paths with spaces don't tokenise — accepted), later definitions win exactly like the live shell, and every entry carries its defining `file` label + `primary` flag. Update writes IN the defining file (a copy in the rc would shadow-fight the sourced line); delete sweeps EVERY file defining the name (removing only the last-wins definition would resurrect a shadowed one). **Focus contract:** taking focus (Enter on the row or Tab-autocomplete — the suggestion handler special-cases `alias` and hands focus to the panel instead of parking the caret in the search bar) selects the COMMAND field. IPC `alias_create(overwrite)` / `alias_list` / `alias_delete`, all async + `spawn_blocking`. Docs: `alias?`, README matrix.

### nosleep — persistent AC sleep profile (`nosleep.rs`, `NoSleepPanel.tsx`, v0.124.0, macOS)

`nosleep [on|off]` toggles the STORED power profile (`pmset -c sleep 0|1`) so the Mac never idle-sleeps on AC and it survives reboots — the persistent counterpart to `wakelock dark` (a session `caffeinate` assertion). Panel shows while the BARE keyword is fully typed (v0.130.0; an `on`/`off` arg stays Enter-only — the panel acts on it immediately, admin prompt) (`nosleepMode`/`nosleepFocus`, `PANEL_KINDS`, `platform:["mac"]`); bare `nosleep` shows the live AC + battery sleep timeouts + a switch, `nosleep on`/`off` act immediately. **⚠️ `pmset sleep` is MINUTES (0 = never), not a boolean** — so `on` remembers the AC value it overwrote (settings `nosleep.prev_ac_sleep`, in the command wrapper) and `off` restores it (fallback `DEFAULT_RESTORE_MIN` = 1). The remember/restore decision is the pure, unit-tested `value_to_remember` / `restore_minutes` / `target_minutes` — pinning the contract that `off` never sets `0` (an invisible re-lock), garbage/`None` falls back, and turning off records nothing. The write needs admin, so `set_ac_sleep` goes through `osascript … with administrator privileges` (one prompt; a cancelled dialog → the `-128` → friendly "Abgebrochen" message). Pure `parse_profile` reads `pmset -g custom`'s Battery/AC sections by exact `sleep` token (keeps `displaysleep`/`disksleep`/`Sleep On Power Button` out) — unit-tested against real output. IPC `nosleep_status`/`nosleep_set` async + `spawn_blocking` (the admin dialog blocks). The footer sleep-status indicator already shows `no-sleep` when the AC profile is `sleep 0`, so the effect is visible there too. Docs: `nosleep?`, README matrix.

### repo / export — git activity stats (`repo_stats.rs`, `RepoPanel.tsx`, `lib/repo.ts`, v0.123.0)

`repo [url|pfad]` analyses a git repo's history into the preview, oriented on the maintainer's **repo2viz** project (`~/claude/repo2viz`); `export [url|pfad]` (+ the panel's downward/`E`) writes the same as a self-contained HTML to `~/Downloads/<owner>-<repo>-activity.html`. Enter-activated (it clones/execs git); `repoMode`/`repoFocus` + `repoAutoExport`, `PANEL_KINDS` + `keepArg`. **Target resolution** (`resolve_repo_target`): a URL (`://` or scp-form) -> `analyze_remote` (full **bare** clone to temp — history + blobs so `--numstat` churn is exact offline, no working tree — analysed then deleted); a local path -> `analyze_local` in place; nothing -> the Finder-selected `.git` folder (`finder_selection::read`), else the `repo.no_target` sentinel. git-log mirrors repo2viz: `--no-merges --numstat --date=iso-strict` + a **control-char-separated** pretty format (RS/US) so subjects can't collide with delimiters. **`parse_git_log` is pure + tested**: commits/contributors, first/last (git log is newest-first, last-seen = first commit), churn, by-weekday (`weekday_hour` via **Zeller's congruence**), by-hour (author-local `%aI`), month timeline, top files/exts/authors, `classify_commit` categories, active days + `longest_streak` (civil day-ordinal). `build_html` is ONE self-contained file (inline CSS + bar charts, **no external request/`<script>`**) and **escapes every embedded name** (injection test pins it). **⚠️ argv-injection guard (security review):** `analyze_remote` rejects a URL starting with `-` and passes it after a `--` end-of-options marker (a value like `--upload-pack=…` would otherwise run as a git flag), and `git_log` uses `Command::current_dir(dir)` — not `-C <dir>` — to keep an untrusted path out of argv; both test-pinned. IPC async + `spawn_blocking`; export reveals in Finder. Pure `lib/repo.ts` (scaling, `sparkPoints`, palette, formatters) unit-tested. Docs: `repo?`, docs/repo.md. Deliberately NOT ported: Azure-DevOps work items, DORA, PO dashboard.

### clock — world clock (`ClockPanel.tsx`, `lib/clock.ts`, v0.121.0)

`clock` renders a live world-clock grid in the preview (shows while fully typed since v0.130.0; Enter hands over focus — `clockMode`/`clockFocus`, `PANEL_KINDS`). Each card shows the zone's time (1 s tick via a single `setNow` — cheap for a handful of cards, no per-card timer), local date, a ±1-day chip, the UTC offset and a day/night sun-moon wash. **All time maths is the platform `Intl` API — no offset/DST tables to rot** (`zoneTime`/`dayDelta`/`utcOffset` in the pure `lib/clock.ts`); the OS owns DST. A curated `CITY_ZONES` catalogue (57 cities across every UTC offset) drives the defaults + autocomplete (`matchCities`: city-prefix > city-substring > region/tz-id, added-zones excluded), but any IANA id the OS knows is reachable by typing its path. **⚠️ `dayDelta` is relative to a REFERENCE zone (default local) — the machine zone is unknowable in CI, so it takes an injectable `refTz` and the tests pin `"UTC"`** (a test that hardcodes local==UTC is the classic timezone-test trap; hit + fixed here). Zones persist server-side (settings `clock.zones`, JSON) via `get_/set_clock_zones` — `normalizeZones` dedupes, drops shell-junk ids, keeps valid unlisted IANA ids, and defaults on garbage, so a hand-edited value can't wedge the panel. localStorage was rejected (unreliable across popup recreation). Docs: `clock?`, README matrix. 11 lib tests.

### rickroll — the gag (`RickrollPanel.tsx`, `lib/rickroll.ts`, v0.122.0; local clip v0.128.2)

`rickroll` plays "Never Gonna Give You Up" with sound in the preview. **⚠️ The video is a BUNDLED asset, not a YouTube embed** (`src/assets/rickroll.mp4` — 480p/~5 MB, ffmpeg `crf 34` + 72k AAC + `+faststart`): the youtube-nocookie iframe died in the Tauri WKWebView with **"Error 153: Video player configuration error"** (embed/referer restrictions) — do not go back to an iframe. **Playback goes through a BLOB object URL (v0.128.3):** WKWebView's media loader streams via Range requests, which the embedded-asset protocol serves poorly → constant stutter; fetching the 5 MB once and handing the `<video>` an object URL gives the decoder full random access from memory (revoked on unmount; direct-URL fallback). Native `<video controls playsInline>`, unmuted-autoplay attempt (the opening Enter/click is the gesture; a rejection shows an honest blocked-hint and flips the restart button to "Abspielen"), lyric marquee (reduced-motion-guarded), open-in-browser fallback (`watchUrl` — the only remaining YouTube reference).

### disk / daisy — DaisyDisk-style disk usage (`disk_usage.rs`, `DiskPanel.tsx`, `lib/disk.ts`, v0.120.0)

`disk [pfad]` (alias `daisy`) reproduces DaisyDisk's core in the preview column: a concentric **sunburst** of on-disk usage (each ring a directory level, each segment's angular span ∝ its size), centre hub with the hovered item's size/share, a volume free-space bar, click-to-drill with a breadcrumb, a largest-files list, and move-to-Trash (the collector). Enter-activated inline panel (`diskMode`/`diskFocus`, `PANEL_KINDS` + `keepArg`); bare `disk` scans `~`, `disk /` the volume, `disk <pfad>` a folder. **On-disk size (`MetadataExt::blocks × 512`), not apparent size** — matches the volume readout; the walk does NOT follow symlinks (`entry.metadata()` doesn't traverse) and STAYS ON ONE FILESYSTEM (same `st_dev` as the root, so `~` never wanders into `/Volumes` or a network mount); permission errors are skipped, never fatal (scanning `/` hits many protected trees and must still finish). **Bounded payload + client-side drill:** the walk builds the full tree, then the pure `prune` bounds it once (depth ≤ `MAX_DEPTH` 6, ≤ `MAX_CHILDREN` 24 per node with the tail folded into a synthetic non-drillable "Sonstiges", global `MAX_NODES` cap) and the frontend drills within that view (instant, no managed state, no re-scan per click). `top_files` is collected across the WHOLE walk (independent of pruning) so "largest files" is honest even for pruned files. Sizes never lost: shown segments + "Sonstiges" always sum to the parent (test-pinned). The scan is async + `spawn_blocking` (a full `~` is 10⁵–10⁶ files) and a sibling thread emits throttled `disk-scan-progress {items,bytes}` events for the live count. `disk_trash` moves a path to the Trash via the `trash` crate (recoverable — not `rm`), confirmed on the frontend (`confirmDialog`), then re-scans. Volume free/used comes from `sysinfo::Disks` matched to the scanned path by the pure `match_volume` (longest mount-point prefix ON PATH BOUNDARIES, so `/Vol` never matches `/Volumes`; test-pinned). Frontend geometry is the pure `lib/disk.ts` (`sunburstArcs` nesting children within the parent's angular span; `arcPath` splits a full-circle segment into two half-arcs — the 100 % donut bug from `loc`; `segmentColor`/`topHue` for the DaisyDisk "same family, lighter outer rings" palette; `nodeAt` for drill), unit-tested. Arc entrance sweeps in staggered per ring (`.disk-arc`, `prefers-reduced-motion` drops it). Docs: CommandDoc (`disk?`), docs/disk.md, README matrix. Deliberately NOT ported: multi-volume side-by-side, drag-collector staging, previews — the full app owns those.

### adb — Android device control (`adb.rs`, `AdbPanel.tsx`, `lib/adb.ts`, v0.119.0)

`adb [remote|apps|wifi]` is the popup-sized companion to the maintainer's ADBOSS desktop app (`~/claude/adboss`): five views (Info · Steuern · Remote · Apps · WLAN) behind a chip row, arg-preselectable with discoverable sub-rows (the snitch/shazam pattern). Shows while fully typed (v0.130.0) inline panel (`adbMode`/`adbFocus`, `PANEL_KINDS` + `keepArg`); the dashboard polls every 5 s while visible (the ADBOSS cadence), device discovery polls alongside so plug/unplug surfaces live. **Every device-facing command form is taken 1:1 from ADBOSS' `core/adb_client.py`** (battle-tested: `svc wifi|bluetooth`, `media volume --stream N --set V --show`, brightness = disable adaptive mode THEN `settings put system screen_brightness`, `zen_mode` for DND, the airplane settings-put + broadcast PAIR, `monkey -p <pkg> -c LAUNCHER 1`, `exec-out screencap -p`, device-side `screenrecord` + pull) — pinned as a pure `control_commands`/`app_action_command` inventory with tests so the forms can't drift. **⚠️ Shell-injection safety is load-bearing:** `adb shell` takes ONE string, so every interpolated value passes strict validators (`valid_package`/`valid_keycode`/`valid_serial`/`valid_ip_port`, all tested against metacharacter payloads), and `escape_input_text` uses **POSIX close-quote-reopen (`'` → `'\''`)** — the ADBOSS backslash form (`\'`) was found to be an actual injection vector during review (single quotes have NO backslash escaping; `x'$(id)'` would have executed on the device shell) and the fix is test-pinned. Text is additionally **ASCII-gated** (sentinel `adb.text_not_ascii` — `input text` can't deliver unicode; honest refusal beats garbled characters), mirrored in the frontend (`textSendable`) so the hint shows while typing. `run()` enforces a hard timeout via watchdog + threaded pipe-drain (a sleeping WiFi device can block adb for minutes; reading after wait would deadlock a full pipe). Screenshot reuses `copy_png_to_clipboard_and_history` (canonical PNG → watcher fuse, no duplicate row) + the screenshot sound; recording holds the local `adb shell screenrecord` child in the managed `AdbRecordState`, stop = device-side `pkill -2` + settle (moov atom) + pull to `~/Downloads` + reveal. WiFi flow grabs the device IP BEFORE `tcpip` (the adbd restart can drop the connection), waits 1.5 s, then connects. Apps view: `pm list packages [-3]`, actions launch/stop/clear/disable/enable via shell + uninstall via top-level `adb uninstall` (`None` marker in the pure inventory); destructive actions go through `confirmDialog`. Docs on three surfaces: CommandDoc (`adb?` + README matrix + Features tab), `docs/adb.md`, and the expandable **Settings → Android (adb)** section (`AdbSection`: live found/authorized chips + setup guide; deep-link id `adb`). Deliberately NOT ported: logcat, Bluetooth-HCI analyzer, file browser, settings browser — they need a full window and stay in ADBOSS.

### loc — lines-of-code statistics (`loc.rs`, `LocPanel.tsx`, `lib/loc.ts`, v0.117.0)

`loc [pfad]` counts the Finder selection (bare form, macOS — same Automation surface as Ctrl+Shift+F) or an explicit path, rendered as an inline preview panel (`locMode`/`locFocus`, in `PANEL_KINDS` with `keepArg`; **Enter-activated** — tokei walks a whole directory tree, never while typing). Engine is the **`tokei` crate (v14, MIT)** — hand-rolling comment parsing per language is exactly the correctness trap this command exists to avoid. Per-language rows use `lang.summarise()` (folds embedded children like JS-in-HTML into the parent's line counts) while `reports.len()` stays the true FILE count (children live inside the same files — never double-counted). `treat_doc_strings_as_comments` is on, so Python docstrings count as comments ("Kommentare und Dokumentation" — a reliable separate docs column doesn't exist in any counter; IntelliJ's Statistic plugin lumps them too). Ignore behaviour: default respects `.gitignore` + skips hidden files (else node_modules dwarfs every JS project's own code); the panel checkbox re-runs with `no_ignore` + `hidden`. **⚠️ Field-learned in the fixture test: the `ignore` crate honours `.gitignore` only INSIDE a git repo** (`require_git`) — a plain folder's `.gitignore` is inert; the test fakes a `.git` dir and the CommandDoc documents the user-visible rule. House style: `count()` is the thin impure shell; `aggregate()` (sorting code-desc + name tiebreak, percentages of CODE lines — GitHub-language-bar semantics — NaN-free on zero code, ghost-row dropping, `inaccurate` surfacing) is pure, and the **correctness itself is pinned by a hand-counted fixture** (temp dir with Rust `//`+`///` comments and a Python docstring; exact `(files, code, comments, blanks)` assertions). IPC `loc_count` is async + `spawn_blocking`; `None` paths → `finder_selection::read()`, empty selection → `loc.no_selection` sentinel (panel renders "select a folder" vs. the Automation-denied hint separately).
