Frontend: pure `lib/loc.ts` — Linguist colour map + FNV-hash HSL fallback (deterministic), `donutSegments` (ring-segment SVG paths; **a 100 % share renders as two half-arcs** — an SVG arc with identical start/end draws nothing, the classic full-donut bug, test-pinned; slivers < 2 % fold into "Other"), de-style formatters. `LocPanel` shows totals tiles, the stacked share bar, donut + legend, the per-language table, an `inaccurate` warning when tokei flagged parse problems, and the ignore toggle; R re-counts, Esc exits (keydown guard skips while typing in the search field — the weather lesson).

### Audio output device (`audio.rs`, v0.80.0)

`sound` (alias **`audio`**) opens an inline **audio output panel** in the right preview column —
same arrow-key model as `brightness`. **Unlike the other inline panels, it shows directly:** typing `sound`/`audio` auto-sets `soundMode` (the `isSoundCmd` effect) so `SoundPanel.tsx` renders in the preview **without pressing Enter**; Enter only hands it keyboard focus (`soundFocus`). The panel has a **volume slider at the top**
(**←/→** adjust ∓5 immediately — no Enter — + click/drag the bar) above the
device list, where **↑/↓** select a device, **Enter** switches the system default
output to it, **Esc** leaves. `useKeyboardNav` is gated off while `soundFocus`;
`soundMode` auto-exits when the query is no longer `sound`. IPC: `list_audio_outputs` / `set_audio_output` (`AudioDevice { id, name, is_default }`) + **`get_system_volume` / `set_system_volume`** (absolute level with read-back; macOS via `osascript`, Linux via `wpctl`/`pactl`; Windows has no cheap read-back so the slider hides — `adjust_volume` stays the relative Shift+↑/↓ path). The volume slider reads the level on mount, applies absolute sets optimistically + reconciles with the returned applied level.

- **macOS:** raw CoreAudio FFI (`#[link(... "CoreAudio")]`). Enumerate
  `kAudioHardwarePropertyDevices`, keep devices with output channels (stream
  config, output scope), read each `kAudioObjectPropertyName`, and get/set
  `kAudioHardwarePropertyDefaultOutputDevice`. `id` is the decimal
  `AudioDeviceID`. Fully tested live (list + set-to-default no-op + bad-id).
- **Windows (runtime-unverified):** the public MMDevice API
  (`IMMDeviceEnumerator.EnumAudioEndpoints(eRender, ACTIVE)` + friendly name via
  `IPropertyStore`/`PKEY_Device_FriendlyName`/`PropVariantToStringAlloc`) lists
  endpoints; the default is switched with the undocumented `IPolicyConfig` COM
  object — created via raw `ole32!CoCreateInstance` directly on its IID (linked
  as `co_create_instance_raw` to avoid clashing with the `windows` crate's typed
  one) + a hand-declared vtable, calling `SetDefaultEndpoint` for all three
  roles. Same approach as NirCmd/SoundVolumeView (no public "set default" API).
  Compile-validated against `windows` 0.61 (isolated `windows-gnu` check); needs
  the `Win32_Media_Audio` / `Win32_Devices_FunctionDiscovery` /
  `Win32_System_Com_StructuredStorage` / `Win32_System_Variant` /
  `Win32_UI_Shell{,_PropertiesSystem}` features.
- **Linux (v0.84.0):** PulseAudio / PipeWire via the `pactl` CLI (PipeWire ships a `pipewire-pulse` shim so `pactl` works on both). `audio::parse_pactl_sinks` (pure, unit-tested) reads `Name:`/`Description:` from `pactl list sinks`, the default from `pactl get-default-sink`, and switches with `pactl set-default-sink`.

### Philips Hue (`hue.rs`, v0.84.40)

`hue` opens an inline **lamp controller** in the right preview column — same arrow-key model as `brightness`/`sound`. Enter on the `hue` row → `App.tsx`'s `hueMode` + `hueFocus` render `HuePanel.tsx`; `useKeyboardNav` is gated off while `hueFocus`, and `hueMode` auto-exits when the query is no longer `hue`. The panel has two states: a **connect card** (when unpaired/unreachable) and the **control view** (an "All lamps" master row + a row per lamp).

- **Local-first, cross-platform.** All bridge traffic is **plain HTTP on the LAN** (the classic Hue local API on port 80) via the new `ureq` dep (`default-features=false` → no TLS/gzip pulled in), and discovery is **local SSDP** (UDP multicast `239.255.255.250:1900`, std-only — no Philips cloud). Bridge IP + paired username persist in the settings table (`hue.bridge_ip` / `hue.username`).
- **Connect flow:** `discover_bridge()` (SSDP → probe each candidate's unauth `/api/config` for a `bridgeid`) or a manually-entered IP → the user presses the bridge's physical link button → `pair()` POSTs `/api {devicetype}` → on success stores the whitelisted username; if the button wasn't pressed the bridge returns error 101, surfaced as the `hue.link_button` sentinel (the panel shows a "press the button" hint).
- **Control:** `list_lights` (GET `/lights`) → per-lamp `{on, brightness%, reachable, supports_color, dimmable}`; `set_light` PUTs `/lights/<id>/state`, `set_all` PUTs **group 0** `/groups/0/action`. The **8 colour presets** are round swatches (warm-white + 7 hues); the frontend sends a hex, `rgb_to_xy` converts it (gamma + Wide-RGB-D65) to the bulb's CIE xy. Keyboard (v0.84.44): **Tab** next lamp / **Shift+Tab** previous (wrap; "All lamps" is the top row; ↑↓ also move) · **←→** ∓10% brightness (debounced) · **Enter**/**Space** toggle on/off · **1–8** colour · Esc exit; everything is also clickable. **The selected row auto-scrolls to the vertical centre (v0.84.46)** — `HuePanel` keeps a `rowRefs` array and a `useEffect([sel])` calls `scrollIntoView({block:"center"})` so Tab-navigating a long lamp list keeps the selection in view. **The brightness bars are also click/drag sliders (v0.84.54)** — `setBrightnessFromPointer` maps the pointer x on the track to 0–100 % → `applyBrightness` (pointer-capture for drag-outside).
- **Pure + unit-tested:** `hex_to_rgb`, `rgb_to_xy`, `percent_to_bri`/`bri_to_percent`, `build_state_body`, `first_error` (101→sentinel), `parse_light`. The HTTP/SSDP calls need a live bridge (not tested). IPC: `hue_status` · `hue_discover` · `hue_set_bridge_ip` · `hue_pair` · `hue_forget` · `hue_list_lights` · `hue_set_light` · `hue_set_all`.
- **Beat sync (`lib/disco-engine.ts` + `HueBeatSync.tsx` + `disco` command, v0.84.43; persistent engine v0.84.46)** — a mic-driven "disco" ported in spirit from the raspi3 `disco-controller`. It reuses IR's own `BpmAnalyzer` (`lib/bpm.ts` — the analyzer the disco app was ported *from*). On each confident beat (`confidence ≥ 0.15`) it drives the lamps as a **round-robin chase** via `hue_set_light` — punch the next lamp bright + colour, settle the previous — because the Hue **group** endpoint is rate-limited to ~1 cmd/s (the disco round-robins individual lamps, ≤10/s, for exactly this reason). 3 modes: **rainbow** (cycle a vivid palette per beat), **pulse** (fixed colour, brightness chase), **strobe** (fixed colour, near-dark between beats). A **sensitivity** slider tunes `BpmAnalyzer.setSensitivity` (per-instance onset threshold; the `bpm` command's analyzer is unaffected). Lamp on/brightness is snapshotted on start and restored (to warm white) on stop. **Tuning (v0.84.48):** punches are **loudness-gated, not confidence-gated** — the lights flash on every onset while music plays (gate on the level), matching the disco-controller (the old `confidence ≥ 0.15` gate dropped most punches on irregular music / before a tempo lock). An **`INPUT_GAIN` (4×)** stage lifts quiet laptop-mic signals; the **gauge** is a full-band **dBFS VU meter** (raw-mic tap before the bass filter, attack/release smoothed) so it swings with the whole mix rather than the ×8 bass-band RMS it used to show; default sensitivity is 0.65.
  - **Engine (v0.84.46 singleton; detection reworked v0.84.52):** the engine is a **module-level singleton** (`discoEngine`), NOT a React component, so its *state* survives popup dismissal. Detection was a `ScriptProcessorNode.onaudioprocess` callback — but it **never fired reliably in WKWebView** (no beats, frozen gauge, dead lamps), so v0.84.52 switched it to the proven **`requestAnimationFrame` + `AnalyserNode`** path (same as the BPM detector): `source→gain(INPUT_GAIN)→HP→LP→detectAnalyser` for the bass band + `gain→levelAnalyser` for the full-band gauge + `source→gain(0)→destination` (anti-stutter). `HueBeatSync.tsx` is a thin UI bound via `useSyncExternalStore(discoEngine.subscribe, discoEngine.getSnapshot)`; the **`disco` command** (`disco 1` = on · `disco 0` = off · bare = toggle; `parseDiscoArg`) drives the same singleton, so panel + command stay in sync. **Persistence caveat:** rAF is throttled while the window is hidden, so disco currently **pauses while the popup is closed**; true run-while-hidden needs an **AudioWorklet** (audio-thread, not visibility-throttled) — a planned follow-up. The ScriptProcessor (the previous attempt at this) didn't actually work. **Pure helpers (v0.84.53):** the beat→light maths is extracted to `lib/disco-math.ts` (`beatColor`, `floorBrightness`, `nextIndex`) and the level maths to `lib/audio-level.ts` (`rms`, `rmsToDbfs`, `dbfsToLevel`, `smoothStep`) — both unit-tested and shared with the BPM detector; `BpmAnalyzer.setSensitivity` uses the tested `onsetThresholdForSensitivity`.

### System stats (`system_stats.rs`, `stats_history.rs`, `StatsPanel.tsx`, v0.84.59; history v0.84.137)

`stats` renders a **read-only, auto-refreshing** dashboard in the right preview column — same inline family as `brightness`/`sound`/`hue`. Enter on the `stats` row → `App.tsx`'s `statsMode` + `statsFocus` render `StatsPanel.tsx`; `useKeyboardNav` is gated off while `statsFocus`, `statsMode` auto-exits when the query is no longer `stats`. The panel polls `get_system_stats` every **1.5 s** and shows: **CPU** (overall + per-core usage bars, brand, freq, core count, load average), **memory** (used/available + swap), **battery & power** (charge %, state, time remaining, **instantaneous watts**, health, cycle count, temp), **sensors** (temps + fans), **disks** (per-mount usage), **network** (live up/down throughput), **host** (OS/kernel/arch/uptime). Read-only (no selection model): while `statsFocus`, **↑/↓** (+ PageUp/Down, Home/End) scroll the panel via a ref-based instant `scrollBy`, Esc exits. **Scroll perf (v0.84.62):** the usage bars are sized with `transform: scaleX/scaleY` (no `transition`/`will-change`) — an earlier `will-change` left ~15 permanent compositor layers that the compositor had to blend every scroll frame (jank); each card also has `contain: content` so scroll repaints stay isolated.

**Value tweens + heat glow (v0.115.0).** Live values glide instead of snapping, and a maxed resource glows like a heating filament — WITHOUT reopening the v0.84.62 wound: no `will-change`, no CSS transitions on bars; a **single shared rAF loop** (`TWEENS` set + `addTween` in StatsPanel.tsx) drives every animated element via **ref-written inline styles/textContent** for `STAT_TWEEN_MS` = 600 ms per poll (< `POLL_MS` 1500, so values come to rest), then stops — idle cost zero. Pure math in `lib/stats-anim.ts` (`easeOutCubic`/`tweenAt`/`heatLevel`/`isHot`/`bytesFormatterFor`/`rateFormatterFor`, unit-tested): heat ramps over `HEAT_START` 70 → `HEAT_FULL` 90 — **derived from the panel's own `loadColor` scale** (≥70 amber, ≥90 red), so "ausgelastet" = exactly where the bar turns red. Hot state: molten-flow gradient in the fill (`.stat-heat-flow`), breathing ember glow, and a **directional heat beam** radiating from the fill's tip into the empty track (`.stat-heat-breathe`, mounted ONLY while hot — usually zero animated layers exist); the card's headline % gets `.stat-heat-num` ember tint. The glow shadow's intensity follows the TWEENED value through the amber band (paint-only, isolated by `contain: content`). Three rules ported from the raspi-monitor lesson: (a) each animated node has ONE writer — the ref; React renders containers only, and a per-render `reassert` layout effect re-applies the last shown frame so reconciliation can't leave a stale one; (b) byte/rate strings stay **width-stable** mid-tween via `bytesFormatterFor(target)` (unit + decimals locked to the TARGET for the whole run); (c) threshold state (`isHot`, colors at target) snaps immediately, never lagging the tween. A mid-tween new target starts from the SHOWN value (smooth handoff). `useStatTween`'s latest-`apply` ref is updated in an EFFECT, not at render (react-hooks/refs; the BpmDetector `onExitRef` pattern), and `reassert` is a `useCallback` so no ref is read during render. Battery's explicit `color` override (charging-green) deliberately gets NO heat — an override is a different semantic than load. Per-core cells tween but skip glow/beam (8–10 animated shadows for little signal). `prefers-reduced-motion`: tweens snap (checked in the hook), CSS heat animations off (media block; a calm static ember remains). History mode is untouched.

**Live ↔ History toggle (`stats_history.rs`, v0.84.137).** The panel header has a **Live / History** segmented toggle; in History mode a **time-range dropdown** (1h / 6h / 24h / 7d) drives a per-metric line-chart view. History needs persisted samples, so a new module `stats_history.rs` runs an **always-on background collector** (`start_collector`, spawned at startup in `lib.rs`): every `SAMPLE_INTERVAL_SECS` (60 s) it `gather()`s, reduces to the **core series** via the pure-tested `sample_from` (CPU %, mem %, net rx/tx, power W?, CPU temp °C?, battery %?), and inserts a row into the `stats_history` table (created in `db::open` via `init_schema`), pruning rows older than `RETENTION_SECS` (7 days) roughly hourly. IPC `get_stats_history(range_secs)` queries the range and **downsamples** to ≤ `MAX_POINTS` (240) bucket-averaged points (the pure-tested `downsample` — fixed-width buckets, nullable metrics average only present values) so the payload stays small + the SVG smooth. Numeric telemetry only (not encrypted). Frontend: `StatsPanel`'s `HistoryView` fetches on range-change + every 30 s and renders a `LineChartCard` per metric (CPU/mem/battery fixed to `[0,100]`; power/temp auto-scaled) + a dual-line `NetworkChartCard` (download accent, upload amber); SVG paths come from the pure-tested `lib/stats-chart.ts` (`seriesExtent` / `linePath` / `areaPath`). The live-poll effect is gated to `mode === "live"` (no `get_system_stats` polling while viewing history); the panel's keydown handler skips when the target is the toggle/dropdown so the native `<select>` keeps its own keys. Until ~2 samples exist the view shows a "Collecting history…" card.

- **Cross-platform, best-effort per OS; sources degrade gracefully (missing sensors omitted, never faked).** `system_stats::gather()` (runs on the Tauri command worker; blocks ~200 ms = the CPU sample window, reused for the network rate):
  - **CPU / memory / swap / disks / network / uptime / load / host** via **`sysinfo`** (workspace dep; features `system`+`disk`+`component`+`network`). Two `refresh_cpu_all` samples spaced by `MINIMUM_CPU_UPDATE_INTERVAL` give usage; the same window yields the per-second network rate.
  - **Battery & power draw** via the **`battery` crate** (`starship-battery`, package-renamed; IOKit/WMI/sysfs). `energy_rate` = live system power in **watts** (`BatteryStat.power_watts`).
  - **Temperatures** via `sysinfo::Components` (hwmon/SMC/WMI), then **summarised** by the pure, unit-tested `summarize_temps` — the raw (often dozens of cryptic, duplicated) sensors are bucketed by label heuristic into clean **CPU / GPU / Battery / SSD** rows (averaged). Apple-Silicon `PMU tdie*` / Intel `TC0P` / Linux `Core N`/`Package`/`Tctl` → CPU; `gas gauge`/`battery` → Battery; `NAND`/`nvme` → SSD.
  - **Fans:** no `sysinfo` API on any OS, so read directly. **macOS** via a **self-contained IOKit SMC reader** (`mod macos_smc`, no external crate): opens the `AppleSMC` user client, enumerates keys once (cached), reads `F<n>Ac` fan-speed keys, and decodes the SMC value FourCC types (`flt` LE-float, `fpe2` `((b0<<8|b1)>>2)`, `sp78` `i16/256`, `ui8/16/32`) — all in the pure, unit-tested `decode()`. Idle (0 rpm) fans are shown too (Apple's fan curve keeps them off until warm). **Linux** via `/sys/class/hwmon/*/fan*_input` (`mod linux_hwmon`, label from `fan*_label`/chip `name`). **Windows:** no rootless fan API → none. On Apple Silicon, if `Components` yields no CPU temp, `macos_smc::cpu_temperature()` supplies one by averaging the per-core thermal sensors (`Tp*`/`Te*`/`Tf*`/`TC*`). Verified live on an M1 Pro (2 fans ~1600 rpm; CPU/Battery/SSD temps; battery 13–18 W draw).
- **Pure cores unit-tested:** the SMC `decode` value formats, `summarize_temps` bucketing/averaging, and the frontend `lib/format-stats.ts` (`humanBytes`/`humanRate`/`humanUptime`/`humanDuration`/`usedPct`, 1024-based). IPC: `get_system_stats` → `SystemStats` (snake_case fields, matching the repo IPC convention). No capabilities/window entry needed — renders inline in the popup.

### Live uptime (`UptimePanel.tsx`, `lib/uptime.ts`, v0.84.64; readable-hero redesign v0.84.66)

`uptime` renders a **live, animated** uptime readout in the right preview column (same inline family as `stats`/`brightness`). Enter on the `uptime` row → `App.tsx`'s `uptimeMode` + `uptimeFocus` render `UptimePanel.tsx`; `useKeyboardNav` is gated off while `uptimeFocus`, `uptimeMode` auto-exits when the query is no longer `uptime`, Esc exits. **Hierarchy (v0.84.66):** the **hero** is the uptime in a normal readable clock format **`Dd HH:MM:SS.mmm`** (big, clean tabular monospace; the 3 millisecond digits are a smaller, constantly-shimmering tail — v0.84.67); the **converted** representation — total seconds down to **microseconds** — sits **below, small and dimmed** (`.uptime-sub`: muted base) but stays animated. (The earlier versions made the µs seconds the giant hero, which read as broken/noisy — v0.84.64 even smeared two half-digits per cell by translating each digit by its *continuous* value; now digits snap to `Math.floor(odometerValue(...))` so each cell shows exactly one glyph.) Every digit gets a **"heat" treatment** — the instant its value changes it flares to the accent colour + glow (`.hot`, no transition) and cools back over ~0.6–0.7 s (CSS transition): the hero's seconds pulse on each tick, and the subtle line's sub-second digits stay lit as a constant quiet shimmer. The boot timestamp is shown under both.

- **Cheap + smooth.** Base uptime comes from a single lightweight IPC `get_uptime_secs` (`sysinfo::System::uptime()`, no `System` instance), fetched **once** and anchored to `performance.now()` so the value flows smoothly from there (absolute value within ~1 s of true, irrelevant for the animation). Driven by **one `requestAnimationFrame` loop writing `textContent` + the `.hot` class to DOM refs** — no React re-render per frame; only `color`/`text-shadow` animate (paint-only), so 60 fps motion is cheap. A per-second hero "pop"; reduced-motion disables the decorative animation.
- **Pure cores unit-tested** (`lib/uptime.ts`, `uptime.test.ts`): `uptimeBreakdown`, `odometerValue` (continuous digit value; the component floors it), `integerDigitCount`, `odometerPowers`. IPC: `get_uptime_secs`. No window/capabilities entry — renders inline.

### tokens — Claude Code usage from Token Tracker (`token_usage.rs`, `TokensPanel.tsx`, `lib/token-usage.ts`, v0.101.0; perf v0.101.3)

`tokens` (alias `usage`) renders Claude Code token/cost totals in the right preview column (same inline-panel family as `stats`/`weather`; `tokensMode`/`tokensFocus` in `App.tsx`, in `PANEL_KINDS`). **Shows while fully typed (v0.130.0)** — one local HTTP fetch to the [Token Tracker](../token-tracker) dashboard. Period chips (Today / 7d / 30d / All; **default Today**), three tabs (Overview · Projects⇄Sessions · Models), and a cache-include toggle. Unreachable → hint card (`token_usage::ERR_UNREACHABLE`); does **not** auto-start the tracker. Esc exits; R refreshes.

- **HTTP only** — `GET http://127.0.0.1:5010/api/{overview,models,projects,sessions,daily}?from=&to=` (local `YYYY-MM-DD`); no SQLite/JSONL reads. Endpoints run **in parallel** (`thread::scope`); **sessions are opt-in** (`include_sessions`, default false — 30d payload is multi-second alone). Period windows match tracker `app.js` (`7d`/`30d` = today−7/30). For `Today`, also peeks `/api/daily` for yesterday so a zero-token day can show a prior-day banner. Pure parsers + period bounds + `day_peek_from_daily` unit-tested; IPC `token_usage_fetch(period?, include_sessions?)`.
- **Frontend:** `TokensPanel` + format helpers in `lib/token-usage.ts` (KPI cards, ranked lists; v1 has no charts). Empty-today banner when tracker reports 0 but yesterday had usage (Cursor/Composer is **not** tracked — Claude Code JSONL only); Sessions sub-tab triggers the lazy sessions fetch.

### weather — current conditions + forecast (`weather.rs`, `WeatherPanel.tsx`, `lib/weather.ts`, v0.97.0)

`weather` (alias `wetter`) renders the current conditions + a **next-12-hours strip** (v0.106.0: up to five 3-hour slots from the SAME `/forecast` response — no extra HTTP call — each with compact scene, temp, and a rain-probability badge when `pop ≥ 0.2`; hours labelled in the **location's** local time via the report's `tz_offset`, so `weather tokyo` shows Tokyo hours; pure `parse_hourly(v, now, max)` keeps a slot while its 3-h window still overlaps `now`, + `forecast_tz_offset`, both unit-tested, frontend `lib/weather.ts::hourLabel`) + a **5-day forecast** in the right preview column (same inline-panel family as `stats`/`hue`/`calendar`; `weatherMode`/`weatherFocus` in `App.tsx`, in `PANEL_KINDS` with `keepArg` so `weather berlin` keeps its city argument). **Shows while fully typed (v0.130.0)** — one debounced fetch for the complete keyword; the live command arg drives a debounced refetch. **City autocomplete (v0.100.0):** while typing `weather <partial>` (before the panel takes focus), matching major cities from the bundled, offline `lib/cities.ts` (`matchCities` — diacritic-insensitive, prefix-before-substring, population-ranked; ~250 cities incl. German down to ~100k) surface as `command-suggestion` rows (`weatherCitySuggestionEntries` in `App.tsx`, `completion: "weather <City>"`). They **REPLACE the raw `weather <partial>` command row** in `combined` (whose Enter would just fetch the partial and fail), so the top/biggest city is the default selection — **Tab/→ fills** it, **Enter completes + opens** the panel (both reuse the generic command-suggestion handlers, no new key wiring). A city not in the list yields no suggestions → the normal command row runs (OpenWeather tries the typed name). Gated off once `weatherFocus` (panel open), so it only appears while typing. `useKeyboardNav` is gated off while `weatherFocus`; the panel auto-exits when the query is no longer `weather`. While focused: Esc exits, and — **only when the search field is NOT the key target** — ↑/↓/PgUp/PgDn scroll and **R** refreshes; auto-refresh every 10 min. **Keyboard gotcha (v0.99.1):** the city is TYPED into the search field *after* the panel opens (`weather berlin` → edit to `weather darmstadt`), so the panel's window-capture keydown handler MUST NOT swallow keys destined for a text input — the `R`-refresh shortcut was eating the "r" in "da**r**mstadt" (→ "damstadt", not found). The handler now returns early when `e.target` is an INPUT/TEXTAREA/contentEditable (Esc still exits from anywhere); the scroll/refresh shortcuts only apply when the user isn't typing. **Unlike `calendar`** — whose arrow keys ARE the navigation and are deliberately captured even from the search field — weather has no such typed-arg-vs-shortcut need beyond scroll, so the guard is safe here but would break calendar's month/year arrows. Cross-platform (just needs network + a key).

- **Provider: OpenWeatherMap** (the free **2.5** API — `/weather` + `/forecast`, callable with a standard free key; One Call 3.0 needs a separate sub). The API key lives in the settings table (`weather.api_key`) — **never committed to the repo** (it's a user secret; the code default is empty). `weather.units` (`metric`|`imperial`) is the other setting. **"My location"** is IP-geolocated by reusing `snitch::geolocate_self()` (keyless ip-api.com → lat/lon); a `weather <city>` arg overrides it with an OWM `q=<city>` query. Only the coordinates/city + the key leave the machine.
- **Pure + unit-tested (`weather.rs`, 10 tests):** `parse_current` (OWM `/weather` JSON → `Current`, tolerant of missing optional fields, errors only without temp/weather), `parse_forecast` (groups the 5-day/3-hour `list[]` **by the API-local date from `dt_txt`**, min/max across all slots, icon+description of the slot **closest to 12:00** — deterministic, no clock read), `owm_icon_to_kind` (OWM icon code → the animation-friendly `WeatherKind` enum: clear-day/clear-night/clouds/drizzle/rain/thunderstorm/snow/mist; unknown → clouds, never panics), `build_url` (coords or percent-encoded city), `location_from_current`. The HTTP calls (`ureq`) aren't tested (need a live key). IPC: `weather_fetch(city?)` (`async` + `spawn_blocking` since v0.105.0, like all network-touching Hue commands — the older sync form ran the two 6-s-timeout round trips ON the main thread; `Err(weather::ERR_NO_KEY)` when unconfigured) · `get_weather_config` (only whether a key is stored — the key never crosses IPC) · `set_weather_config(api_key?, units?)` (a `None` api_key leaves the stored key untouched — so saving from the settings form with a blank field keeps the existing key).
- **Frontend `WeatherPanel.tsx`:** three states — a **connect card** (no key → inline API-key input + "get a free key" link; the panel detects this via the `weather.no_key` fetch sentinel), an **error/retry** card, and the **report**. The report is a **hero** (animated scene as the backdrop, big current temp + condition + feels-like + hi/lo overlaid), a 3-chip current-stats row (humidity/wind/pressure — the wind chip is units-aware: km/h for metric, mph for imperial), and up to **5 forecast cards** (weekday · compact animated icon · hi/lo). **Animation:** a self-contained `<WeatherScene kind compact?>` — CSS keyframes injected once (`wx-*`), all `prefers-reduced-motion`-guarded — draws a spinning-ray sun, a moon + twinkling stars, drifting cloud puffs (overlapping rounded divs), falling rain/drizzle drops, drifting snow, a periodic lightning flash, or drifting mist bands, with an index-based (memoised, non-reshuffling) particle layout. `lib/weather.ts` holds the pure display helpers (`WEATHER_LABELS`/`WEATHER_GRADIENT`, `mpsToKmh`, `windCompass` 8-point, `roundTemp` no-"-0", `parseYmd`/`dayName`/`isToday` local-date, `isNight`/`hasPrecip`), unit-tested in `weather.test.ts`. Settings → **Weather** (`WeatherSection`: password-masked key field that keeps a stored key when left blank + a metric/imperial select; deep-link id `weather`).

### figlet — ASCII-art banners (`figlet/mod.rs`, `figlet/fonts.rs`, `FigletPreview.tsx`, `lib/figlet.ts`, v0.85.0)

`figlet` (aliases `banner`/`ascii`) turns text into a FIGlet ASCII-art banner. It's a **whole-list override** like `meme`/`kill`: `isFigletMode` (`App.tsx`) replaces `combined` with **`figlet-font` rows** — the left list is a font gallery where each row previews the user's text in that font; the selected font's **big banner** renders in the preview (`PreviewPanel` → `FigletPreview`, monospace `whitespace-pre` + `overflow-x-auto` — never wrap the art). **Enter** copies the full banner via `pasteGenerated` (exactly the rendered text incl. newlines; respects `save_history`); **Shift+Enter copies it as a PNG instead (v0.85.8)** — the pure, unit-tested `lib/figlet-png.ts::cropBanner` trims blank edge lines + the common indent + trailing whitespace, `bannerPngBase64` renders the cropped grid to an offscreen monospace canvas in the CURRENT theme colours (opaque, 2× scale, small padding) and the `figlet_copy_png` IPC writes it canonically to the clipboard + history (`[figlet · <text>]`) via the shared `copy_png_to_clipboard_and_history` helper (extracted from `qr_copy_png` — canonical re-encode + watcher fuse, so no duplicate image row); **Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+Enter is the TRANSPARENT variant (v0.85.10)**: the PNG is rendered with a transparent background (`themePngColors(transparent)` → `bg: null`, only the glyphs in the theme text colour) and BOTH saved to `~/Downloads` via `figlet_save_png` (`figlet-<slug>-<ts>.png`, Finder-revealed) AND copied to the clipboard/history via `figlet_copy_png` (copy first — the reveal steals focus, so it's the last visible action; alpha survives the canonical clipboard re-encode). `useKeyboardNav`'s `onEnter` passes `metaKey` through to `activate` for this. The preview-header hint reads `Enter copies · ⇧⏎ PNG · ⌘⇧⏎ transparent`. Relatedly, `save_image_entry_to_downloads` (the every-image-clip **Save to Downloads** button / Cmd+S in `PreviewPanel`) now derives its filename from the row's summary via the pure `image_basename_from_summary` + `sanitize_file_slug` (`[figlet · hello]` → `figlet-hello-<ts>.png`; plain images keep `inspector-rust-image-<ts>.png`). **Tab/→** fills `@font` (rz semantics). Full reference: [docs/figlet.md](./docs/figlet.md).

- **Engine (`figlet/mod.rs`):** rendering is the `figlet-rs` crate (MIT, `FIGlet::from_content`/`convert`, respects the font's smushing). Everything figlet-rs does NOT do — width-wrap (word-group: each wrapped row is a full smushed render), align L/C/R, trailing-trim, box border, comment-wrap (`//`/`#`/JSDoc/`<!-- -->`, block styles guard their close delimiter; order = box→comment), and unsupported-char detection (`convert(single char) == None` → reported, never silently dropped) — is **pure, unit-tested** here. Wrapped behind a `RenderEngine` trait (render + font enumeration) so a future `boxes`/`cowsay` engine plugs in via a reserved `@engine` selector without touching the parser/gallery/layout (a `DummyEngine` test proves it).
- **Font bundle (`figlet/fonts.rs`, `build.rs`, `assets/figlet-fonts/`):** the full **~550-font** set (vendored from pyfiglet via `scripts/gen-figlet-fonts.mjs`) is embedded **gzip-compressed** (build.rs generates the `FONT_BLOBS` table) and inflated **lazily on first use**, `Arc`-cached (never twice). Binary carries **~2.3 MB**, nothing decompresses at startup. Metadata (name/category/popular) is the committed `assets/figlet-fonts.categories.json` overlay — the frontend gets metadata via `figlet_fonts`, never bytes. **Vendoring gotchas** (in the gen script): Latin-1 `.flf` are transcoded to UTF-8 (figlet-rs needs UTF-8), and a **malformed code-tag tail** (figlet-rs's strict parser rejects ~11 fonts incl. `standard` on `codetag_lines % (height+1) != 0`) is truncated to the required 95 ASCII + 7 German glyphs so they parse (rarer accents become a reported "unsupported char"). Licenses harvested → `THIRDPARTY-FONTS`; header-hostile fonts excluded (0 found). The `every_font_inflates_parses_and_renders_nonempty` test iterates all 550 (catches a broken/mis-encoded font).
- **Settings (bruno pattern):** `FigletDefaults` (font/width/align/trim/comment/boxed/pinned/save_history), one settings row per field, `figlet_set_defaults` emits `figlet-defaults-changed`. Settings → Figlet (`FigletSection`, mirrors `FakerSection`).
- **IPC:** `figlet_fonts` (meta + pinned merge) · `figlet_render` (big preview/copy payload → `Banner{text,unsupported,wrapped}`) · `figlet_gallery` (compact per-font samples, capped lines×cols) · `figlet_get_/set_defaults`. Frontend: `parseFigletCommand` (order-agnostic `@font`/`--flag`/free text; unknown `--token` stays literal), `fuzzyFont`/`galleryFonts`. Gallery samples are batched+debounced+cached per text (name-only above `FIGLET_SAMPLE_TEXT_CAP`; ≤`FIGLET_MAX_ROWS` rows — the long tail is reached by `@name` fuzzy search).

### shazam — song recognition from the mic (`shazam.rs`, `ShazamPanel.tsx`, `lib/mic-record.ts`, v0.84.250)

`shazam` records ~10 s from the microphone, generates a Shazam **audio-signature**, queries Shazam's public discovery API, and shows the matched track (cover · title · artist · album · genre · year · Shazam link) in the inline preview panel. Enter-activated (opens the mic), inline-panel family (`shazamMode`/`shazamFocus` in App.tsx, in `PANEL_KINDS`). Cross-platform (no macOS gate) — it just needs a mic + network.

- **`shazam.rs` is a faithful Rust port of the reverse-engineered Shazam signature algorithm** (marin-m's work, as shipped in shazamio's pure-Python `algorithm.py`/`signature.py`): 16 kHz mono `i16` → a 2048-point FFT (`rustfft`) every 128 samples over a rotating ring, Hanning-windowed → **peak spreading** (frequency + temporal max-spread into the `-1/-3/-6` slots) → **peak recognition** (a bin is a peak iff it beats its frequency neighbours in `fft[-46]` AND the spread neighbours across `fft[-49]` + the far temporal offsets) → per-band `FrequencyPeak`s → the binary `data:audio/vnd.shazam.sig` signature (48-byte header + magic `0xCAFE2580`/`0x94119C00` + IEEE CRC-32 (hand-rolled) + TLV peak lists). **Only bands 0/1/2 (250–3500 Hz) are filled — faithful to the reference (its 3.5–5.5 kHz branch is dead code).** The port was **cross-validated bit-for-bit against the Python reference** (same input → identical peak count + identical base64 URI) and **verified end-to-end live** (a real song snippet → correct Shazam match).
- **API:** `recognize(&[i16])` builds the signature URI + `samplems`, POSTs to `https://amp.shazam.com/discovery/v5/en/US/iphone/-/tag/<uuid>/<uuid>?…` (iPhone headers, `Accept-Encoding: identity`, two random v4 UUIDs, `timestamp` from `SystemTime`) via **`ureq` with the `tls` feature** (added for HTTPS — hue/ip-api stay plain HTTP; JSON parsed via the existing `serde_json`, not ureq's `json` feature). `parse_response` (pure) pulls title/subtitle/coverart/url/genre + album+year from the SONG section + the Apple-Music hub link. `Ok(None)` = no match.
- **Native mic capture (v0.84.254 — the "playback stutters while recording" fix):** recording is done in **Rust via `cpal`** (`record_mic_16k`), NOT the webview. `getUserMedia`/Web-Audio mic-open makes WKWebView reconfigure the shared CoreAudio device (sample rate/buffer) → other apps' playback stutters; native `cpal` opens only the input device directly, so playback is untouched. `record_mic_16k(seconds, on_progress)` opens the default input (any sample format: F32/I16/U16), averages to mono, and `resample_to_16k_i16` (pure, tested linear resampler) → `Vec<i16>`. The **`shazam_listen(seconds)`** command runs it on `spawn_blocking`, emits `shazam-progress` (0..1) events ~10×/s, then `recognize` + `history_insert`. `ShazamPanel` listens for `shazam-progress` for its bar; the old `lib/mic-record.ts` (Web-Audio ScriptProcessor path) was removed. `ShazamPanel` shows a pulsing "listening" animation (`shazam-ring`/`shazam-bob` keyframes, reduced-motion-guarded) with a countdown, then a searching spinner, then the result card (cover `<img>` — CSP is `null` so external art loads — with Open-in-Shazam / Copy-title / Again; R re-records, Esc exits) or a no-match/permission/error state. The cover image loads directly (external URL) since the app CSP is disabled.
- **Music links (v0.84.251):** `parse_response` also builds `spotify_url` + `youtube_url` (pure `music_links`) — a direct Spotify link from `track.hub.providers[type=SPOTIFY]` if present (usually a `spotify:` app-URI), else `https://open.spotify.com/search/<title artist>`; YouTube is always a `youtube.com/results?search_query=<title artist>` (URL-encoded). Shazam / YouTube open via `tauri-plugin-opener`. **Spotify opens via the `open_spotify` command (v0.96.0)** — the old `openUrl` path was dead for a `spotify:` URI (the opener can't handle that scheme). Pure `spotify_targets(url, title, artist) -> (app_uri, web_url)` converts a specific track between the `spotify:`/`open.spotify.com` forms (query string stripped) and rebuilds a search link cleanly from the metadata; `open_spotify` opens the **app when installed** (`spotify_installed`: macOS app-bundle check) via the native opener, **else the web player**. Both the result card and history rows route through it.
- **Recognition survives the overlay closing (v0.96.0):** `shazam_listen` runs the record + match entirely in the backend, so dismissing/navigating-away mid-listen doesn't interrupt it — it finishes, persists to history, and emits a **`shazam-done`** event `{ matched, error }`. A `SHAZAM_LISTENING` `AtomicBool` guard rejects a concurrent start (no second cpal stream), and `shazam_is_listening` lets a freshly-opened panel **reconnect** (`reconnectedRef` → show live state, apply the result from `shazam-done`) instead of re-recording. `App.tsx` has a global `shazam-done` listener that fires a **status toast** (`kind: "shazam"`, Music icon) when the panel is closed — so a background recognition surfaces visibly.
- **Local history (v0.84.251):** a `shazam_history` table (created in `db::open` via `shazam::init_schema`; `id, recognized_at, title, artist, cover_url, shazam_url, spotify_url, youtube_url, genre, album, released`, indexed by time). `shazam_recognize` (now takes `State<DbHandle>`) persists each match via `history_insert` — which **dedups against the most-recent row** (re-listening to the same song doesn't pile duplicates). IPC: `shazam_history_list(limit)` / `shazam_history_delete(id)` / `shazam_history_clear`. `ShazamPanel` has a header **Listen ⇄ History** toggle; **`shazam history` (v0.84.252)** opens the panel straight into the History view (no mic) — an arg-selected sub-view like `snitch map`, with a discoverable `shazamSubEntry` sub-row (typing `shazam` shows both *Identify song* + *shazam history*), `shazam` added to `keepArg` in `PANEL_KINDS` canonicalisation, and `initialView` passed to the panel. **`viewSignal` (v0.88.3):** `initialView` is consumed once at mount, so with the panel already open a click on the OTHER list row (or the same row after the internal Listen⇄History toggle diverged from `shazamMode`) did nothing — App.tsx bumps `shazamSignal` on every shazam dispatch and the panel applies each click: history row → switch view without touching the mic (an in-flight recognition keeps running, its match still lands in history), identify row → fresh `run()`, or — guarded by `busyRef` — just re-shows the live recognize view instead of starting a second concurrent native recording. the History view lists past recognitions (cover · title · artist · time-ago) each with Shazam/Spotify/YouTube link chips + per-row delete + Clear-all. **v0.87.3:** platform buttons/chips render **brand icons** (inline Simple-Icons SVGs — lucide has no brand set; Shazam #0088FF, Spotify #1DB954, YouTube #FF0000); a **Lyrics** button (result card, `L` key + per-history-row chip) opens an in-app lyrics view fed by `shazam_lyrics` → `shazam::fetch_lyrics` (lrclib.net, keyless/anonymous, pure `parse_lrclib_response` prefers plainLyrics and strips LRC timestamps from syncedLyrics via `strip_synced_timestamps` — both unit-tested; `Ok(None)` = not in catalogue → browser-search fallback button; Esc from lyrics goes back, not out); **Copy lyrics (v0.101.4):** Copy button (+ `C`) copies the visible text (Original verbatim; +DE via `lib/shazam-lyrics.ts::formatBilingualForCopy`); the **history is searchable** (pure `lib/shazam-filter.ts::filterShazamHistory` — all whitespace-terms must match across title/artist/album/genre; load limit 100 → 500).
- **Pure + unit-tested (Rust):** `crc32` (known vector), signature envelope (silence → valid header/CRC/size), a tone → peaks, `parse_response` (+ direct-Spotify preference), `music_links` (search fallback + encoding), `url_encode` (RFC-3986 unreserved set + per-byte UTF-8 percent-encoding), `spotify_targets` (track form-conversion + clean search rebuild), `strip_synced_timestamps` / `parse_lrclib_response` / `parse_google_translate` (lyrics), `history_insert/list/delete/clear` (in-memory DB, dedup). `resample_to_16k_i16`. IPC: `shazam_listen` (native record+recognize, emits `shazam-done`) · `shazam_is_listening` · `open_spotify` · `shazam_recognize` (samples, compat) · `shazam_history_{list,delete,clear}`. Deps: `rustfft` + `cpal` (native mic) + ureq `tls`.

### Live translation — `tr*` commands (`translate.rs`, `lib/translate` wiring in `App.tsx` + `PreviewPanel.tsx`, v0.93.0)

**Full reference incl. the provider comparison: [docs/translation.md](./docs/translation.md).** The `tr*` search-bar commands (`tr`/`tren`/`trde`/`trde2it`/… — data-driven from `TRANSLATE_LANGS`) show the translation **live in the preview while typing**; **Enter copies the translation to the clipboard**, **Shift+Enter opens Google Translate** in the browser; click either language box to copy that side (v0.101.5).

- **Backend (`translate.rs`) is a provider (strategy) pattern over keyless, free endpoints.** A `Provider` trait (`name`/`supports_source`/`translate`) with two impls, tried in order by the pure orchestrator `translate::translate(text, sl, tl)`: **`GoogleGtx`** (the unofficial `translate.googleapis.com/translate_a/single?client=gtx&dt=t` endpoint — same engine + privacy trade-off as the browser command, handles `sl="auto"`) is primary; **`MyMemory`** (`api.mymemory.translated.net/get?langpair=sl|tl`, keyless; `supports_source` returns false for `"auto"`) is the automatic fallback. Per-provider timeout `TIMEOUT = 2500 ms`. Only the text + language pair leave the machine. The response **parsers are pure + unit-tested** (`parse_google` concatenates the `[0]` sentence segments + reads `[2]` detected source; `parse_mymemory` requires `responseStatus == 200` as number *or* string) — 8 tests; the HTTP calls are not tested (need network). The orchestration is split from the concrete providers as `translate_with(providers, …)` so the fallback logic (order, `supports_source` skipping, last-error survival, blank-input rejection) is unit-tested with stub providers rather than the network. `ureq` (blocking) → the IPC `translate_text` runs it on `spawn_blocking`. Deliberately a **fresh focused parser**, not the lyrics-specific `shazam::parse_google_translate` (different response shape).
- **Frontend (App.tsx + PreviewPanel).** `App.tsx` derives `translateReq` (`{sl,tl,text}`) from the parsed command via `isTranslateKind`/`TRANSLATE_LANGS`, keyed by the stable `translateKey = sl|tl|text`. A debounced effect (**350 ms**) calls `translateText`: a **per-session cache** (`translateCacheRef`, `Map` keyed by `translateKey`) returns instantly on a hit; a **request-sequence guard** (`translateSeqRef`) drops stale responses so fast typing always resolves to the latest text; status is `loading | ok | error`. **Enter copies** the translation (`liveTranslation` / cache / on-demand fetch → clipboard); **Shift+Enter opens Google Translate**. `liveTranslation` is passed to `<PreviewPanel>`, whose `TranslatePreview` shows clickable source/target boxes (click copies that side) plus the copy / ⇧Google footer. IPC types: `Translation { text, detected_source, provider }` in `ipc.ts`.

### snitch — network monitor + best-effort per-app blocker (`snitch.rs`, `SnitchPanel.tsx`, `SnitchMapPanel.tsx`, v0.84.246; macOS)

`snitch` opens an inline network monitor; `snitch map` (`/conn`/`show`/`world` in the arg) opens a world-map connections view. Both are macOS-only (`CommandSpec.platform=["mac"]`), inline-panel family (`snitchMode: null|"apps"|"map"` + `snitchFocus` in App.tsx; shows while fully typed since v0.130.0, the arg picking the view live).

- **HONEST-SCOPE (must stay documented):** a *real* per-app outbound firewall like Little Snitch needs a **NetworkExtension system extension** with the `com.apple.developer.networking.networkextension` entitlement — obtainable only via Developer-ID + Apple approval, which this self-signed app can't have. So blocking is **best-effort**, never presented as a hard firewall. pf can't scope by app (all apps share uid 501; `pfctl` parses `user` but can't isolate a process), only by IP.
- **App blocker (`SnitchPanel`):** `snitch_list_apps` runs `lsof -nP -iTCP -sTCP:ESTABLISHED -iUDP` → the pure `parse_lsof` → `apps_from_connections` groups by process COMMAND (blocked-first sort). A per-app toggle writes the blocked set via `snitch_set_blocked` (persisted `snitch.blocked` + a daemon-readable plain file `snitch-blocklist.txt`). **The blocker daemon** (`assets/snitch-blockd.sh`, `include_str!`-embedded, materialised + launched as **root** via one `osascript … with administrator privileges` prompt on the first block — `snitch_arm`) polls every 2 s: `lsof` → the blocked apps' remote IPs → `pfctl -a io.celox.inspector-rust.snitch -t ir_snitch -T replace`. pf setup mirrors the maintainer's `network-lock` tool exactly (anchor file `/etc/pf.anchors/…` + a **backed-up** `/etc/pf.conf`, `pfctl -e`, restored verbatim on teardown) — it NEVER `pfctl -F all`s. **Fail-OPEN:** if the daemon dies, blocks lapse (safe for connectivity). Disarm (`snitch_disarm`) just drops a stop-file — the root daemon self-cleans pf on its next poll (no second prompt). Toggling apps while armed = no prompt (daemon re-reads the file). Caveats surfaced in the UI: first packets of new connections leak before the next poll; blocking a shared server IP affects every app talking to it. `is_armed` probes the pidfile via a one-line `kill(pid,0)` FFI (libc is Linux-only here).
- **Connections map (`SnitchMapPanel`, redesigned v0.84.248):** `snitch_connections` returns the raw `lsof` connections; the frontend extracts unique **public** remote IPs and resolves them via `snitch_geolocate` → `snitch::geolocate` (ip-api.com free `/batch`, ≤100/call, no key; `is_private_ip` filters LAN/loopback/ULA/link-local so private addresses are never sent out; `parse_ipapi_batch` is pure + tested). Locations are cached per-IP in the frontend. The basemap is an **offline dotted world** from `lib/worldmask.ts` — a 360×180 equirectangular land bitmask (≈10.8 KB base64) generated once from public-domain Natural Earth 110m land (point-in-polygon raster). A `<canvas>` rAF loop draws land dots (muted) + a dot per server, with **live activity highlighting**: `snitch_home` (ip-api `/json`, no IP → locates the caller = this machine) gives a "home" origin; `snitch_activity` samples **per-process throughput** (`nettop -P -x -n -l 2`, two ≈1 s samples → the pure-tested `parse_nettop_deltas` byte-delta per PID) and a server is **active** when any of its connections' processes exceeds `ACTIVE_BPS` (2 KB/s, ignoring keepalives). Active servers glow **emerald** with a curved arc from home + **animated packets flowing** along it (quadratic-bezier `bez(u)`, `u` cycling); idle servers are dim accent dots. The server list sorts active-first, active rows tinted emerald with live `humanRate`. Activity is polled every 3 s (separate from the 4 s connection poll); the render loop reads the activity via a ref, the list via a state mirror (`activityMap` — refs can't be read during render). Caveat: throughput is **per-process**, so all of a busy app's servers light up together (per-connection bytes aren't cheaply available). **Zoom/pan (v0.84.249):** scroll zooms toward the cursor, drag pans (1:1), double-click zooms in, +/−/reset controls + keyboard (+/−, arrows, 0); an eased `zoomCur/centerCur → zoomTgt/centerTgt` transform, off-screen land cells are culled per frame (high zoom stays smooth), land-dot radius scales with zoom (capped), server/home dots keep constant screen size, city labels appear past 2.6× (and on hover), and the hover info-box is drawn **on the canvas** (a `hover` ref → zero React re-render while moving the mouse).
- **Two visible sub-commands (v0.84.248):** typing `snitch` surfaces BOTH options as adjacent rows — the primary `command` row (app blocker) + a `snitchSubEntry` `command-suggestion` for **`snitch map`** (and vice-versa when the arg targets the map) — so the map is discoverable without knowing the `map`/`connections`/`show`/`world` keyword. Enter runs the selected view; Tab autocompletes.
- **Pure + unit-tested:** `parse_lsof`, `split_host_port` (v4/v6/wildcard), `apps_from_connections`, `is_private_ip`, `parse_ipapi_batch`, `parse_nettop_deltas`. The pf/daemon plumbing needs root + a live machine (not unit-tested). IPC (all `#[cfg(macos)]` + async): `snitch_list_apps` · `snitch_connections` · `snitch_geolocate` · `snitch_activity` · `snitch_home` · `snitch_set_blocked` · `snitch_is_armed` · `snitch_arm` · `snitch_disarm`. `ureq` (already a dep) does the HTTP.

### Timesheet — time tracking (`tracking/` module, `TimesheetPanel.tsx`, v0.84.77–.85)

Opt-in, **offline**, **encrypted-at-rest** time tracking. Full design + privacy
model + delivery history in **`docs/timesheet.md`**; the browser extension in
**`extension/`**. macOS is verified end-to-end; Windows/Linux active-window+idle
modules are compile-validated but runtime-unverified, so the `track` command is
**macOS-gated** in the UI (`CommandSpec.platform = ["mac"]`).

- **Commands / entry points:** `track on` / `track off` (start/stop a session,
  with a `track` status toast + a footer **REC** LED — green recording, amber
  when idle- or manually paused); bare **`track`** *or* the global
  **`Ctrl+Shift+T`** hotkey (`hotkey.rs`, emits `open-timesheet-tab`) opens the
  **Timesheet tab**; **`Ctrl+Shift+Alt+T`** (rebindable `ActionId::TrackToggle`,
  v0.84.204) toggles tracking from anywhere. **Manual pause/resume (v0.84.204):**
  a header button (+ `track_set_paused` IPC / `Runtime.manual_paused`) pauses
  recording without ending the session — the open interval closes at the pause
  moment, the run loop records nothing until resume, `TrackStatus.manual_paused`
  drives the amber state. **Scope-aware export + cleanup (v0.84.204):** the
  CSV/HTML export buttons export the *visible scope* (day, or Mon–Sun in Week
  mode) and Week mode gains a **Clean up week** button (`track_cleanup_range`
  IPC — same idle/fragment sweep over a whole date range, live event excluded).
- **Tracker core (`tracking/mod.rs`):** `TrackerState` + a focus/idle `run_loop`
  (~1.5 s tick) → the pure-tested `apply_tick(db, rt, sid, Tick{…})` state
  machine. Gap-free, non-overlapping intervals; a focus change closes the open
  one + opens the new; **retroactive idle auto-pause** (input idle past
  `track.idle_seconds` closes the active interval at the real idle-start + marks
  an `idle` span). Browsers (`is_browser`) get the extension's `last_tab`
  host/title and split on tab-URL change. The privacy **denylist** strips
  title/url/host (`is_denied`/`parse_denylist`). `day_report`/`aggregate_day`
  build the tab + export aggregations, including the **per-app breakdown**
  (`DayReport.app_breakdown`: every app grouped, with total time + an expandable
  detail list — browsers → hosts, other apps → window titles, each with time +
  count) — a sub-view of the active time already in `by_app` (not double-counted;
  idle + Claude excluded). The tab's events area has a **By app ↔ Timeline**
  toggle: *By app* shows the expandable grouped breakdown, *Timeline* the
  editable chronological event list (merge/edit/delete). The HTML export renders
  it as a native `<details>` block per app ("By app (detailed)", no-JS). (browser
  history was the v0.84.86 special case; generalised to all apps in v0.84.87.)
- **Survives restarts (v0.84.89):** the open event is heartbeated every tick
  (`db::touch_event` → fresh `ended_at`), so a crash/quit/update leaves it ended
  at the last-alive moment (no phantom offline time). At startup `lib.rs` calls
  `tracking::resume_if_active`, which — if a session wasn't cleanly ended
  (`active_session` returns one) — flips it back to `active`, and re-arms the loop
  + Claude watcher + bridge on the **same** session, so recording continues; the
  offline gap is simply not recorded.
- **No over-count (v0.84.90):** resume FIRST runs `finalize_all_open_events`
  (close every dangling `ended_at IS NULL` event from an unclean shutdown of an
  older build — each would otherwise count to *now* and overlap every later
  event) + `end_stale_sessions` (end leftover duplicate "active" sessions, keep
  only the resumed one). And `aggregate_day`'s `total_active_s`/`total_idle_s` are
  the **union** of intervals (`union_seconds`), not the raw sum — so overlapping
  events can never make "today" exceed real wall-clock. (by_app/category/host
  stay raw per-bucket sums.)
- **Per-OS active window + idle (`tracking/os/`):** macOS — **native fast path
  (v0.84.205): `NSWorkspace.frontmostApplication` (app name/bundle, no
  permission) + AX `AXFocusedWindow`→`AXTitle` (title, needs Accessibility)**;
  with Accessibility granted that's **zero process spawns** (previously one
  osascript spawn per 1.5 s tick ≈ 19 000 per 8-h day). Without Accessibility
  the old osascript path (Automation grant) still supplies the title; if that
  fails too, the native app-level info is returned. Idle via
  `CGEventSourceSecondsSinceLastEventType`. The run loop also **caches the
  idle-threshold + denylist settings** (re-read every ~30 s instead of twice per
  tick). Windows (`GetForegroundWindow` + `QueryFullProcessImageNameW` +
  `GetLastInputInfo`), Linux (X11 `xdotool` + `xprintidle`; Wayland → `None`,
  clean degrade).
- **Persistence (`tracking/db.rs`):** `track_sessions`/`track_events`/
  `track_claude_turns`/`track_categories`; `window_title`+`url` AES-256-GCM via
  `crypto` (TEXT columns); plaintext app/host/category/project. `prune_before`
  (retention), `claude_tokens_by_project`, `merge_events`, `update_event`
  (`EventPatch`, `""`=clear), etc.
- **Claude watcher (`tracking/claude.rs`):** `notify` on
  `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` → per-project `claude` intervals + token turns
  (a **separate** dimension, not in the focus/browser active total).
- **Browser bridge (`tracking/bridge.rs`):** loopback-only (`127.0.0.1`),
  token-authenticated WebSocket (sync `tungstenite`) the MV3 extension reports
  the active tab to. **Token is read live per frame** so `regenerate_token`
  revokes in-flight sockets (fail-closed). `track_export_extension` writes the
  extension to `~/Downloads` (Chrome can't auto-install unpacked; load-unpacked).
  **Battery-drain fix (v0.84.239):** `handle_conn` MUST `set_nonblocking(false)`
  on the accepted stream — on macOS/BSD an `accept()`ed socket **inherits the
  listener's O_NONBLOCK** (Linux clears it), so the 500 ms read timeout never
  engaged: every `ws.read()` returned `WouldBlock` instantly and the per-
  connection loop **spun a full core for as long as the extension was
  connected** (measured live: ~4.6 CPU-hours in one morning, 99.5 % CPU).
  A 10 ms sleep in the WouldBlock arm is the belt-and-braces cap; the
  regression test `accepted_stream_blocks_after_clearing_nonblocking` pins the
  blocking behaviour.
- **Export (`tracking/export.rs`):** flat **CSV** + a **self-contained HTML**
  report (inline-SVG charts, zero external requests, footer
  `© 2026 Martin Pfeffer | celox.io`).
- **UX (v0.84.91–.93):** events **auto-categorize** from saved app→category
  rules (`category_for_app`); the "By app" view assigns a category to a whole app
  (rule + back-fill); Settings → Timesheet manages rules. Timeline supports
  **manual add** (`insert_event`/`manual_session_id`), **clean up** (idle +
  sub-15s fragments, `cleanup_day`), and Enter/Esc in the editor. A **Day ↔ Week**
  toggle: week = per-day bars + category/app/project + productive% over the
  Mon–Sun week (`range_report`); the day adds a **By project** card + Productive%.
- **Integrity fixes (v0.84.203):** (1) **Live-event guard** — the run loop's
  heartbeat writes to `Runtime.open_event_id`, so delete/merge on that id (its
  heartbeat-stamped `ended_at` makes it look closed) or a cleanup during
  idle-pause silently killed all further persistence in the focus span;
  `tracking::live_event_id` + IPC guards reject delete/merge of the live row and
  `cleanup_day` takes an `exclude` id. (2) **Retention on resume + hourly** —
  pruning only ran on `track on`; with keep-alive a session can run for weeks, so
  `prune_by_retention` now also fires in `resume_if_active` + hourly in the run
  loop. (3) **DST-safe `day_bounds`** — upper bound is the *next* local midnight,
  not `from + 24h` (23/25-h transition days double-counted/dropped an hour);
  invariant `day_bounds(d).1 == day_bounds(d+1).0` is unit-tested; frontend
  mirror `dayEndMs` + `timelineBand(.., dayEnd)`. (4) **Editor validation** —
  the inline editor rejects `end <= start` (was silently persisting inverted
  rows); IPC errors now surface in a panel banner instead of console-only.
  (5) **In-place tab enrichment** — the extension's tab report arrives a tick
  after a browser interval opens; `apply_tick` now enriches the young (≤15 s)
  interval via `enrich_event` (whose dead `ended_at IS NULL` guard — always
  false after the first heartbeat — is removed) instead of splitting off an
  "(unknown)" fragment; denied intervals are never enriched
  (`Runtime.open_was_denied`).
- **Consolidated slots (`tracking/slots.rs` + `tracking/bcsbook.rs`, `SlotsView.tsx`, v0.90.0).** The raw event stream is one row per focus change — a real day reached **463 fragments of 1–30 s**, which measures correctly but cannot be booked. The Timesheet tab's third view (**Slots**, now the default) derives the few contiguous blocks a person would actually book. **Derived, never stored:** recomputed per call, every slot keeps its source `event_ids`, and the raw timeline is untouched — deliberately unlike the older "Clean up" button, which DELETEs idle spans + sub-15 s rows and cannot be undone.
  - **Project inference** (`infer_projects`) in order of signal strength: explicit tag → Claude cwd → project-name tokens in title/URL/host (`match_project_in_text`: *all* tokens of the project name must appear, longest name wins, tokens < 3 chars dropped so "de"/"io" can't attach a random tab) → **temporal neighbourhood** (`fill_from_neighbours`: untagged time adjacent to a project block inherits it; an idle span breaks the chain so a project can never bleed across a break). The `Origin` is carried to the UI, so a guessed row is visibly a guess.
  - **Consolidation** (`build_slots`): contiguous same-key runs, gaps ≤ `bridge_gap_s` bridged, **foreign events shorter than `noise_s` absorbed** (the main lever — a 20 s Slack glance must not split a 2 h block), idle ≥ `min_break_s` splits. Unassigned time still consolidates *by app* rather than staying fragmented.
  - **Rounding must not inflate the day** (same invariant as bcsbook's presence module, learned there the expensive way): `snap_slots` rounds each edge to the **nearest** quarter, and the `min_slot_s` gate runs on the **measured** span **before** snapping. Outward rounding turned a measured 7 h into 7.5 h; gating after snapping let a 20-min stretch survive a 30-min floor as a 45-min block. Snapping then pushes rounded neighbours apart so slots never overlap (the target system rejects overlapping bookings). Test fixtures **must** use a 15-min-aligned epoch base — snapping rounds absolute epoch ms.
  - **Handover to bcsbook** (`track_push_bcsbook`): maps `project → shortcut` (`parse_project_map`, `name=shortcut` per line) and posts to bcsbook's local API. **Its `/api/suggestions/save` replaces the whole day**, so the push does GET → `merge_rows` → POST: non-colliding slots are appended, colliding ones are **skipped and reported** rather than overwriting git/presence/manual rows; `replace: true` is the explicit opt-in to wipe the day. Verified live against a running bcsbook (`hours` agrees with the window — 09:00–11:45 → 2.75 h — and the rows render editable in its GUI).
  - Settings live under `track.slot.*` (`get_/set_slot_config`, all clamped). IPC: `track_slots` · `track_slots_range` · `track_bcsbook_preview` · `track_push_bcsbook`.
  - **Private-app filter (v0.91.0)** — `DEFAULT_PRIVATE_APPS` (curated: music/messengers/streaming/social/dating/games + common browser hosts) is seeded once into `track.private_apps` (flag `track.private_apps_seeded_v1`, never overwrites a user list) and, gated by `track.private_filter` (default on), dropped from the Slots view **and** the CSV/HTML export via the pure `strip_private` / `event_matches_patterns` (the same case-insensitive app/host/url matcher `is_denied` now shares). **Non-destructive** — the raw timeline + By-app/Totals keep everything; only the derived slots + exports are filtered, so removing a line ("releasing" an app) applies retroactively. Both live on `SlotConfig` (`private_filter`/`private_apps`), edited in the Slots ⚙ config. Deliberately excludes work-typical apps; password managers stay on the title-strip denylist. `track_export` (the top CSV/HTML buttons) `strip_private`s the events AND computes the consolidated block for the same window. **The export uses `slots::project_totals` (per-project union), NOT the timeline `build_slots` (v0.91.3):** a real day of **parallel Claude sessions** produces thousands of heavily overlapping events (a raw sum reported 40+ h/day for one project) — the chronological timeline-walk splinters on the overlaps and the 15-min floor then drops almost everything, so the export showed only raw events. `project_totals` groups by inferred project and **unions each project's intervals** (`union_seconds`, now `pub(crate)`) → overlap-corrected real time per project, robust to parallel/fragmented days. `export::html` gets a "Consolidated per project" card + `export::csv` a `# Consolidated per project (overlap-corrected)` block (date · project · hours · first · last · apps). `range_project_totals`/`day_project_totals` wrap it (private-filtered). The **Slots view + bcsbook push keep `build_slots`** — they need bookable from–to blocks, which a per-project total doesn't have.
- **IPC:** `track_start/stop/status/get_day/get_range/update_event/delete_event/
  merge_events/set_category/category_rules/delete_category_rule/
  distinct_categories/add_event/cleanup_day/clear_all/export/bridge_info/
  bridge_regenerate/export_extension`, `get_/set_timesheet_config`. Frontend
  chart/format helpers
  in `lib/timesheet.ts` (unit-tested). Settings → **Timesheet** (idle/retention/
  Claude toggle/denylist) + the tab's "Browser extension" disclosure
  (port/token/regenerate/save-extension).

### Cleaning workflow (`cleaner.rs`, v0.60.0)

**Full reference: `docs/cleanup.md`** (category catalogue per level + the safety guarantees). Two v0.84.264 changes are load-bearing for anything you touch here:

**Directory rows + backend-held plan (v0.84.264).** The plan is (and stays) **file-granular** — that's what makes "execute deletes exactly what was planned, re-validated, and nothing that appeared after the scan" true; deletion is never `remove_dir_all`. But a cache/DerivedData scan is 100k+ files, so the plan now stays in the backend (`cleaner::PlanStore`, Tauri state) and only an **aggregated view** crosses IPC: `CleanPlanView { dirs: Vec<CleanDir>, … }`, one row per directory (`aggregate_dirs` rolls each file up into `root + one component`, pure + tested). `cleaner_execute(selected: Vec<String>)` takes just the ticked `CleanDir.path`s, `filter_by_selection` maps them back to items, and the allowlist re-validation is unchanged. Frontend: `CleanPanel` renders directory rows grouped under category headers (Space on a header toggles the whole group); `lib/clean-select.ts` (`buildRows`/`dirsOfCategory`/`selectionTotals`) is the pure selection math. **Background jobs (v0.101.1):** scan/execute keep running after Esc — `PlanStore` tracks `CleanPhase` + `last_view`, emits `clean-done`, and `cleaner_status` lets a reopened panel reconnect (toast when the overlay was closed). **Don't reintroduce a file-list IPC** — it was the reason the old panel could only offer all-or-nothing categories.

**Developer targets (v0.84.264).** Stale `node_modules` / Cargo `target/` (`find_stale_artifacts`, Standard, **opt-in**): the only categories whose allowlist is *derived* — roots are the concrete artifact dirs under the user's **dev roots** (settings `cleaner.dev_roots`, default `~/claude`, `~/cursor`, `~/dev`; `cleaner.stale_days`, default 90). Two gates, both unit-tested: the artifact is only listed when its **manifest sits next to it** (a bare `node_modules` is never touched), and the *project* — newest mtime of manifest / `src` / `.git/HEAD` / the dir itself, deliberately **not** the artifact's own mtime — must be untouched for `stale_days`. These categories set `Category::ignore_age` (the per-file age filter would otherwise spare a fresh dependency install inside a dead project). Also new: `jetbrains_orphans` (a version dir is an orphan when its product isn't installed **or** a newer version dir exists — the newest is always kept; numeric per-segment version compare, so `2023.10` > `2023.2`), `xcode_archives` (Aggressive + opt-in — **they hold the dSYMs**), and three more **command-based** categories alongside `docker` (`is_command_category`): `brew_cleanup`, `pnpm_store`, `simctl_unavailable` — no file roots, previewed via the tool's dry-run, executed via the tool's own reclaim command, only if the binary exists, never with sudo. `categories()` takes the `CleanerConfig` now (the stale roots depend on it). The pure developer-target helpers are unit-tested: `parse_dev_roots` (comma/newline split, blank-drop), `is_command_category`, `parse_jetbrains_dir` (product↔version split at the first digit), `version_key`/`normalise_product`, and `jetbrains_orphans` end-to-end — including the load-bearing `2023.10 > 2023.2` numeric ranking a string sort would get wrong (deleting the newest instead of the oldest).

`clean` (alias `cleanup`) deletes cache/log/temp files — **safety is the whole design** (it deletes user files). Guarantees: (1) **strict hard-coded allowlist** of cache/log/temp roots per OS in `cleaner::categories()` — never documents/Desktop/Pictures; (2) **canonicalise + containment check** (`is_contained`) before every delete, **symlinks never followed** (`symlink_metadata`/`lstat` → skipped, both when walking and deleting) so a symlinked subdir can't escape the allowlist; (3) **dry-run first** — `scan(cfg)` is read-only and returns a `CleanPlan { items, total_bytes, categories }`; nothing is deleted until `execute(cfg, plan)`, which **re-validates** every path against the allowlist again (TOCTOU-resistant); (4) **conservative opt-in levels** `Level { Safe (default), Standard, Aggressive }` — each category has a `level` + `default_enabled` (dev-tool caches are Aggressive **and** opt-in), plus a `min_age_days` mtime filter. **Broad categories + per-category exclusions (v0.84.241 — "clean frees more"):** a `Category` may carry `exclude` subtrees that are neither recursed nor deleted, so a **broad** root can coexist with more specific categories without double-claiming files. New categories: **`other_caches`** — the whole user cache dir (`~/Library/Caches` / `~/.cache`; routinely the largest reclaimable tree, Homebrew/pip/go-build/Playwright/Electron…) at **Standard**, with the browser + own-app roots carved out; **`xcode_caches`** (DerivedData + CoreSimulator caches) and **`trash`** (`~/.Trash`, only items older than the age filter) — both **Aggressive + opt-in**; `dev_caches` additionally covers `.cargo/registry/src` + `.cargo/git` (they dwarf the `.crate` cache); `logs` also sweeps `~/.pm2/logs` + `/Library/Logs` (root-owned files fail per-item, never aborting). **Downloads categories + duplicate finder (v0.84.243):** a `Category` may also carry an `exts` extension filter (pure `ext_matches`); **`installers`** (Standard, all platforms) sweeps old `dmg/pkg/mpkg/iso/xip/msi/appimage` in `~/Downloads` (age filter applies — re-downloadable by definition); **`dupes`** (Standard, all platforms, key `KEY_DUPES`) is **special-cased in `scan`** — `duplicate_items` (pure, tested) groups Downloads files by size then **SHA-256** (only same-size files are ever hashed), always keeps the **oldest** copy (mtime, then path, deterministic) and returns the rest; the age filter deliberately does NOT apply (a byte-identical keeper survives), zero-byte files are ignored, unhashable files drop out of their group, and `append_duplicates` skips paths another category already claimed. Because these touch **user files in Downloads**, the CleanPanel **pre-deselects** them (`PRESELECT_OFF = {installers, dupes, trash}` — offered, never silently included). **Gotcha (field-learned):** sweeping `~/Library/Caches` also empties build-tool binary caches like `ort.pyke.io` (ONNX Runtime) — the tool re-downloads, but cargo **caches the ort-sys build-script output**, so a later `cargo build` fails with "could not find native static library onnxruntime" until `cargo clean -p ort-sys` forces the script (and its download) to re-run. **Developer categories + scan perf (v0.84.244):** **`editor_caches`** (Standard, on — all platforms) sweeps the Electron editors' (VS Code/Cursor/VSCodium) index/GPU/renderer caches, which live under the **app-data dir** (`~/Library/Application Support` / `%APPDATA%` / `~/.config` — invisible to the broad cache category; `editor_cache_roots` lists only regenerable subdirs, never settings/extensions/state); **`docker`** (`KEY_DOCKER`, Standard, all platforms) is **command-based** — no file roots (images/volumes live inside ONE VM disk file; file-level deletion would destroy them): `scan` estimates via `docker system df --format '{{.Type}}|{{.Reclaimable}}'` (pure parsers `parse_docker_size`/`parse_docker_df_build_cache`/`parse_docker_prune_output`, all tested) and pushes a pseudo-item; `execute` strips docker items from the file plan and runs `docker builder prune -f` (only when the category is enabled in cfg); daemon-not-running → the category contributes nothing. `xcode_caches` additionally sweeps `iOS DeviceSupport` (stale per-iOS-version symbols, 5–20 GB) + `XCTestDevices`/`XCPGDevices`; `dev_caches` adds `.m2/repository`, `.gradle/{daemon,wrapper/dists}`, `.rustup/{downloads,tmp}`, `.android/{cache,build-cache}` (+ NuGet on Windows). **Deliberately excluded:** Go's module cache (read-only tree — needs `go clean -modcache`) and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\VisualStudio` (instance dirs mix caches with window layouts). `PRESELECT_OFF` += `docker`. **Scan perf:** the walk no longer canonicalises per directory (symlinked dirs are already lstat-skipped; the canonicalise+containment gate stays at EXECUTE time on every deletion) — a cache-tree scan is now a plain lstat walk; the duplicate finder pre-filters same-size candidates with a **64-KiB prefix hash** (`sha256_prefix`, `PREFIX_HASH_LEN`) and only full-hashes prefix collisions (files ≤ the prefix length skip the second read). Exclusions are enforced at scan **and** re-checked at execute **per category** (`excludes_by_cat` — a specific category legitimately owns paths a broad one carved out; a tampered plan claiming carved territory under the broad key is rejected). **`cleaner_scan`/`cleaner_execute` are `async`** — walking the full cache dir on the main thread would freeze the UI. The pure core (`scan_roots`/`execute_plan`/`is_contained`/`is_excluded`, explicit roots) is exhaustively unit-tested against temp fixtures (containment, symlink-escape rejection, outside-allowlist rejection, age filter, exclusion at scan + per-category at execute, plan→execute consistency, empty no-op) — **no test touches a real user path**. Settings `cleaner.{level,min_age_days,categories}`; IPC `cleaner_scan` / `cleaner_execute` / `cleaner_categories` / `get_cleaner_config` / `set_cleaner_config`. **Frontend — interactive category picker (`CleanPanel.tsx`, v0.84.242; background jobs v0.101.1):** Enter on the `clean` row opens a picker in the **right preview column** (same inline family as brightness/sound/stats/calendar; `cleanMode`/`cleanFocus` in App.tsx, Enter-only — the scan walks the whole cache dir, too heavy for while-typing). The dry-run scan renders as a **checkbox list of categories** (size + file count; the selected row shows its **3 largest files**), keyboard-first: **↑/↓** row · **Space** toggle · **A** all/none · **Enter twice** (arm → red "press Enter again" confirm) deletes only the checked categories · **Esc** disarms, then exits. **Esc mid-scan / mid-delete closes the UI only** — the Rust job keeps running (`PlanStore` phase + `clean-done` event, same pattern as `shazam`); App toasts when the panel is closed (`Scan ready` / `Cleaned`); reopening reconnects via `cleaner_status` or reuses the pending view. Only the selected categories' items are handed to `cleaner_execute` (which re-validates every path anyway); selection math is the pure, unit-tested `lib/clean-select.ts` (`categorySummaries`/`topItems`/`filterPlan`/`selectionTotals`). This replaced the all-or-nothing `window.confirm` flow — no choice, and native confirm is unreliable in the Tauri webview (the TOTP-delete lesson). Ends in the `clean` status toast; Settings → Cleaning has the level picker + age + per-category checkboxes + an Aggressive warning.

### Bruno — German net-pay calculator (`bruno.rs`, `lib/bruno.ts`)

`bruno <€>` in the search bar. The actual tax/social-contributions compute (Steuerjahr 2025) runs in the **frontend** (`lib/bruno.ts`, constants in `TC`) for instant per-keystroke feedback as a `bruno` `ListEntry`; the Rust module only persists per-user defaults (`BrunoDefaults { tax_class, state, children, is_church_member, health_add, kv_type, pkv_monthly, kv_sick_pay, business_type, hebesatz, self_married }`) as individual `bruno.<field>` settings rows. Defaults: single, childless, NRW, TK Zusatzbeitrag 2.45%. IPC `bruno_get_defaults` / `bruno_set_defaults`; Settings → Bruno edits them. Not tax advice — a simplified §32a tariff. **Selbständigen-Modus (`f`-Suffix, v0.86.0):** `bruno 80000f` / `bruno 7000mf` / `bruno 90000-15000f` (Einnahmen − Betriebsausgaben) runs `computeBrunoSelf` (constants in `TCF`): freiwillige GKV (14,0 % ermäßigt / 14,6 % mit Krankengeld + Zusatzbeitrag, geklammert zwischen Mindestbemessungsgrundlage ⅓ × Bezugsgröße und `healthCap`) ODER PKV-Fixbeitrag; volle PV (4,2 % kinderlos / 3,6 % mit Kind, −0,25 %-Punkte je Kind 2–5, Floor 2,6 %); keine RV/AV; Grund- oder Splittingtarif (`self_married`); Gewerbesteuer nur bei `business_type=gewerbe` (Freibetrag 24.500, Messzahl 3,5 % × `hebesatz`, Ertrag auf 100 € abgerundet) mit §35-Anrechnung `min(4×Messbetrag, GewSt, ESt)`. Die Employee-förmigen Kopf-Felder des `BrunoEntryView` spiegeln die Kernzahlen (Row-Rendering + Enter-Paste unverändert); die volle Selbständigen-Aufstellung hängt als `data.self` (`BrunoSelfBreakdownView`) dran — PreviewPanel rendert sie eigenständig, ⇧⏎ nutzt `formatBrunoSelfBreakdown`, die geteilte Annahmen-Zeile baut `brunoSelfAssumptions`. Expenses-Form ohne `f` wird abgelehnt (Angestellte haben keine Betriebsausgaben); USt nur als §19-Hinweis. **Animated result (v0.84.36):** the **Netto / Monat** + **Netto / Jahr** headline rows render through `AnimatedNumber` (the same slot-machine reveal as the calculator — digits roll, settle left→right, re-roll while you type the gross); the other breakdown rows stay static. `BrunoRow` is a module-level component so the animated rows keep a stable identity and don't restart on unrelated re-renders.

### App launcher (`app_launcher.rs`)

Spotlight-like launcher. **macOS:** walks `/Applications`, `~/Applications`, `/System/Applications`, `/System/Applications/Utilities` (top-level `*.app`); Enter does `/usr/bin/open`; icons lazy + bounded-LRU-cached (cap 100) via `sips … *.icns → png` → base64. **Linux (v0.84.0):** scans XDG `.desktop` entries (`$XDG_DATA_HOME`/`~/.local/share`, `$XDG_DATA_DIRS`, Flatpak exports), honouring `Type=Application`/`Hidden`/`NoDisplay`; launches via `gtk-launch <id>` (parsed-`Exec` fallback). **Windows (v0.84.0, runtime-unverified):** scans Start-Menu `*.lnk` (ProgramData + APPDATA, recursive); launches via `cmd /C start`. The `.desktop` parsers (`app_launcher::parse_desktop_entry`/`parse_desktop_exec`) are pure + unit-tested; no icons on Win/Linux. IPC: `list_apps`, `refresh_apps`, `launch_app`, `get_app_icon`.

**Matching (frontend, `App.tsx` `appEntry`, v0.84.169):** the app launcher is **fuzzy** — exact prefix → substring → **first-char-anchored ordered subsequence** (reusing `commands.ts::fuzzyScore`, 3+ chars, best score wins; e.g. `vsc` → "Visual Studio Code"). This mirrors the power-command matching and is **deliberately the opposite of clipboard history**, which is plain **substring** (`useFuzzySearch` — fuzzy was removed so a clip only surfaces when it literally contains the query). Fuzzy is for the *known-name* targets (commands + apps); substring keeps the clip list precise.

### Meme picker (`meme.rs`, `lib/meme.ts`, v0.70.0)

`meme [query]` browses a folder of GIFs/images and copies the selected one on Enter. `meme.rs::scan` **recursively** walks `meme.dir` (setting; default is **home-relative** via `meme::default_meme_dir()` → `~/My Drive/media/memes`, so it resolves per-user on every OS — v0.81.1; was a hard-coded macOS path) collecting image files (gif/png/jpg/jpeg/webp/bmp/apng), each a `MemeEntry { name (stem), category (parent folder), path }`, sorted by category+name. **The directory is configurable in Settings → Meme library** (IPC `get_meme_dir`/`set_meme_dir`; blank resets to default) — needed on Windows where Google Drive in *streaming* mode mounts under a drive letter (e.g. `G:\My Drive\media\memes`), not the home dir. `meme` is a **whole-list override** (like `kill`): `App.tsx` `isMemeMode` fetches the library once (`list_memes`), `lib/meme.ts::matchMemes` fuzzy-ranks it against the arg (name match outranks category; exact > prefix > infix > 3+-char subsequence), and the rows replace `combined`. The selected meme **previews animated** in `PreviewPanel` via `convertFileSrc(path)` in an `<img>` (the asset-protocol scope in each `tauri.conf.json` includes the default meme dir — `$HOME/My Drive/media/memes/**` — on macOS/Windows/Linux; a *custom* folder still lists + copies but the animated preview only renders inside the scoped default). Enter → `copy_meme(path)` → `meme::copy_to_clipboard`, which on **macOS writes an `NSURL` file-URL to `NSPasteboard`** (raw `objc2` msg_send) so pasting into a chat app / Finder keeps the **animation** (not a flattened still); other platforms fall back to `set_image` of the decoded bytes. IPC: `list_memes`, `copy_meme`, `get_meme_dir`, `set_meme_dir`.

**Build flag (with / without memes).** The meme command is gated by the Vite flag **`MEME_ENABLED`** (`import.meta.env.VITE_IR_MEME !== "0"`, default on) in `lib/meme.ts`; the `meme` `CommandSpec` is conditionally spread into `COMMANDS`. A meme-less artifact is built with `pnpm build:{macos,win,linux}:nomeme` (sets `VITE_IR_MEME=0`) — the command never appears and the folder is never scanned. The Rust `meme.rs` stays compiled in both (no startup side-effects; it only runs when invoked), so this is a frontend-only gate — a fully meme-stripped binary would additionally need a cargo feature (not done, to keep the change low-risk).

### Finder selection (`finder_selection.rs`, `frontmost_app.rs`, `osascript_util.rs`)

**`Ctrl+Shift+F`** reads the current Finder selection via `osascript` and shows the files as `finder-file` rows; selected images can be resized (`resize_file`), optimised (`optimize_file`), or cut out. This path needs the **Automation** TCC grant (Privacy → Automation → Finder) — distinct from Accessibility/Screen-Recording. Denial → errno -1743 → sentinel `ERR_AUTOMATION_DENIED` (`"finder.automation_denied"`) → `"finder-automation-needed"` event + amber banner. IPC: `get_finder_selection`, `resize_file`, `optimize_file`, `get_finder_automation_status`, `open_finder_automation_settings`, `force_reset_finder_automation_grant`.
