The command-mode **takeovers** spring in with `.md3-pop-in`: the `2fa` (`TotpOverlay`) and `bpm` (`BpmDetector`) full-shell overlays, and the inline `brightness`/`sound` panels.

**Calculator slot-machine reveal (`AnimatedNumber.tsx`, `lib/scramble.ts`, v0.84.20).** The big calc result in `PreviewPanel` spins its digit characters for ~0.5 s then settles **left→right** (a cascade) with a spring pop + accent flash on lock-in (`.md3-num-lock`). Pure per-frame math in `lib/scramble.ts` (`scrambleFrame(target, progress, rng)` — only `[0-9]` chars spin; signs/decimal/separator/letters stay fixed so `0xff` / `5 km` / dates stay legible; per-digit lock thresholds fan from `LOCK_START`=0.35 for the leftmost to 1.0 for the rightmost), unit-tested. The component runs a rAF loop with a **settle deadline pushed forward on every value change**, so the digits roll while the expression is still being typed and lock 0.5 s after the last keystroke (graceful at any speed, no restart flicker). Reduced-motion / no-digit results render instantly.

**Popup overlay size** (v0.49.0+) — a second Appearance control: a three-way `Small` / `Medium` / `Large` picker that resizes the `popup` window. Presets in `commands::window_size_dimensions` — small `600×430`, medium `700×500` (the historical `tauri.conf.json` default), large `840×600`. Persisted under `appearance.window_size` (`normalise_window_size` whitelist → `"medium"` on anything unknown); IPC `get_window_size_preference` / `set_window_size_preference`. `set_*` resizes the live window (`set_size` on the main thread); `commands::apply_window_size` re-applies the saved preset at startup from `lib.rs` setup. The next `show_and_position` recentres the window with the new dimensions, so no explicit re-centre is needed.

### `getshaky` — hidden Pong easter egg (`components/PongGame.tsx`, `lib/pong.ts`, v0.21.0+)

Typing the exact word **`getshaky`** into the search bar (detected by `commands::isGetShakyTrigger` — case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant) sets `App.tsx`'s `gameMode` state (`"pong" | "snake" | null`) to `"pong"`, which full-screen-takes-over the app-shell with `<PongGame>`. **Deliberately NOT in the `COMMANDS` catalogue** — it must never surface in autocomplete; you have to know the word.

- `lib/pong.ts` — pure, unit-tested game maths: `clamp`, `botMaxSpeed` (ramp-up: 4.5 cap → +0.75 per bot point), `nextBallSpeed` (per-rally speed-up, capped), `paddleBounce` (edge-hit deflection, magnitude-preserving), `serveBall`, `frameScale` (frame-rate independence), `paddleHit` (swept collision).
- `components/PongGame.tsx` — the stateful `<canvas>` + `requestAnimationFrame` loop. Three phases: `intro` (~1.3 s shake transformation — `getshakyShake` / `getshakyTitle` CSS keyframes in `styles.css`), `playing`, `over`. Mutable game state lives in a `useRef` so the 60 fps loop never re-renders React; only score + phase changes do. Player paddle: mouse **and** arrow/W-S, both live. Board colours read live from the theme CSS vars.
- `useKeyboardNav` gained an `enabled` flag — set to `!gameMode` in App.tsx so the popup's nav handler doesn't double-fire Esc / arrows while a game owns the keyboard. **Esc is the only abort** (Space rematches on the game-over screen — not an abort).
- Entirely client-side: no backend, no IPC, no new Rust module.
- **Persistence (`lib/game-storage.ts`)** — every game persists a high score and a *suspended run* in `localStorage` under a per-game key (`pong` · `snake-classic` · `snake-wrap` · `space`). Pressing **Esc mid-game writes the full game state**; the next launch loads it (via a `useState` initializer), skips the intro, and **resumes exactly where it left off**. Ending a game (or Esc on the over/intro screen) clears the suspended run and finalises the high score. Pong has no per-match score, so its persisted stat is **career wins**; the two Snake variants keep **separate** high scores + separate suspended runs. All best-effort — a throwing/full `localStorage` degrades to "no saved data".

### `rockthebox` — hidden Snake easter egg (`components/SnakeGame.tsx`, `lib/snake.ts`, v0.24.0+)

The second hidden game, same shape as `getshaky`. `commands::rockTheBoxMode` detects the trigger word and returns the variant: **`rockthebox`** → `"classic"` (walls kill), **`rockthabox`** → `"wrap"` (the snake reappears on the opposite edge). `App.tsx` maps these to `gameMode` `"snake-classic"` / `"snake-wrap"`, replacing the app-shell with `<SnakeGame wrap={…}>`. Also **not** in `COMMANDS`.

- `lib/snake.ts` — pure, unit-tested grid logic: `step` (move / eat-grow / self collision with the tail-follow nuance; an optional `wrap` arg toggles wall-death vs. modulo-wrap), `spawnFood` (uniform free-cell pick), `tickInterval` (score-driven speed ramp, capped), `initialSnake`, `dirDelta`, `isOpposite`. Grid is `GRID_COLS × GRID_ROWS`.
- `components/SnakeGame.tsx` — the stateful `<canvas>` loop. Three phases: `intro` (~1.9 s box-assembling flourish — `rockTheBoxRock` / `rockTheBoxTitle` CSS keyframes; `INTRO_MS` must match the keyframe durations), `playing`, `over`. The game advances on a **fixed-timestep wall-clock accumulator** (frame-rate independent). Steered by arrow keys **and** WASD; a buffered `pendingDir` is reversal-checked so the snake can't whip into its own neck. Board colours read live from the theme CSS vars. **Each variant keeps its own high score + suspended run** (`snake-classic` / `snake-wrap`) — see the persistence note under `getshaky` and `lib/game-storage.ts`.
- Entirely client-side: no backend, no IPC, no new Rust module (persistence is `localStorage` via `lib/game-storage.ts`).

### `opener` — hidden German pickup-line easter egg (`lib/openers.ts`, v0.26.0+)

The third hidden trigger, same shape as `getshaky` / `rockthebox`. Typing **`opener`** in the popup search bar surfaces a random German pickup-line at the top of the list; Enter pastes it via the existing `pasteText` IPC. Detected by `commands::isOpenerTrigger` (`/^opener\b/i` against the trimmed query) — anchored to a word boundary so `opener foo` triggers (and re-rolls on every keystroke) while `openers` / `bopener` do not. Also **not** in `COMMANDS`.

- `lib/openers-data.ts` — auto-generated from the maintainer's `nicetobenice_db` PostgreSQL DB on the VPS via `ssh root@69.62.121.168 "sudo -u postgres psql -d nicetobenice_db ..."`. The export query LEFT-JOIN-LATERALs per-user rating + favourite state onto every approved opener, then `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY user-priority DESC, my_rating DESC, avg_rating DESC, id ASC) <= 100` — guarantees 100 entries even if the user has fewer than 100 marked (the remaining slots are filled with the highest global `avg_rating` rows). Output piped through `json_agg(text ORDER BY ord)` and embedded as a `ReadonlyArray<string>`. Re-run the same SQL to refresh.
- `lib/openers.ts` — pure picker. `hashString` (FNV-1a-variant) returns an unsigned 32-bit integer; `pickOpener(seed)` returns `TOP_OPENERS[hash % length]`. Deterministic per seed, so the React render loop doesn't flicker between picks while the query is unchanged.
- App.tsx wires an `openerEntry: ListEntry | null` (kind `"opener"`, `data.text` + `data.dir`) into the top of `combined` when the trigger matches; the seed is the full query, so each keystroke re-rolls. Selecting the opener row lets **← / →** cycle the pick (`openerIndex`); the switch direction is tracked in `openerDir` and carried on the entry. The activate-handler pastes via `pasteText`. `HistoryItem` renders it with a `Sparkles` icon + italic body + an "opener" chip; `PreviewPanel` shows the full text with a "type any key to re-roll" hint. **Animated switch (v0.84.36):** the preview keys the text div on its value and replays a directional slide+fade (`md3-opener-in-right` / `md3-opener-in-left`, picked from `data.dir`; `prefers-reduced-motion`-aware) so each ← / → reads as a small carousel.
- Entirely client-side at runtime — no live DB call, no IPC, no Rust module.

### `spacer` — hidden Space Invaders easter egg (`components/SpaceInvadersGame.tsx`, `lib/space-invaders.ts`)

Typing the exact word **`spacer`** (`commands::isSpaceInvadersTrigger`) sets `gameMode` to `"space"` and replaces the app-shell with `<SpaceInvadersGame>`. Not in `COMMANDS`. Arrow/A-D to move, Space/W/↑ to fire, Esc to quit (Space rematches on game-over). (The trigger word was `space` before it was removed in v0.73.0 and re-added as `spacer` in v0.74.0 — `space` collided with the literal idea of "space".)

- `lib/space-invaders.ts` — formation movement, bullets, collision, scoring (row bonuses).
- `components/SpaceInvadersGame.tsx` — canvas loop; intro uses `space-invaders-descend` / `space-invaders-title` in `styles.css` (`INTRO_MS` = 1400) showing the title **SPACER**. Persists best score + a suspended run (key `spacer`) via `lib/game-storage.ts` — Esc resumes; see the persistence note under `getshaky`. **Visual polish (v0.84.68):** classic pixel-art invader sprites (squid/crab/octopus, 2 animation frames each, defined as bitmaps) **pre-rendered once** to offscreen canvases with the glow **baked in** (so the per-frame loop is cheap `drawImage` — no runtime `shadowBlur` over 55 aliens); colours derive from the theme (`parseColor`/`mixRgb`/`rgba` from `lib/bpm-visual`) — accent-bright at the top row → cooler toward the bottom; a player cannon sprite with a flickering additive engine flame; glowing capsule laser bolts (player accent, enemy danger-red); a parallax twinkling **starfield**; **explosion particle bursts** on each hit + a player-hit screen flash; a vignette; DPR-aware crisp canvas. Lives render as ▲ ship glyphs in the header. All effects read theme CSS vars so they track the active palette.

### `learningtofly` — hidden Flappy Bird easter egg (`components/FlappyGame.tsx`, `lib/flappy.ts`, v0.69.0+)

Typing the exact word **`learningtofly`** (`commands::isFlappyTrigger`) sets `gameMode` to `"flappy"` and replaces the app-shell with `<FlappyGame>`. Not in `COMMANDS`. **Space / ↑ / W / click (or tap)** flap, **Esc** quits (Space/click rematches on game-over). **Flap fires only on a *fresh* press (v0.84.70)** — OS key auto-repeat (holding Space) is ignored (`!e.repeat`) so the bird no longer climbs continuously. **AI autopilot easter egg:** if the player is *holding* the flap key the moment the bird hits the **ground**, the AI takes over — the bird becomes invincible and flies forever (`🤖 Autopilot` banner). Flying into the **ceiling** now ends the run (it used to clamp).

- `lib/flappy.ts` — pure, unit-tested physics faithful to the original: fixed-x bird with vertical velocity only; `GRAVITY` per-frame accel, `FLAP_VY` upward impulse, `MAX_FALL_VY` terminal cap; pipe pairs (`PIPE_GAP`) scroll left at `PIPE_SPEED` and spawn at `PIPE_SPACING` with a randomised `gapTop`; **+1 per pipe passed**; death on pipe (circle-vs-rect `hitsPipe`), ground (`hitsGround`), or — as of v0.84.70 — the **ceiling**. `step(state, fieldW, fieldH, dt, nextGapTop, invincible=false)` is a pure mutate-and-return; with `invincible` (the AI autopilot) the bird clamps off the ceiling/ground and passes through pipes instead of dying. The pure AI controller is `aiTargetY` (next-gap-centre, else mid-field) + `aiShouldFlap` (flap when sunk past the target and not already rising) — the component flaps on a cadence and runs the invincible step. Physics is frozen until the first flap (`started`) and frame-rate-independent via `frameScale`; the caller supplies a fresh `randGapTop` each call so it stays deterministic for tests.
- `components/FlappyGame.tsx` — canvas loop; intro uses `flappy-rise` / `flappy-title` in `styles.css` (`INTRO_MS` = 1500). The bird tilts with its velocity and bobs while idle. Persists best score + a suspended run (key `flappy`, the whole `FlappyState`) via `lib/game-storage.ts` — Esc mid-flight resumes; see the persistence note under `getshaky`. The idle-hint visibility mirrors `game.started` into React state (`flying`) so render never reads the ref.

### Image tools (`recolor.rs`, `cutout_ml.rs`)

Two image actions surface in the preview pane:

- **Recolor** (`recolor.rs`) — `image::load_from_memory` → for each RGBA pixel, replace RGB with `lerp(target, white, luminance)` (alpha untouched) → re-encode → `db::upsert_clip` as a new history row. Eligibility gate: `image_chromaticity` samples up to 4096 opaque pixels (`max((max-min)/max)`). Toolbar only shown when chromaticity < 0.12 (logos / silhouettes).
- **Cut-out background** (`cutout_ml.rs`) — runs U2Netp via the `ort` crate (ONNX Runtime). Decode → resize to 320×320 → ImageNet-normalise (mean `[0.485, 0.456, 0.406]`, std `[0.229, 0.224, 0.225]`) → inference → resize mask back → apply as alpha on the original RGB → encode PNG. Output to `~/Downloads/<name>-cutout-<ts>.png`. Triggered by button in PreviewPanel or `Cmd/Ctrl+B`. Works on real photos (subject/background colour overlap is no longer fatal).
  - Model file: `core/rust-lib/models/u2netp.onnx` (~4.5 MB, embedded via `include_bytes!`).
  - ONNX Runtime is statically linked via `ort`'s `download-binaries` feature → release binary ~40 MB.
  - Session is held in `OnceLock<Mutex<Session>>` so the first cutout pays the model-load cost (~150 ms) once and subsequent calls reuse it.
- **Cutout source variants** — IPC has both `cut_out_image_entry(id)` (clipboard image rows) and `cut_out_image_file(path)` (single-file Files entries pointing at PNG/JPG/WebP/GIF/BMP). Same `cutout_ml::cut_out_subject` underneath via `commands::write_cutout`.
- The legacy chroma-key (`cutout.rs`) stays in the tree under `#![allow(dead_code)]` as a future fast-path for true-uniform-background images.

Both modules share the 16 MP hard cap and the multi-format `image` 0.25 dependency (PNG / JPEG / WebP / GIF / BMP).

### Copy shapes + lineage rails (`lib/lineage.ts`, `clipboard_watcher::preview_text`, v0.93.1)

**Full reference: [docs/clipboard-shapes.md](./docs/clipboard-shapes.md).** Three rules govern what a copy does to the history, and one visual makes them legible:

- **A rich copy keeps its source text.** A rich copy puts *two* flavours on the clipboard (the styled one + the source text). The Html/Rtf capture branches used to derive `content_text` with `strip_html`/`strip_rtf`, and **`strip_html` collapses every newline into a space** — so a copied Markdown document was stored (and, since `paste.plain_text_only` defaults to true, *pasted*) as one mangled line: the "md is displayed as html" report. `preview_text(plain, || fallback)` (pure, unit-tested, lazy fallback) now prefers the pasteboard's own text flavour, so the Markdown source survives verbatim; the stripper remains the fallback for a rich-without-text clipboard.
- **Enter = use it → back to the top.** `paste_entry`/`paste_entry_formatted` already `db::touch`; they now also emit `clipboard-changed` so the list reorders immediately instead of only on the next popup open.
- **Copying in another shape = a NEW entry, original untouched.** The transforms (`Cmd/Ctrl+1…9`) and the preview's plain-text copy go through `commit_transformed_text(text, source_id, kind)` → `db::upsert_clip_derived`: the copy lands at the top, the source keeps its content **and its position** (deliberately not touched). Re-running the same transform is idempotent (hash dedup bumps the existing copy).
- **Discoverability (v0.93.2).** `TransformBar` renders its chips only while `useModifierHeld` is true (Cmd on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere) — previously it returned `null` otherwise, so the feature was invisible until you knew about it. It now renders a `Hold ⌘ for formatting options` hint in that slot; clicking it sets a local `pinned` flag that keeps the chips open for mouse users (× in the header collapses). The bar is only mounted for text/html/rtf clips, so the hint never shows on images/files.
- **The rails make it visible.** `lib/lineage.ts::computeLineage(rows)` is pure + unit-tested (15 tests): union-find over the `derived_from` edges groups clips into *families*, each family spans `min..max` of its member positions (the source can be **above** its copy — pasting the original bumps it back to the top, so nothing may assume an order), and a greedy allocator gives overlapping families their own lane; non-overlapping families reuse lanes/colours, like a commit graph. Colours come from `LINEAGE_COLORS` **by lane**, so adjacent lineages can never collide. Rows are passed positionally with **`null` for non-clip rows** (commands/snippets) so lane spans match the rendered list. `HistoryList` computes rails over the whole list (not the virtual window) + one uniform `railGutter`, so rows never jitter while scrolling; `HistoryItem`'s `LineageRails` draws an absolutely-positioned line per lane + a node dot where the row is a member. Off via settings `history.lineage_highlight` (IPC `get_/set_lineage_highlight`, Settings → Appearance); App.tsx re-reads it whenever the History tab comes back into view (no event plumbing). A derived clip whose source is gone still shows a lone node.

### Clipboard capture priority

`clipboard_watcher::capture` checks formats in this order: **image → files → html → rtf → text**. Image-before-files matters on macOS, where copying a PNG/JPG/HEIC from Finder puts both the bitmap and the file path on the pasteboard — capturing as Files first meant the user only saw paths in history.

**Clipboard privacy (v0.76.0).** Two opt-in settings, gated in `Handler::store`: **app exclusion** (`clipboard.exclude_apps`) — when the frontmost app at copy time matches the comma/newline list (case-insensitive substring via the pure, unit-tested `clipboard_watcher::is_excluded_app`), the clip is dropped silently so password-manager secrets never reach history (the slow `frontmost_app::name()` lookup only runs when the list is non-empty); and **auto-clear** (`clipboard.auto_clear_seconds`, 0 = off) — after a capture, a worker sleeps N s and wipes the system clipboard *unless a newer copy superseded it* (a per-`Handler` `clear_gen: AtomicU64` generation guard; clamped to ≤3600 s). IPC `get_clipboard_privacy` / `set_clipboard_privacy`; Settings → **Clipboard privacy** (exclude-apps textarea + auto-clear seconds).

### `UiState` and modal focus

`UiState.suppress_hide` (AtomicBool, Tauri state) prevents the popup's "hide on focus-loss" handler (`lib.rs` `WindowEvent::Focused(false)` → `hotkey::hide_popup`) from firing while a native file dialog is open. **`UiState.close_on_blur` (v0.87.4)** is the PERSISTED sibling (settings key `popup.close_on_blur`, default true — the historical behaviour): `false` makes the `Focused(false)` handler return before EVERY auto-hide path (macOS immediate + Windows settle), so click-outside keeps the popup open — Esc and the toggle hotkey remain the ways to dismiss (an open inline panel still consumes the first Esc as back). Seeded from settings in `lib.rs` setup, toggled live via IPC `get_/set_popup_close_on_blur`; UI = `PopupBehaviorSection`, the TOPMOST Settings section (deep-link id `behavior`, `settings overlay`). The frontend toggles it via `set_suppress_hide` before/after calling `tauri-plugin-dialog` commands (`dialog:allow-open`, `dialog:allow-save`).

**Windows: confirm-after-settle (v0.84.131).** The fixed-grace + instant-PID-snapshot scheme below kept losing the race — the WebView2 bounce fires `Focused(false)` 700–900 ms post-show (past any fixed grace), and a single `GetForegroundWindow` snapshot can hit the instant the foreground is momentarily not ours → the popup "opens then closes by itself". The durable fix **stops deciding synchronously on Windows**: a `Focused(false)` (once the popup has been focused at least once — `popup_was_focused`) schedules `hotkey::schedule_settle_hide`, which re-evaluates ~250 ms later (`SETTLE_MS`) on the main thread via the pure `settle_should_hide(captured_gen, current_gen, suppressed, foreground_is_ours, visible)`. A monotonic `SHOW_GEN` (bumped by `mark_shown` + `mark_popup_focused`) is captured at the bounce and compared at settle-time — so any intervening re-show or focus-regain (a *resolving* bounce) cancels the hide, regardless of when the bounce arrives. A real click-away keeps a foreign window foreground past the settle → hide proceeds. A `Focused(false)` **before** the first `Focused(true)` is the `SetForegroundWindow`-failed show-race → ignored. **Foreground hardening (v0.84.225 — the actual fix for "after a while, opening closes it immediately"):** `show_and_position` on Windows calls `force_foreground`. The v0.84.201 attempt (zero `SPI_SETFOREGROUNDLOCKTIMEOUT` around the activation) was **circular** — per the `SystemParametersInfo` docs that call only succeeds for a process *already allowed* to change the foreground window, so exactly when needed it silently no-oped; worse, `AttachThreadInput` often activated the popup for a *moment* before the OS snapped foreground back (→ `Focused(true)`, `Focused(false)`, settle sees a foreign foreground, hides ~250 ms after the popup appeared — the observed 200 ms flash). `force_foreground` now (1) synthesizes a benign **ALT press+release via `SendInput`** first (the PowerToys-Run/Flow-Launcher technique: our process becomes "the process that received the last input event", a documented `SetForegroundWindow`-allow condition, and an ALT press additionally unlocks foreground changes), then (2) `AttachThreadInput` + `SetForegroundWindow` + `BringWindowToTop`, verifies via `GetForegroundWindow` and logs a miss. **Behavioural safety net:** the settle-confirm's pure `settle_verdict` (replaces `settle_should_hide`; exhaustively unit-tested) never hides a popup summoned < 2 s ago (`SHOW_INTENT`) — a hide-verdict inside that window means snapped-back activation, so it **retries `force_foreground` once** (`SETTLE_RETRIED`, reset per show) and after a second failure **leaves the popup visible** (unfocused) instead of closing it. macOS/Linux keep the immediate-hide path below (no focus bounce there). Isolated `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` validated the FFI.

**Post-show grace window (v0.84.58; hardened v0.84.113; process-guard v0.84.114; macOS/Linux path only as of v0.84.131).** The same focus-loss handler uses a layered strategy to distinguish genuine dismissals from spurious events. **PRIMARY guard — process ownership check (Windows, v0.84.114):** Before any time-based check, the `Focused(false)` handler calls `hotkey::foreground_belongs_to_our_process()` (`GetForegroundWindow` + `GetWindowThreadProcessId` + `GetCurrentProcessId`). WebView2 routes focus between its internal child HWNDs and the popup's host HWND while the popup is still the active foreground app — those bounces generate `Focused(false)` events that arrive *after* the 600 ms grace has expired (typically 700–900 ms post-open, after `always_on_top` windows such as the status toast have perturbed the foreground z-order). If the OS foreground window still belongs to our process, the event is an internal WebView2/compositor transfer and is suppressed. A genuine user dismiss (click-away, Alt+Tab) always brings a window from a *different* process to the front, so that case falls through to the secondary guards. **SECONDARY guard — time-based grace:** `within_show_grace()` — a 3 s safety net (`SHOW_GRACE_NOFOCUS`) when `Focused(true)` has never arrived (covers `SetForegroundWindow` silent failure), and a 600 ms short-flicker window (`SHOW_GRACE`) once focus is confirmed. **Positioning (v0.84.113):** `clamp_into_monitor()` is called on the **hidden** window before `show()`, so `SetWindowPos` never fires on a focused visible window. macOS is unaffected (Accessory-app focus model does not produce spurious events). Pure core `hotkey::is_within_grace(last, now, grace)` and `hotkey::foreground_belongs_to_our_process` are the key building blocks; `is_within_grace` is unit-tested.

### Platform-specific behaviour in shared code

- **Paste shortcut** (`paste.rs`): `Key::Meta` (Cmd+V) on macOS; `Key::Control` (Ctrl+V) elsewhere.
- **Focus-settle delay** (`paste.rs`): 120 ms on macOS, 50 ms on Windows/Linux.
- **Word-select modifier** (`expander.rs`): `Key::Alt` (Option) on macOS; `Key::Control` elsewhere.
- **Accessibility check** (`expander.rs`): `AXIsProcessTrusted()` via direct CoreFoundation FFI on macOS; always `true` on other platforms.
- **Dock visibility** (`lib.rs`): `set_activation_policy(Accessory)` on macOS.
- **Autostart tray label** (`lib.rs`): `cfg!(target_os = "windows")` → "Start with Windows", else "Start at Login". As of v0.14.0 it's a `CheckMenuItem` reflecting the current state (probed from `app.autolaunch().is_enabled()` on tray build); toggling updates the check + emits `autostart-changed`. IPC: `get_autostart_enabled` / `set_autostart_enabled`. Settings → Startup mirrors the toggle.
- **OCR engine** (`ocr.rs`): macOS Vision (`objc2` FFI); Windows WinRT `Windows.Media.Ocr`; Linux the `tesseract` CLI (`apt install tesseract-ocr` + language packs).
- **Region capture** (`region_picker.rs`): macOS `screencapture -i`; Windows a GDI overlay; Linux `grim`+`slurp` on Wayland, else `scrot -s` on X11.
- **Global-shortcut registration** (`lib.rs`): non-fatal since v0.25.0 — a failure (common on GNOME/Wayland) logs a warning instead of aborting startup; the tray menu and CLI flags still work.

### Linux notes (v0.25.0+)

The Linux port mirrors `win/` and `macos/` — `linux/src-tauri/` is a thin 2-line shell, all logic stays in `core/`. Two Linux-specific concerns drove new code:

- **`cli_dispatch.rs`** — Tauri global shortcuts often don't receive key events under GNOME + Wayland. The module parses CLI flags (`--toggle-popup` / `--open`, `--ocr`, `--screenshot`, `--pick-color`, `--help`) and dispatches them — the same actions as the tray menu (the tray handlers were refactored to call `cli_dispatch::dispatch`). `tauri-plugin-single-instance` routes a second `inspector-rust --ocr` invocation to the already-running instance, so the flags can be bound as desktop "custom shortcuts". **The single-instance callback runs on a tokio worker thread** — the TogglePopup arm hops to the main thread via `run_on_main_thread` (v0.88.2), because `toggle_popup`'s native `NSWindow setFrame` positioning is main-thread-only on macOS and SIGTRAPped in AppKit when called from the callback (two field crashes 2026-07-19; the tray call site is already on the main thread, where the hop is a direct call). The OCR/screenshot/eyedropper arms spawn their own worker threads by design.
- **`desktop_shortcuts.rs`** (Linux-only `#[cfg]` module) — on first start under GNOME/Cinnamon Wayland it auto-registers `gsettings` custom keybindings (`Ctrl+Space` popup + `Ctrl+Shift+O/S/C` → `inspector-rust --…`). Desktop env is detected via `XDG_SESSION_TYPE` / `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP`. The install is recorded under settings key `linux.desktop_shortcuts_profile`; clear it to re-apply. KDE is detected but not yet automated.
- `scripts/install-linux.sh` provisions apt deps + Node + Rust; `scripts/install-desktop-shortcuts.sh` and `scripts/ubuntu-terminal-copy-paste-ctrl-cv.sh` are standalone helpers. Build prerequisites + the per-feature support matrix live in `linux/README.md`.
- Not on Linux yet: the in-app eyedropper and the in-place AX/UIA text expander (the clipboard-paste expander fallback is used instead).

### macOS notes

`macos/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` requires `tauri = { features = ["macos-private-api"] }` for transparent windows. `enigo`'s `CGEventPost` (paste/expander) is gated by the TCC **Accessibility** permission (System Settings → Privacy → Accessibility), *not* an entitlement — the first paste or expander use triggers the prompt; after granting, a relaunch is required (macOS caches `AXIsProcessTrusted` per process) and the Settings panel offers a one-click relaunch. The Finder-selection + Markdown→PDF features (v0.46–0.47) additionally need the **Automation** TCC grant for Finder; `com.apple.security.automation.apple-events` + `NSAppleEventsUsageDescription` are injected into the bundle post-build by `scripts/install-macos.sh`. Three independent TCC surfaces are therefore in play and each has its own status/force-reset IPC: Accessibility (paste, expander, input-lock), Screen Recording (`screen_recording.rs` — OCR, screenshot, NOT the eyedropper), and Automation→Finder (`finder_selection.rs`).

`scripts/grant-permissions-macos.sh` streamlines the one-time grant: it `tccutil reset`s stale entries, relaunches the app, and opens each Privacy pane via `x-apple.systempreferences:` deep links, then prints a checklist. It is a **guided** helper, not an auto-granter — macOS TCC forbids any app/script from flipping those toggles (only the user can; the alternatives are an MDM PPPC profile or SIP-off TCC.db editing, neither of which the script does). The stable self-signed cert means it's needed only once.

### Logging & crash capture (`logging.rs`, v0.84.9)

Bundled builds have no terminal, so the previous stderr-only `tracing` output vanished — making field hangs/crashes undiagnosable. `logging::init()` (called first thing in `lib.rs::run`) installs a `tracing_subscriber` registry writing to **both** stderr and a **daily-rolling file** via `tracing-appender`: `<data dir>/InspectorRust/logs/inspector-rust.log.<date>` (macOS `~/Library/Application Support/InspectorRust/logs/`). Level defaults to `info`, overridable with `RUST_LOG`. It returns a non-blocking `WorkerGuard` that **must stay alive for the process** (`run` binds it to `_log_guard`, which lives until the blocking `.run(...)` returns). `logging::install_panic_hook()` records panics — thread + source location — to the rolling log **and** a dedicated `crash.log` written **synchronously** (so the trace survives an immediate abort the async writer would miss), then chains to the previous hook. Key interactions log breadcrumbs (e.g. `hotkey::toggle_popup` show/hide). Diagnosing a hang: `sample <pid>` on the live process prints the main-thread stack (this is how the v0.84.10 global-shortcut re-entrancy deadlock was pinpointed).

### Backup

`backup.rs` serialises history + snippets (+ **snippet groups**) + notes + TOTP entries + settings — and, opt-in, the timesheet — into a single versioned JSON document (`CURRENT_VERSION = 3`). Import merges: snippets upsert by abbreviation, history upserts by hash (dedup), notes append verbatim (no dedup key → re-import creates duplicates), TOTP entries upsert by (issuer, account) with re-encryption on import, settings upsert by key. Importing a higher version is rejected. v1/v2 backups import cleanly (missing fields default to empty via `#[serde(default)]`).

**Clip lineage in the backup (v0.93.3).** `derived_from` is a foreign key and ids are reassigned on import, so `apply` collects an old→new id map while importing the history and re-points each rail in a second pass via `db::set_lineage_if_absent` (fills only an empty lineage — a backup states a fact about that very clip, unlike the live coincidence case). A rail whose source isn't in the backup is dropped rather than aimed at whatever now owns that id. Both covered by tests.

**Snippet groups in the backup (v0.84.259 — additive, NO version bump).** When `include_snippets`, the export adds a `snippet_categories: Vec<SnippetCategoryBackup>` (`{ name, sort_order }`) and each snippet carries its group **NAME** (`Snippet.category`, `#[serde(default)]`). Both fields are `#[serde(default)]`, so the format stays compatible — a groups-less backup from an older build still imports, and a groups-carrying backup imports into an older build (it just ignores the extra fields), which is why this needed **no** version bump. Import (`apply`) is name-based + portable across machines: step 0 recreates each backup group by name (`create_category`, idempotent); step 1 resolves each snippet's `category` name → id via `category_id_for_name` (get-or-create) and passes it to `upsert_by_abbreviation`. A snippet with no `category` re-imports as ungrouped without clearing an existing group. Covered by `snippet_categories_round_trip_by_name`. Works identically for a snippets-only export and a full-settings export.

**Snippet exchange — export + drag-and-drop import (v0.84.263).** The Snippets tab can **export all snippets + their groups** to a file (⬇ button → save dialog → `export_snippets_to_file` → `backup::export_snippets_json`): a normal backup document with **only** the snippet sections filled (`version: 2`, everything else empty — which the importer treats as "don't touch"), so it's a safe, self-contained exchange format for editing snippets in another app. Import is **format-agnostic and snippets-only**: `backup::import_snippets_json` accepts a **full app backup** (applies *only* its snippets + groups — history/notes/2FA/settings in the file are ignored, so dropping a full backup here can't restore an app), a snippets-only backup, or the lean `[{abbreviation,title?,body}]` / `{"snippets":[…]}` shape (no groups); an **encrypted** backup is rejected with a pointer to Settings → Backup & restore. `import_snippets` + `import_snippets_from_file` both route through it, so the file picker and the drop target behave identically — and the lean import is now group-aware too. The snippet-apply half of `backup::apply` was extracted to `apply_snippets` and is shared by both paths (one place resolves groups by name). **Drag-and-drop:** `SnippetsPanel` registers a window `onDragDropEvent` (same pattern as `TotpOverlay`) — dropping a JSON file anywhere on the tab imports it, with a "Drop to import" overlay while dragging. It **pins the popup open** for the drag (`setSuppressHide(true)` on enter, cleared on drop/leave/unmount) because dragging from Finder steals focus and the popup hides on focus-loss.

**Group assignment is three-valued — `CategoryAssign` (v0.84.262).** `upsert_by_abbreviation` takes a `snippets::CategoryAssign { Keep, Clear, Set(id) }`, not an `Option<i64>`, because the two "no group" cases must behave differently: a re-import that simply doesn't *mention* groups must **not** wipe the user's grouping (`Keep` — the historical `None`-preserves-current rule, what a lean `[{abbreviation,title,body}]` import does), but an **external editor round-trip needs a way to say "this snippet is ungrouped now"** (`Clear`). The wire sentinel for `Clear` is an **empty-string `category`** in the backup JSON (`"category": ""`); `null`/absent stays `Keep`. Export never emits `""` (an ungrouped snippet serialises as `null`), so this is an import-only affordance — but it makes a third-party editor (the `cue` snippet workbench) able to express every grouping change losslessly. `From<Option<i64>>` keeps the old call sites (`import_from_json_into`, seed) on `Set`/`Keep`. Covered by `upsert_category_keep_clear_set`, `empty_category_string_ungroups_a_snippet`, `missing_category_field_preserves_grouping`.

**Snippet versioning — cue-compatible content revision (v0.84.267).** Every snippet carries `version: i64` (schema `version INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`; lazy `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` migration back-fills existing rows to 1). It bumps by 1 **only on a content change** (abbreviation / title / body) — `update` reads the current row and compares before writing, so a group-only edit or a genuine no-op save leaves it untouched; organisational changes (group assign/reorder/rename/delete) never touch it. `create` starts at 1. The number rides through the backup by being a field on `Snippet` (`#[serde(default = 1)]` → older files load as v1; envelope stays version 2). The **merge on import is the pure, unit-tested `snippets::merge_version(incoming, local, content_differs)`** — identical to cue so both converge and a re-import is a no-op: new → `max(incoming, 1)`; content identical → `max(incoming, local)`; content differs → `max(incoming, local + 1)`; missing/0 incoming → 1. `upsert_by_abbreviation` gained an `incoming_version` arg and now reads the local row (decrypted body) to run the merge. Frontend: a `vN` badge on each Snippets-tab row + in the editor header (both editors) + the read-only snippet preview. Covered by `merge_version_matrix`, `update_bumps_only_on_content_change`, `migration_adds_version_column_and_backfills_v1`, `upsert_merge_respects_incoming_version` (snippets.rs) + `snippet_versions_survive_the_roundtrip_and_reimport_is_a_noop`, `backup_without_version_field_imports_as_v1`, `backup_merge_takes_the_higher_version_and_bumps_on_content_change` (backup.rs).

**Timesheet in the backup (v3, v0.84.237 — opt-in).** `ExportOptions.include_timesheet` (default **false**, also in `all()`) adds a `TimesheetBackup` (sessions/events/claude_turns/categories). `window_title`/`url` are **decrypted at export** (portable file, like TOTP secrets) and **re-encrypted with the target machine's key on import**. **A timesheet-less export still claims `version: 2`** so older app builds keep importing it; only a timesheet-carrying file claims 3. Import merge (`import_timesheet`) remaps ids and dedupes — sessions by `started_at`, events by (session, started_at, app, source), claude turns by (event, ts), categories upsert — so re-import creates no duplicates; an imported `active` session is forced to `ended` (closed at its last event's end) so it can never fight the machine's live session. `BackupImportResult.timesheet_imported` counts events.

**Encrypted backups (v0.79.0+; full UI v0.84.237).** Export optionally accepts a `password` parameter; the plaintext JSON is encrypted with AES-256-GCM using a key derived via Argon2id from the password. The on-disk format is a JSON envelope: `{ "encrypted": true, "kdf": "argon2id", "salt": "<b64>", "nonce": "<b64>", "ciphertext": "<b64>" }`. On import, `is_encrypted()` detects the envelope; if encrypted, a password is required. IPC: `export_backup` / `save_backup_to_file` (optional `password` + `include_timesheet`), `import_backup` (optional `password`), `is_backup_encrypted` (path → bool).

**Cloud-Sync mit cue (`sync.rs`, v0.87.0).** Automatischer bidirektionaler Snippet-Sync mit der cue-Web-App: ein Worker-Thread (`sync::start`, gestartet im lib.rs-setup) pollt `GET {url}/api/sync/snippets` (Bearer `sync.token`) alle 60 s und — via `sync::request_sync()`, aufgerufen neben JEDEM `auto_expand::rebuild_table` in commands.rs — debounced nach jeder Snippet-Mutation; danach Push per POST. Der **Scope kommt mit jedem Pull aus cue** (Gruppen-Namen + ungrouped-Flag; IR speichert keinen Scope — cue ist der organisatorische Master, auch für die Gruppenzuordnung bei Versions-Gleichstand). Merge in `apply_pull` (pure über der DB, unit-getestet): incoming>local → übernehmen; gleich+identisch → No-op (nur Kategorie folgt cue); gleich+verschieden → cue gewinnt (Bump auf local+1 konvergiert beim nächsten Push); incoming<local → behalten. **Tombstones** (`snippet_tombstones`-Tabelle in `snippets::init_table`, Helfer `record_tombstone_conn`/`resurrect_floor_conn` nehmen `&Connection` — Aufrufer halten den Lock, parking_lot ist nicht reentrant): `snippets::delete` und ein Abbreviation-Rename in `update` hinterlassen `{abbreviation, version}`; `create`/`update`/`upsert_by_abbreviation` landen via resurrect-floor über einem Tombstone (`max(v, ts+1)`, Tombstone gelöscht); `deleted_at` refresht nur bei Versions-Erhöhung (Peers echoen Tombstones jeden Zyklus zurück — sonst überlebten sie die 90-Tage-TTL ewig). Nach Änderungen: `auto_expand::rebuild_table` + Tauri-Event `snippets-synced` (App.tsx → `refreshSnippets` + `snippetRev++`). Settings-Keys `sync.{enabled,url,token,last_ms,last_error}`; IPC `get/set_sync_config`, `get_sync_status`, `sync_now`; UI = `CloudSyncSection` in SettingsPanel.tsx (vor Backup & restore). Wire-Format-Feldnamen (`abbreviation/title/body/category/version`, `groups/sync_ungrouped/snippets/tombstones`, `deleted_at_ms`) sind der Vertrag mit cue (`backend/app/routers/sync.py`) — nicht umbenennen. Live-E2E: `CUE_SYNC_URL=… CUE_SYNC_TOKEN=… cargo test -p inspector-rust-core --lib sync -- --ignored` gegen ein lokales cue. **Interaktive Status-Chips (v0.87.1):** `sync::test_connection(cfg)` probt den Pull-Endpoint live; die pure, getestete `classify_probe(status, err)` unterscheidet 2xx = ok · 401/403 = Token abgelehnt · sonstige HTTP = erreichbar-aber-kaputt · Netzwerkfehler = nicht erreichbar (`SyncProbe { reachable, authorized, message }`). Async-IPC `sync_test_connection` (ureq blockiert → läuft off-main). Der Worker emittiert nach JEDEM Zyklus (Erfolg wie Fehler) `sync-status-changed`; die `CloudSyncSection` zeigt drei ✓/✗-Chips (Server erreichbar · Token gültig · Letzter Sync), probt automatisch beim Öffnen + nach URL-/Token-Änderung, hat einen „Verbindung testen"-Button und aktualisiert event-getrieben statt des alten 4-s-Blind-Polls.

**`settings`-Command (Alias `config`, v0.87.1).** Öffnet den Settings-Tab per Texteingabe; ein Argument deep-linkt fuzzy (DE+EN Synonyme) zu einer Sektion — Registry = pure, unit-getestete `lib/settings-sections.ts` (`SETTINGS_SECTIONS` mit 21 Einträgen, `matchSettingsSection` via `fuzzyScore`). `dispatchCommand` setzt `settingsJump {id, nonce}` + `setActiveTab("settings")`; `SettingsPanel` bekommt `jumpTo`, scrollt (`scrollIntoView`, reduced-motion-aware) zum Anker `settings-<id>` (die `Section`-Komponente hat dafür eine optionale `id`-Prop) und flasht `.settings-jump-highlight` (styles.css, paint-only). Beim Hinzufügen einer neuen Settings-Sektion: `id` an der `<Section>` vergeben + Eintrag in `SETTINGS_SECTIONS`.

**Settings → Backup & restore (reworked v0.84.237).** Export always covers the whole app (the old per-section tickboxes are gone) + one opt-in **"Timesheet data"** checkbox; an **"Encrypt with password"** checkbox reveals password + repeat fields (show-toggle, match validation, no-recovery warning). Import picks a file, probes `is_backup_encrypted`, and for an encrypted file shows an **inline unlock row** (password field + "Unlock & import"; a wrong password keeps the row so the user corrects it without re-picking the file). The result banner shows all counts (history/snippets/notes/2FA/settings + timesheet events).
